# Re-attestation brief — spine rows owing a human act

_Generated by `trace.py --ratify modified` (WI-316). One section per SR that is `Drafted` or `Modified`, or whose chain has DRIFTED from the approved snapshot; each chain row shows only its CHANGED cells, before (the snapshot) vs after (the working tree), ratified cells first. `Status` itself is never listed — the marker is not the amendment. Rule on each section: bless → set `Status` to `Approved` (process.md §7) — and from the first signing onward, run `intake.py snapshot` in the SAME commit, or the record of what was blessed does not move._

_Baseline: `docs/archive/last_approved` — no snapshot exists yet, so every row below awaits a FIRST approval and renders its current text in full._


## SR-006 — Gate/tier harness enforces required steps

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-006 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-006
- **Title**: Gate/tier harness enforces required steps
- **SN-Refs**: SN-004;SN-008
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall run the required steps of the gate that must next be passed (the strictness selector cached in docs/gate) and fail that gate when a required tool is missing, reporting SKIP(missing) rather than silently passing — and, on a claimed work branch (one whose history holds a docs/work/active/<branch>/ claim), SKIP its generated-artifact freshness steps with a stated trunk-lane reason, generated artifacts being trunk-only, while every non-freshness step still runs.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-004 (gates enforce their bar), SN-008 (no false green) and the dissolved edge expectation that a missing tool fails, never skips. ABSORBS SR-133, 2026-08-15: that row's own rationale read "Decomposed from SR-006", which is the census's demotion criterion stated by the row about itself — a sanctioned SKIP class of this harness's own step plan is not a second crossing observable, it is the same one qualified. The skip is not a weakening: a work branch cannot commit generated artifacts at all (concurrency-restructure §5.2), so demanding their freshness there would force the very conflict the rule deletes; the skip is reported, never silent, and any signal ambiguity resolves toward the full trunk bar. SR-133 was `Verified`; the demotion overrides that attestation deliberately, under the owner's 2026-08-15 ruling, and is named in the log rather than ridden in quietly.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Invoked for a bar, the harness runs that bar's declared steps; a required-but-absent tool fails the gate with guidance (never a silent pass), degraded to a reported SKIP only under the explicit lenient mode; a tier selection runs exactly the declared step subset; on a claimed work branch the declared freshness steps SKIP with a stated trunk-lane reason and the exit code untouched while every non-freshness step still runs; the closing commit and its successors keep the skip (the signal derives from branch history, not the working tree); the trunk lane never skips them.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

- **Detail**: Builds the required-step plan per gate/tier and reports SKIP(missing) with a nonzero exit when a required tool is absent; the plan listing still names every step. The work-branch lane skip is LLR-141, which decomposes from this same row.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-006)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-098 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-098
- **SR-Refs**: SR-006
- **Title**: Per-module coverage-floor comparator
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_coverage.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load_floors/load_report/evaluate
- **Detail**: Reads the docs/coverage-floors census (module + min percent; blank and # lines ignored) and coverage.py's JSON report (per-file percent_covered, path-normalized), fails on any module below its floor or absent from the report, SKIPs when the report is absent, is a no-op with an empty census, and fails loudly on a malformed floor line. A failure names the offending module, and all floors holding exits 0 — the per-module floor is what keeps the global aggregate from hiding a thin high-risk module.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-101)
- **Status**: Modified

### LLR LLR-141 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-141
- **SR-Refs**: SR-006;SR-170
- **Title**: Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _claimed_work_branch/steps
- **Detail**: On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
- **Rationale**: A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-134)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-007 — Declared stack profile, refused when it is broken

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-007 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-007
- **Title**: Declared stack profile, refused when it is broken
- **SN-Refs**: SN-003
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall read the toolchain (format/lint/test commands, tiers, coverage, arch-map mode) from the declared stack profile so a stack swap edits only that declaration, failing loudly on a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold rather than proceeding on a bad one.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-003 — the kit is stack-agnostic; the harness is re-pointed by declaration, not by editing a kit script. ABSORBS SR-008, 2026-08-15: reading a declaration and REFUSING a broken one are one obligation, not two — a reader that proceeds on a malformed profile has not read it. SR-008 named a script and a mechanism, which is the census's demotion criterion, and `trace.py` had already been warning that its only child (LLR-008) "mostly re-words" it, the shape a row takes when it is a design detail wearing an SR id. Its third clause, a MISSING DECLARED BINARY, is not folded here: SR-006 already states it verbatim as its own ("fail that gate when a required tool is missing"), which is why LLR-008 now decomposes from both rows rather than one. SR-008 was `Verified`; the demotion overrides that attestation deliberately, under the owner's 2026-08-15 ruling, and is named in the log rather than ridden in quietly.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Each step's command resolves from the declared stack profile; changing the profile changes the commands run, with no script edit; a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold produces a clear nonzero failure at the relevant step rather than a run on a bad profile (a missing declared binary fails under SR-006, which states that clause).
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-007 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-007
- **SR-Refs**: SR-007
- **Title**: Stack profile loader
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load_profile
- **Detail**: Reads format/lint/test/tiers/coverage/arch-map from docs/stack.ini and resolves each step's command from that profile.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-007)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-008 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-008
- **SR-Refs**: SR-007;SR-006

- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load_profile
- **Detail**: Rejects a malformed profile, non-integer coverage threshold, or missing declared binary with a clear loud failure at the relevant step, rather than proceeding on a bad profile. Two parents on purpose (2026-08-15, retired SR-008): the malformed-profile and non-integer-coverage refusals decompose from SR-007, which is the row that reads the declaration; the missing-declared-binary refusal decomposes from SR-006, which already states that clause in its own requirement. Splitting the refs is what stops the obligation being re-stated at SR level to give this row a single parent.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-008)

## SR-011 — Idempotent re-runnable scaffold

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-011 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-011
- **Title**: Idempotent re-runnable scaffold
- **SN-Refs**: SN-001;SN-007
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered scaffold generator shall skip an existing file on a re-run unless an explicit overwrite is requested.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-001 (safe drop-in onto an existing repo) and SN-007 — an adopter picking up kit updates must not lose their own edits, and a scaffolder that overwrites cannot be run twice, so it stops being run at all. Overwrite-always with a backup copy was rejected: it leaves the repo carrying a second copy of every file it touched. The deliberate kit-vs-project merge is a separate, operator-driven process (SR-036).
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A re-run leaves every existing file (kit- or project-owned) byte-unchanged unless the explicit overwrite is requested; with the overwrite requested, the file is replaced.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

- **SR-Refs**: SR-011
- **Title**: Idempotent write + kit-version stamp

- **CodeSymbol**: write/--force + write_kit_version
- **Detail**: Skips an existing file unless --force.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-011)

- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-015 — Performance-budget back-links

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-015 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-015
- **Title**: Performance-budget back-links
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered performance-budgets registry (PB-###) shall keep every row's Refs resolvable to a real SR/LLR/Module.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-002 — a budget row that cannot be traced to a requirement, design item or module cannot demonstrate what it constrains, so the off-spine budget rows stay traceable to the spine. Deliberate pair (census F6): this row states the delivered data invariant; the checker that polices it is the harness's, decomposing under SR-157 — an on-purpose split, not an echo. The hat.PERFORMANCE label added earlier on 2026-08-16 was WITHDRAWN the same day (adversarial round, finding F7, CONFIRMED): the charter's failure class is a speed or size risk left unassessed, or a budget with no measurement behind it — it says nothing about whether a trace reference resolves, so citing it here claimed support the charter does not give. SN-002 was always the real basis and is the only one now.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A PB row with an unresolvable or empty Refs is a finding; a resolvable one passes.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: perf

## SR-017 — Always-on secrets floor

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-017 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-017
- **Title**: Always-on secrets floor
- **SN-Refs**: SN-009
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-01;B-04
- **Requirement**: When a session attempts to commit or push content matching a secret pattern, the system shall refuse at the hook floor — scanning every commit's staged diff, message and outgoing range regardless of the privacy toggle, and naming the finding — unless the declared secrets_scan dial is explicitly false.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-009 — a credential that reaches a shared branch must be treated as disclosed and rotated, and expunging it from history is a rewrite every clone has to follow, so the cheap moment to catch it is before the commit and the expensive one is any moment after. Folding the scan into the privacy toggle was rejected: that makes the highest-severity check opt-in alongside a preference. The one opt-out is an explicit declaration, so turning it off is a visible act.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A staged PEM header, token, or key pattern is flagged and blocks; a clean diff passes; the floor runs with privacy-check off, and is disabled only by an explicit secrets_scan = false.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-017 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-017
- **SR-Refs**: SR-017
- **Title**: Secrets pattern scan
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_privacy.py
- **CodeSymbol**: Scanner/KEY_RE
- **Detail**: Always-on scan of staged diff/message/outgoing range for PEM/token/key patterns, independent of the privacy toggle.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-017)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### TC TC-017 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-017
- **Verifies**: SR-017;LLR-017
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the check-privacy suite; a staged secret is blocked with privacy-check off.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-017 AcceptanceCriteria

- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-018 — Privacy gate two-axis

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-018 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-018
- **Title**: Privacy gate two-axis
- **SN-Refs**: SN-009
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-01;B-04
- **Requirement**: When the declared privacy_check dial is on, the system shall additionally refuse, at the same hook floor, a commit or push carrying a PII/identity class — exempting configured author identities.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-009 — identity and PII classes are a policy choice a repo makes about its own contributors, unlike a leaked credential, which is a defect in every repo. Making them always-on was rejected because the exemption list they need is project-specific: a repo that has not declared its author identities would have the layer firing on its own commit metadata, and the fix would be to silence the scanner rather than to configure it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: With privacy-check on, an identity/PII class is flagged unless the author is a configured exempt identity; with it off, only the secrets floor runs.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-018 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-018
- **SR-Refs**: SR-018
- **Title**: Two-axis privacy gating

- **CodeSymbol**: EXEMPT_EMAILS/email_ok
- **Detail**: Adds the PII/identity classes when docs/process.toml [policies] privacy_check is on (the legacy docs/privacy-check is still read through the migration window), exempting configured author identities.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-018)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### SR SR-019 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-019
- **Title**: Pre-commit hook floor
- **SN-Refs**: SN-005;SN-009
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-01;B-04
- **Requirement**: The pre-commit hook shall run the integrity + secrets floor on every commit, agent-neutrally — a LOCAL floor, bypassable by the session (git commit --no-verify), so it discharges "no unchecked write enters governed state" only as a PAIR with the hosted re-run of the same bar (SR-151/SR-152), never alone.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-005 and SN-009 — a floor only CI enforces is one the local tree crosses freely, and a secret caught after the commit is already in history. Trusting the agent to run the checks was rejected: a floor exists for the times the agent forgets, so it is git-enforced and agent-neutral. Parents the interpreter probe from 2026-08-15 (retired SR-021): a hook that crashes cryptically because no python3 resolves, or because a Windows Store alias exits nonzero, has not run the floor — so probing by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly is machinery serving THIS obligation, and it decomposes to LLR-021 rather than standing as a requirement of its own.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The hook runs the registry-integrity floor and the secrets scan on staged content and blocks a commit that fails either; a --no-verify commit bypasses it and is caught instead by the hosted re-run, which is what makes the pair — not this row — the honest unit of the claim.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

- **SR-Refs**: SR-019

- **TestRefs**: (see TC-019)
- **Status**: Approved

- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-021 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-021
- **SR-Refs**: SR-019;SR-020
- **Title**: Hook python probe
- **Module**: project-trajectory/hooks/pre-push
- **CodeSymbol**: python3 probe
- **Detail**: Probes candidate interpreters by RUNNING one — never by testing for the name on PATH, which a Windows Store alias satisfies while exiting nonzero — and skip-or-reports clearly when none works, so neither hook crashes cryptically on a box without a resolvable python3. Shared by pre-commit and pre-push (the module named here is the representative home; both hooks read the same probe). The equivalent obligation on the ROOT LAUNCHERS is stated on SR-160, not here: retired SR-021 spanned both audiences at once, and the launcher half is an observable of the front door rather than machinery of the hook floor.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-021)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-026 — Coordinator resumes headless

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-026 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-026
- **Title**: Coordinator resumes headless
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered coordinator shall resume headless with stdin closed, never blocking on a prompt: a worker resumes from its explicit claimed assignment plus the committed trailer evidence on its branch, and the integrator derives claim and queue state from trunk history alone — the generated status surface never a session input.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run. ACCEPTANCE NARROWED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied): the acceptance carried two obligations the shall never stated - transient-limit backoff and stall abort - and an acceptance cell cannot mint a requirement. Both were MINTED rather than deleted, because each describes live behaviour worth keeping: SR-171 and SR-172. An unattended run blocked on a prompt is indistinguishable from a hung one and holds its lane until a human notices. Keeping a separate serial resume path beside the dispatcher was rejected: two authorities over one decision drift, and the divergence surfaces as a run that resumes differently depending on how it was started.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A worker reconciles from its assignment + committed evidence and the integrator from trunk history; every mode runs with stdin closed and a prompt cannot wedge it.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-026 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-026
- **SR-Refs**: SR-026
- **Title**: Headless session launch
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_argv/run_session/_codex_lastmsg_setup
- **Detail**: Runs the agent CLI headless: build_argv substitutes {model} into argv and, when a CmdTemplate carries no {prompt} placeholder (the default for both provider CLIs), routes the prompt to the child's STDIN (run_session writes it from a daemon thread then closes), while the no-wedge invariant holds — a fixed prompt plus EOF, never an interactive read. A codex session additionally captures that CLI's own --output-last-message file as the result (_codex_lastmsg_setup/_codex_lastmsg_read). stdin is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status.md-resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them.
- **Rationale**: A large prompt passed as argv dies at the OS command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows .CMD shim, 32767 via CreateProcess) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so stdin is the only delivery that survives — both limits verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that CLI echoes its banner and the whole prompt into stdout, so stdout is not the result.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-026)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-061 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-061
- **SR-Refs**: SR-026
- **Title**: Explicit WI/train/worktree worker assignment
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_worker_assignment/worker_prompt
- **Detail**: Replaces internal --track assumptions with explicit --wi/--train/worktree assignment; assembles the worker prompt from AGENTS.md + WI row + SpecRef + predecessor context + train diff + rework finding (never the generated status surface); collision-safe per-assignment logs/review evidence naming the exact reviewed commit; the --track lane machinery is retired outright. The assignment is the branch integrate.py's claim cut, and the session tag defaults to the branch name; the prompt never includes docs/status.md; a worker branch never edits root status, another branch's claims, the root log or generated artifacts; the result channel is committed trailer evidence (WI: / Blocked-WI: + BlockRef:).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-061)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-143 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-143
- **SR-Refs**: SR-026
- **Title**: Plain-launch drive loop (claim->build->integrate)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
- **CodeSymbol**: run/_resume_or_claim/_stranded_claims/_default_worker
- **Detail**: The serial scheduling front end a plain agent_loop.py launch runs: each cycle re-derives the ready frontier (schedule.frontier), refuses on the tracked pause and on a dirty trunk at the cycle top, resumes a parked claimed branch else claims the next queued WI in build order (integrate.claim, branch name check-ref-format-validated), launches one worker session on the claimed branch's worktree (agent_loop.py --worktree --wi with the session dials forwarded), drains the serial merge queue (integrate.integrate) counting merges from finished_branches, and stops loudly on any composed refusal - stranded claims (active specs with no branch ref) refuse by name, NEEDS-HUMAN propagates as exit 7, a stall aborts, an empty frontier drains to exit 0. The session-config preflight applies only when work needs a worker. Never pushes.
- **Rationale**: Ordering only, no new authority: every refusal stays where it already lives, and the driver is ~400 lines of composition against the 4,042-line dispatcher it replaces.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-137)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the agent-loop suite; the dispatcher and its workers resume headless (registry + reservations + explicit assignments) without blocking on any prompt; a no-{prompt} CmdTemplate delivers its prompt on stdin (build_argv/run_session), a large prompt surviving untruncated.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-026 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes

- **Tier**: Full

### TC TC-137 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-137
- **Verifies**: SR-026;LLR-143
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Run the drive suite: an unwired agent command refuses before any claim while an empty queue still drains to exit 0; a refusing claim rung, a stranded claim, a dirty trunk and a red composed-tree bar each stop the run by name; the tracked pause appearing mid-run stops the next cycle at exit 8; NEEDS-HUMAN propagates at exit 7 with the claim parked; a DONE-reporting worker that finishes nothing trips the stall guard; and two end-to-end runs against the real bar prove the green path (claim->build->merge->drained banner) and the red path (candidate parked, claim intact). The plain agent_loop.py launch enters the drive mode as a subprocess.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-026 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_dispatch.py
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-027 — Coordinator preflight

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-027 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-027
- **Title**: Coordinator preflight
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered coordinator shall refuse a broken footing in preflight — not a git repo, no agent CLI, a private author under privacy-check, or a second coordinator in the same checkout while another holds its lock — exiting a typed nonzero code rather than hanging.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that a preflight on a non-git working directory reports and exits nonzero rather than hanging — a run that starts on a broken footing fails later and further from the cause, and an unattended loop cannot be asked what went wrong. Continuing with a warning was rejected: each of these conditions makes every subsequent step meaningless, so a typed nonzero exit is what lets the caller act on it. ABSORBS SR-030, 2026-08-15: a second coordinator in the same checkout is a FOURTH broken footing, not a separate obligation — it fails in the same direction, at the same moment, through the same refusal, and SR-030 named a script and a lock mechanism, which is the census's demotion criterion. Two coordinators in one checkout write the same working tree and the loser's commits interleave into the winner's diff where no review will separate them; coordinating by convention was rejected there for the reason that keeps it here — the failure is silent and shows up only as a garbled history. The lock machinery decomposes to LLR-029/LLR-030.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Outside a git repo, with no agent CLI, or with a private author under privacy-check, preflight exits a typed nonzero code; a second coordinator on a held lock is refused so only one writer runs per checkout, preventing a two-writer race.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-027 (current)

- **SR-Refs**: SR-027
- **Title**: Preflight footing guard
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_common.py
- **CodeSymbol**: preflight/EXIT_PREFLIGHT
- **Detail**: Refuses a non-git dir, a missing agent CLI, or a private author under privacy-check with a typed nonzero exit rather than hanging.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-027)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 1

- **CodeSymbol**: acquire_lock/_take_os_lock
- **Detail**: Takes an OS advisory lock the kernel releases on process death, so no stale-pid file wedges the next run. The lock scope is per-worktree — the dispatcher, each worker and an interactive sitting take the same lock for their checkout.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-029)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 1

- **Detail**: Refuses a second coordinator holding the same per-worktree lock, preventing a two-writer race; the refusal is the preflight's typed nonzero exit, not a warning, so only one writer runs per checkout.

## SR-028 — Coordinator typed outcomes

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-028 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-028
- **Title**: Coordinator typed outcomes
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered coordinator shall end each session in a typed outcome code, guard a zero-commit HEAD, and report an all-ERROR stall as an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectations that a repository with no commits yet (HEAD absent) does not crash the loop and that an all-ERROR run is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall — a coordinator ending in an untyped code forces its caller to parse prose; a session that produced no commit still has a HEAD to report, so without a guard an empty run is indistinguishable from a productive one in the record it leaves; and an all-ERROR run reported as a work stall sends a human looking for a blocked work item that does not exist. Treating an agent error as a stall was rejected for that reason: an unreachable agent and a blocked item need different responses, so they need different outcomes.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Every declared end state returns its assigned outcome code, and an unrecognized end state fails rather than returning a success code; a repo with no commits does not crash the loop; an agent-CLI error is logged ERROR; an all-ERROR region is reported as an unavailable agent.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-028 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-028
- **SR-Refs**: SR-028
- **Title**: Typed outcome + ERROR handling
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
- **CodeSymbol**: classify_outcome/stall_verdict
- **Detail**: Ends each session in a typed code, guards a zero-commit HEAD, and reports an all-ERROR region as an unavailable agent, not a work stall.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-028)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 1

- **TC-ID**: TC-028
- **Verifies**: SR-028;LLR-028

- **Phase**: 1

## SR-031 — Declared-policy readers agree

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-031 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-031
- **Title**: Declared-policy readers agree
- **SN-Refs**: SN-004;SN-005
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Every delivered enforcer of a declared policy dial shall read the same value for that dial as every other enforcer — one parse, one answer, across the hooks, the loop and the checkers alike.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-004 (policies drive the gate) and SN-005 (one source, every enforcer reads it the same way). Partition with SR-137 (census): the one-home surface and its checked shape are SR-137's obligations at B-01/B-04 — corrected 2026-08-15 from a stale B-01/B-02 written before that row's crossings were revised — while this row is the package-wide coherence property those mechanics serve. ONE HOME, settled 2026-08-15: the two-grammars-agree observable lives HERE and nowhere else. SR-137 stated it too and the texts had already diverged, only this row's naming the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN; the duplicate clause is struck from SR-137 and nothing of its wording was unique. The shared-parse mechanism and the legacy migration-window read decompose to this row's LLRs.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Every enforcer reads the same value for the same dial; the two delivered reader grammars return the same answer over the adversarial file-shape table, including the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-031 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-031
- **SR-Refs**: SR-031
- **Title**: Shared declared-line parse

## SR-034 — Kit scripts run on stdlib plus ledger-declared dependencies

### SR SR-034 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-034
- **Title**: Kit scripts run on stdlib plus ledger-declared dependencies
- **SN-Refs**: SN-011
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Every kit script shall run on a clean Python 3.11+ using the standard library plus only those non-stdlib dependencies declared as a reviewed row in the dependency ledger.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-011 — the kit must impose no *unargued* dependency stack on a downstream repo to run its checks, but a blanket ban is itself a design constraint that can force a worse hand-rolled alternative than a well-chosen tool, so the bar is a reviewed argument per dependency (owner RULING-3, 2026-07-28), not abstinence. Leaving the row at "stdlib-only" was rejected: it had already drifted from the standing ruling, leaving the registry and the ledger giving two answers to one question.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Every kit script's top-level imports resolve to the standard library, a local kit sibling, or a reviewed row of the dependency ledger; an undeclared non-stdlib import fails the suite; every declared row names what it replaces, why hand-rolling is worse, and the ruling that admitted it; a shipped-tier row (one an adopter is forced to install) exists only with an owner ruling recorded in the ledger, stdlib remaining preferred there.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Analysis
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: portability

## SR-040 — Per-phase routing and review dial

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-040 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-040
- **Title**: Per-phase routing and review dial
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered coordinator shall route each in-process session phase (PLAN/BUILD/REVIEW-A/REVIEW-B/DESIGN-CHECK/CRITIQUE) through its declared per-phase command template, falling back to the single declared command — surfacing the declared reviewer dial at run start without enforcing it, and warning (never blocking) when a lane resume surface exceeds the declared size threshold.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run — two samples of one model share blind spots, so a review by the family that built the change agrees with it for the wrong reasons; cross-family routing is what makes the second opinion independent, and one model for every phase was rejected on that basis. Dispatch stays keyed off the in-process session phase because the alternative, a per-phase code branch, makes adding a phase a code change and so discourages adding one. The resume surface's growth tripwire warns rather than fails because a growing resume surface is a smell and not a defect, and failing on a smell trains an operator to bypass the check. Hat-derived (hat.UX-ENGINEER + hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): only the resume-surface tripwire clause is orphaned, and two lenses reach it independently — a view must stay usable as its content volume grows (C-UXE-2), and at 3am the reader of a resume surface is another automated step, which needs the growth legible from the artifact rather than from a human noticing (C-UNA-8). Both also explain the warn-not-fail severity: a smell is reported, not gated. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
THE TRIPWIRE'S CARRIER IS RETIRED AND THE OBLIGATION IS LEFT STANDING — investigated 2026-08-18, stated rather than quietly dropped or quietly re-pointed. The dial this row's acceptance used to cite and the pure warner behind it were deleted with the serial resume driver; the coordinator now carries a TOMBSTONE COMMENT where that function stood, and its own words say the PREMISE dissolved rather than the code moving — no session inherits the status surface as its resume surface any more, and that surface is a generated integrator artifact whose size the generator owns. So the clause has no implementation, and its SUBJECT as originally drawn has no referent either.
THE NEAREST LIVE CANDIDATE WAS EXAMINED AND REFUSED AS A SUBSTITUTE. The documentation checker warns when the status surface passes a declared line budget, which sounds like this clause and is not it: it differs on ACTOR (a documentation checker, not the delivered coordinator this row's subject names), on MOMENT (the commit and gate bar, not run start or mid-run), and on SURFACE (the status page judged as lean prose for a human reviewer — the very "human noticing" the C-UNA-8 lens above says is the wrong mechanism at 3am). It is also already the carrier of a DIFFERENT requirement, so citing it here would put one mechanism under two requirements that do not agree about what it must do. Decisively, its threshold semantics are the INVERSE of this row's: a declared zero or negative budget makes it warn on EVERY non-empty file and only an explicit off token silences it — measured by exercising the function on declared values 0, -1, off and 200, not read off its source. Adopting it would have been carrier substitution across a different requirement, component, actor and moment, with inverted semantics, and it is refused on that evidence.
WHAT THIS ROW THEREFORE IS: an obligation with no current carrier, and the ONE residue this sitting must still rule. Two honest dispositions, neither taken here because both are the owner's: rebuild the tripwire inside the coordinator against whatever the CURRENT resume surface is, or strike the clause and let the two hat lenses that reach it be answered elsewhere. It is not restated as satisfied by anything, because nothing satisfies it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A mapped session phase invokes its own command template and the default template does not fire; a broken map entry fails preflight before iteration 1; the banner surfaces the declared reviewer dial at run start. The resume-surface clause states its FIT CRITERION and explicitly NOT a discharge: a satisfied state would be the coordinator reporting, without halting the run, that a resume surface a resuming session inherits has passed its declared threshold — and NOTHING IN THE DELIVERED COORDINATOR DOES THIS, so this clause is an open obligation with no current carrier rather than a criterion any evidence today satisfies (the rationale records the retirement, and the live look-alike that was examined and refused).
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: unattended-loop

### LLR LLR-037 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-037
- **SR-Refs**: SR-040
- **Title**: Per-phase cmd map and the reviewer dial
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
- **CodeSymbol**: session_template/parse_map/read_declared
- **Detail**: cmd_map parsed by parse_map (same KEY=value syntax; templates may not contain , or ; — the dispatcher wrapper covers those); session_template(phase) falls back to AGENT_CMD; every map entry preflighted (parse + executable); review-policy read via read_declared and printed in the banner.
THE SIZE-TRIPWIRE HALF IS GONE FROM THIS ROW, corrected 2026-08-18 — this cell described `status_size_warning` as "pure and warn-only (limit<=0 disables)" for a symbol that no longer exists, and an Approved design row publishing a deleted symbol into the generated knowledge bundle is worse than a gap, because it reads as a carrier. The function retired with the serial resume driver and the module now carries a tombstone comment in its place, whose own words are that the PREMISE dissolved rather than the code moving: no session inherits the status surface as its resume surface any more, and that surface is a generated integrator artifact whose size the generator owns. SO THIS MODULE HAS NO SIZE TRIPWIRE AND NO DESIGN IS CLAIMED FOR ONE. SR-040's tripwire clause is therefore an obligation with no design child — stated as the honest state rather than repaired by pointing at the documentation checker's status line-budget warn, which was examined and refused as a substitute (a different actor, a different moment, a different surface, already the carrier of another requirement, and inverted threshold semantics: a declared zero or negative budget makes it warn on every non-empty file). The sitting rules whether the tripwire is rebuilt here against the current resume surface or the clause is struck; until then this row carries only what the module actually does. `parse_map` is live and re-exported from agent_common, so it stays named.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-040)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 1

### TC TC-040 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-040
- **Verifies**: SR-040;LLR-037
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the agent-loop suite; a REVIEW-B-mapped phase invokes the second fake CLI and not the default, a broken map entry exits preflight naming the phase, and the banner carries review-policy.
CORRECTED 2026-08-18: this method claimed a fourth clause — "a bloated status.md warns without blocking" — that NO test in the evidence file performs, and the row was `Approved` while claiming it. The file-granular evidence cell is what hid it: naming a 63-test file rather than the functions leaves a stated clause and an absent test indistinguishable, so the evidence below is now function-granular and the clause is dropped rather than left as a green nothing checks. The tripwire it referred to has no carrier at all (SR-040's acceptance and LLR-037 record the retirement and the refused substitute); when the sitting rules whether to rebuild it, its test is minted with it.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-040's routing and dial criteria; the resume-surface tripwire clause is unverified BY CONSTRUCTION because it has no carrier
- **Automated**: Yes

- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-043 — Subagent spawn gate

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-043 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-043
- **Title**: Subagent spawn gate
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-04
- **Requirement**: While an unattended run is in progress, the delivered subagent-spawn gate shall refuse or defer subagent spawns per the declared subagent-gate dial in the single policy declaration home (off|ask|deny; absent=off; the legacy one-word file is still read through the migration window), honor a launcher-set SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override the model cannot set, and fail open on any error so a broken gate never wedges the tools.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run stays safe — bounded, supervised fan-out with the override held by the human, not the model) and SN-012 (opt-in: absent/off is a vacuous allow, so a non-adopter pays nothing). Supervision, not security — a model that can edit files can remove the hook (adapted from stop-subagent-fanout, MIT; spec docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4). Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-2 asks that the irreversible actions an unattended run can take be enumerated, each naming the dial or human authority that permits it — which is this row exactly, for the one action that starts further actors. A direct hit, and the reason this row is not accretion: its parentage was real and lived only in SN-006's `why` cell until the safety half moved into that need's normative text at this same pass. OPEN QUESTION FOR THE SITTING, recorded rather than resolved here (2026-08-16 adversarial round, finding F1): with the bound now normative in SN-006, this row's fail-open-on-any-error clause is in tension with that bound — a gate error drops the ceiling the need promises — and the two candidate resolutions (fail closed, or keep failing open under a narrower ceiling that stays enforced when the hook errors) are both behavior changes an owner must rule; the fail-open behavior is UNCHANGED pending that ruling, because a gate that wedges every tool on its own bug is the failure this clause was written against. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: With the declared subagent-gate dial deny, a subagent spawn is refused (a deny decision at the tool layer, exit 2); ask defers to approval; off or absent, and the launcher-held allow override, admit it; a non-spawn tool is not gated; a malformed payload fails open (allow) and is logged.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-040 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-040
- **SR-Refs**: SR-043
- **Title**: Subagent spawn gate
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/subagent_gate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: decide/main
- **Detail**: Pure decide(tool_name, policy, override) -> allow|ask|deny|defer over SPAWN_TOOLS={Task,Agent}; main reads the PreToolUse JSON payload, reads docs/process.toml [checks] subagent_gate (falling back to the legacy one-word docs/subagent-gate through the migration window), applies the SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override, emits permissionDecision (deny -> exit 2), and appends each decision to out/subagent-gate.log; any exception fails OPEN (allow) and logs, so a broken gate never wedges the agent's tools.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-043)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### TC TC-043 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-043
- **Verifies**: SR-043;LLR-040
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the subagent-gate suite; a Task/Agent spawn under deny is refused (permissionDecision=deny, exit 2), under ask defers, off/absent and the SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override allow it, a non-spawn tool is not gated, and a malformed payload fails open (allow) with a log line.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-043 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_subagent_gate.py
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-046 — Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository root

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-046 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-046
- **Title**: Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository root
- **SN-Refs**: SN-001;SN-035
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The launchers at the repository root shall expose every declared runnable capability consistently to an interactive operator, to a direct caller naming one capability, and to automated discovery - from a single declaration, on Windows and POSIX alike.
- **Rationale**: RE-VOICED 2026-08-17 (Sol F14, applied): the shall was a MENU SPECIFICATION - numbered interactive menu, direct named launch, machine listing, empty-declaration text, exit-code passthrough, and the name/name.desc declaration grammar - things that fail independently, which is a decomposition wearing a requirement's clothes. It now states the one capability-level decision: every declared capability is reachable, the same way, from one declaration, by three kinds of caller. Numbering, listing format, declaration grammar, fallback text and exit handling are LLR-047's and the acceptance's. Realizes SN-001 - the evaluator's rungs make running the product never depend on recalling a command, and the capability set is now declared once in docs/stack.ini instead of hard-wired and duplicated across the platform launchers. A new SR formalizing the launcher surface, which previously existed only as process prose.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A machine listing prints one name-and-description line per declared capability in declaration order; a direct call naming a declared capability runs that capability's command and returns its exit code; the no-arg menu launches a stdin-selected capability non-interactively and does not hang on closed stdin; an absent or empty capability declaration prints guidance and exits 1; a declared description renders in both the menu and the listing; the platform launchers delegate to the one selector rather than carrying commands of their own; a fresh scaffold ships the selector and the delegate launchers.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-047 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-047
- **SR-Refs**: SR-046
- **Title**: Run capability menu reader
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/run_menu.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load_capabilities/interactive_menu/direct/launch
- **Detail**: load_capabilities parses the docs/stack.ini [run] section (configparser, interpolation=None, case-preserving optionxform; declaration order kept; a name.desc row attaches to its command and an orphan or empty-value command is dropped) into an ordered (name, command, desc) list; --list prints name<TAB>desc per row; direct(name) launches by exact-match name (exit 2 on an unknown name) and launch() runs the shell line via subprocess shell=True, passing the child exit code straight through; interactive_menu prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from stdin, and returns EOF-safe without hanging; an absent or empty [run] prints guidance and exits 1. The run.* launchers are thin delegates forwarding their args to run_menu.py.
- **Rationale**: The command being run is the user's own declared line, so shell=True is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. EOF-safety is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed stdin turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-047)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 1

### TC TC-047 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-047
- **Verifies**: SR-046;LLR-047
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the run-menu suite: --list prints name<TAB>desc for each declared capability in declaration order (an empty desc renders as a trailing tab); run_menu.py <name> runs the command and returns its exit code (a nonzero passes through); the no-arg menu launches a capability from piped stdin and returns without hanging on closed stdin; an unknown name exits 2 and an absent or empty [run] section prints guidance and exits 1; a name.desc row renders in the menu; and the scaffold ships scripts/run_menu.py plus run.* launchers that delegate to it with no RUN_CMD.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-046 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_run_menu.py
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-049 — Derived gate from artifact states

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-049 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-049
- **Title**: Derived gate from artifact states
- **SN-Refs**: SN-004;SN-008;SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall derive the gate the project must next pass from the spine artifact states alone, caching it as a derived value — never accepting a hand-set one — with a post-attestation amendment dropping the derived gate exactly as a newly introduced draft does, and the pending-amendment count carried beside the draft count so the pending state never hides.
- **Rationale**: SSOT applied to the gate itself (docs/archive/specs/derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md): a hand-set marker can be bumped without the work; deriving it makes gate advancement mechanical (SN-004) and un-gameable (SN-008). The hybrid cache keeps the value readable on checkout; --check guards rot. SN-029 JOINED 2026-08-15 (second top-down read, cross-reference repair): the need's acceptance says in its own words that "an AMENDED requirement drops the derived stage exactly as a newly introduced one does", and this row's shall is the only place in the SR layer that states it — the join was simply unrecorded, so a reader asking which rows realize SN-029 got SR-139 (which tiers are held), SR-140 (what an acceptance record contains) and SR-148 (loop selection), none of which carries the drop clause. Nothing moved between rows; the citation now matches the text that was already here.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The per-phase gates derive from the current artifact states alone (an amended row's phase reads the decomposed-unverified rung until re-attested); the freshness check exits nonzero when a state change is not reflected in the cached derived value's basis; the compared basis line includes the pending-amendment count beside the draft count.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-050 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-050
- **SR-Refs**: SR-049
- **Title**: Derived-gate computation + hybrid cache
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/derive_gate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compute
- **Detail**: Per-artifact gate rules (sr_gate/maturity_gate/sn_gate) + min-aggregation over in-scope artifacts + the cached docs/gate (basis line + --check rot guard, legacy value-only); the derived current phase (max over ratified rows via phase_num) rides the basis line and --print, the phase analogue of the derived gate. is_modified is recognized (F5-mirrored from trace.py, pinned by test_rule_sync) and counted across SR/LLR/TC onto the compared basis line as modified=N beside drafts=N - a surfacing count only, no gate arithmetic (a Modified SR reads DevStg-Tests through the existing decomposed-unverified rung of sr_gate; maturity_gate stays Draft-only so a Modified child never caps).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-050)

### LLR LLR-118 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-118
- **SR-Refs**: SR-049
- **Title**: Generated owner decision surface (registry + attestation depth)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_open_items.py
- **CodeSymbol**: render/_attestation_cards/word_diff/mask_local
- **Detail**: The RENDERED half of the Modified/Draft attestation regime whose gate SR-049 derives. gen_open_items renders (a) every pending row of docs/requirements/open-items.toml as a decision brief and (b) every SR whose Status is Draft (ratification owed) or Modified (re-attest owed) with its whole chain's per-cell before/after. Two contracts the emitted file carries: the attestation-baseline STAMP (a --since render must be reproducible by the freshness gate, or --check compares against a different history) and the check-the-baseline empty state (an auto-derived baseline sitting AFTER a pre-regime amendment renders no cells, which must never read as nothing-changed). The machine-local advisory region is masked before comparing. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: this module RENDERS and owns no second opinion — the attestation model is trace.reattest_model and the pending pointers are gen_trajectory.pending_block, both imported, so a disagreement with `trace.py --ratify` means this view is the defect and the brief wins. NOT claimed: that the rendered page is legible or well-composed.
- **Rationale**: A human cannot bless a delta they cannot see, and the depth that makes a re-attest readable is a WORD-LEVEL diff — which the markdown surface this replaces could not mark, so a reviewer was asked to sign a change they could only read as a whole cell. Re-deriving the attestation model here was rejected: a second opinion that disagreed with the brief would leave the reviewer arbitrating between two surfaces. Both emitted contracts exist because their absence fails SILENTLY in front of someone about to attest, which is the worst place for a quiet failure.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-123)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-147 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-147
- **SR-Refs**: SR-049
- **Title**: SN-coverage gate rung + uncovered basis count
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/derive_gate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: sn_gate/sn_cited_ids
- **Detail**: sn_gate reads the cited set sn_cited_ids builds from SR SN-Refs (an F5 duplicate of trace.py's parse, pinned equal by test_rule_sync like phase_num and LLR_EXEMPT): a RATIFIED SN cited by zero SRs caps the raw level at DevStg-Below - a ratified-but-unanswered need has not earned DevStg-Reqs - while a Draft SN stays exempt from the coverage rung and rides the existing draft rung (one fact, one finding; the itemized 'SN has no SR' listing remains trace.py's orphan rule at DevStg-Tests strictness, reading the SAME cited set, so the gate input and the listing cannot contradict on one registry state). _raw_level rebuilds the cited set from the rows in scope, so the ex-draft counterfactual drops a Draft SR's citation with its row and never fabricates coverage. The compared basis line gains uncovered=N (ratified-and-uncited count) beside drafts=N/modified=N - additive for readers, but --check compares the line whole, so it is a cache-format change downstream repos regenerate through.
- **Rationale**: Before this rung SN->SR linkage was not a gate input at all: an unanswered ratified need left the gate untouched, orphaning only at DevStg-Tests strictness (owner ruling 2026-08-01, dispatcher-flow review Q2). Measured safe before landing: the census on this repo read 25/25 SNs covered and zero ghost SN-Refs, so the rung lands green here and the uncovered case is constructed by fixture, not found.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-141)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-148 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-148
- **SR-Refs**: SR-049
- **Title**: Derived next phase as an output mode
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/derive_gate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: main
- **Detail**: --next-phase prints max(Phase over non-draft spine rows) + 1 - the basis line's phase=N derivation exposed as an output mode (no second parse, docs/gate never written), bare so the intake mint helper and any agent can int() the answer when a confirmed scope change opens a new phase; a Draft row's phase is not yet scope and never bumps the answer; an unphased spine is the implicit foundation (1), so it prints 2. The boundary it serves is ruled (owner 2026-08-01): a phase increments on an adjudication-confirmed scope change or a ratified draft-SN batch, never on the raw derived-gate drop.
- **Rationale**: One derived call keeps the boundary number mechanical - hand-picked numbers drift, and a spurious Modified window must not burn a phase number (the counterexample: 19 traced cells once flipped 11 SRs with no scope moved).

- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-052 — Dashboard accessibility (rubric-adjudicated)

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-052 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-052
- **Title**: Dashboard accessibility (rubric-adjudicated)
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-023
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The state view the delivered generators produce shall be operable and readable without a pointer or full color perception: every interactive element (tabs, expandable blocks, SVG nodes with detail panels) is keyboard-reachable and carries an accessible name (title/aria/visible text); no information is encoded by color alone (status/phase/type encodings carry a text or shape cue); text keeps readable contrast against its fill.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — an accessibility bar left unstated silently reads as no bar at all, so the obligation is stated here even though its clauses were not measurable when the row was written. They since became so, one clause at a time, which is why this row's method is mechanical: the thresholds are the child rows' and the acceptance is their chain. Hat-derived (hat.ACCESSIBILITY): keyboard operability, an accessible name per element, colour never the only channel, and a readable contrast floor are the charter's own subject, and no other lens in the roster names a measurable perceptual threshold — the UX pair reaches robustness (it does not overflow), never legibility. That dependency is now CLOSED: the ACCESSIBILITY charter was ruled `always` by the owner on 2026-08-16, so the lens this row derives from is put to every decomposition — and it had already reached this row once SN-023 carried the `a11y` tag, which is why the gap was narrower than the label first recorded. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Operating the produced view with keyboard alone reaches and activates every interactive element — tabs, expandable blocks, SVG nodes with detail panels — with no focusable element hidden inside a container assistive technology skips; every interactive element announces an accessible name that is neither empty nor a bare id; with colour perception removed, every status, phase and type encoding still reads through its text or shape cue; every text-and-fill pair clears the declared contrast floor. One unreachable or unnamed element, one colour-only encoding, or one pair under the floor is a fail; the pass is the full sweep of every emitted view, never a sampled subset.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 3
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-101 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-101
- **SR-Refs**: SR-052
- **Title**: Non-pruning SVG container roles (A2 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _svg_role/_FOCUSABLE
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): no emitted <svg> declares the CHILDREN-PRESENTATIONAL role=img over a FOCUSABLE descendant, and any svg that does declare role=img carries its own accessible name (aria-label, or a first-direct-child <title> — per SVG-AAM a <title> nested in a <g> names the <g>, not the svg). Focusable means tabindex in any quoting OR a native SVG <a href>, which is tab-ordered without a tabindex. The role is chosen from the emitted BODY by _svg_role. SCOPE — and no wider: the container-role invariant as PROVEN by TC-104, over the artifact this repo ships plus the tiered fixture plus the predicate's own unit cases; it is NOT a proof over every reachable emitter configuration, so an emitter no rendered artifact exercises is unguarded. Name QUALITY belongs to LLR-108/TC-113.
- **Rationale**: role=img prunes its subtree from the accessibility tree, so declaring it over focusable descendants hides every one of them from assistive technology while the page still looks correct. The predicate must treat a native SVG <a href> as focusable and not tabindex alone: a tabindex-only predicate passes a diagram of linked stage cards that are tab-ordered without one. Setting the role at each call site was rejected — deriving it from the body is what stops a future emitter silently reintroducing the conflict.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-104)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-108 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-108
- **SR-Refs**: SR-052
- **Title**: Accessible-name quality, not merely presence (A2 residue)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _render_drill breadcrumb label; every focusable/role=img emit site
- **Detail**: The QUALITY half of accessibility anchor A2, mechanizing what LLR-101 leaves perceptual. Three rules over every focusable element and role=img graphic: it HAS an accessible name; that name is never a bare registry id; and navigation LANDMARKS are distinct. SCOPE, narrower than a first measurement suggests: A2 governs INTERACTIVE elements and meaningful graphics — a <title> on a decorative edge path is a tooltip, not an accessible name. Uniqueness is asserted for landmarks ONLY: a descend control for the same container legitimately recurs across drill layers, one visible at a time, so those repeats are one control reached by different paths. Whether a well-formed, unique, descriptive name is the RIGHT description belongs to SR-054's reading-task critique.
- **Rationale**: A bare registry id tells a screen-reader user nothing about what the control is, and a reader listing landmarks hears a flat list in which two called 'Breadcrumb' are indistinguishable — which is what three drills produce unless the names are made distinct. Asserting over decorative graphics too was rejected: it would manufacture findings the accessibility standard does not make, and a check that cries wolf is one a reader stops reading.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-113)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-112 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-112
- **SR-Refs**: SR-052
- **Title**: Keyboard reachability of every wired control (A1 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: emitted querySelectorAll wiring; tabindex + native-link emission
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): every element the emitted page WIRES an interaction to is keyboard-focusable. The wired set is DERIVED from the document's own DOM-selecting calls and then classified — a control selector (its matches receive per-element click/focus/dblclick/keydown listeners) may match only elements carrying tabindex=0 or natively tab-ordered (a button; an SVG a-with-href, the LLR-101 predicate), and each non-control selector records why its matches are not focus stops. A newly wired selector fails the owning test until classified. The derivation reads EVERY DOM-selecting API (querySelector/querySelectorAll/closest/matches, any quote style) and fails loudly on a non-literal selector argument, which a static read cannot classify. A control-selector wiring loop that attaches click or dblclick must attach focus or keydown beside it, statically asserted. Order-integrity: document order IS emission order, no positive tabindex may reorder the walk, and tabindex=-1 is legal only on the roving tablist buttons. SCOPE — and no wider: focusability, order-integrity and activation PAIRING of the wired set over the shipped artifact plus the seven-fixture emitter sweep. Runtime behaviour equivalence is not driven, and whether the order FEELS sensible stays a reader-experience judgement. Runtime-created controls (the breadcrumb buttons) are native <button>s, keyboard-operable by construction and outside the static walk — a recorded limit.
- **Rationale**: A control reachable only by pointer is unreachable for a keyboard user, and the page still looks complete, so nothing reports it. Enumerating the controls by hand was rejected: a per-element list goes stale the moment a selector is added, whereas deriving the wired set from the document's own calls means a new selector fails until someone classifies it. Two narrowings are load-bearing and must not be relaxed back: the derivation has to read every DOM-selecting API rather than one spelling of one call, or it silently misses wired controls; and the key-handler half has to be asserted statically rather than deferred to a browser, or mouse-only wiring ships.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-117)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-113 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-113
- **SR-Refs**: SR-052
- **Title**: No information by colour alone (A3 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: tier_legend + sw/phase/status legend emitters; STATUS_GLYPH
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A3: for every declared colour vocabulary (enumerated from the module constants, the U5/U2 closure, so a new vocabulary enters by existing), every member that actually PAINTS in a rendered document resolves to a legend swatch labelled in words in that same document, with CSS tokens resolved. Status members additionally pair a per-status glyph. The emitted JS detail-badge maps are SUBSTITUTED from TIER_FILL/STATUS_FILL rather than hand-copied, and three drift guards hold every copy equal to the constants: the emitted JS maps byte-match the Python palettes; the CSS status tokens match STATUS_FILL and are asserted theme-invariant; and the tier-legend bijection derives its expectation from the TIER_FILL dict, so a member dropped from the legend also fails. SCOPE and its narrowing: the worded cue must exist in the same document — that it sits within eyeshot of the painted element is layout this does not re-assert. The JS detail-panel badge stays out of the LEGEND rule because its background carries its concept as the badge's own visible text.
- **Rationale**: Colour alone excludes any reader who cannot resolve it, and the failure is silent: the page looks informative to everyone who can. A raw hex scan was rejected as the detection method because tokenised fills under-report it — the status legend paints through a CSS variable, and a hex scan finds one member of four. The substitution and the three guards exist because a hand-copied palette copy is the recurring defect here: one stale hex shipped in three separate places, and each copy that a test shares with its emitter is a copy the test cannot catch. The same-document narrowing costs a reader nothing today (every painted member is cued in its own tab panel) and a re-attestation should bless it knowingly.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-118)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-114 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-114
- **SR-Refs**: SR-052
- **Title**: Text-contrast floors, closed over the vocabulary set (A4)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS + _ring_ink
- **Detail**: Accessibility anchor A4 bound. The WCAG 2.1 AA arithmetic is owned by six sibling tests — node fills vs label ink at 4.5:1, the emitted badge/boundary pairs, the no-opacity-discount rule, theme-token fills behind white text in BOTH themes, the hub-is-not-accent regression, and the per-fill focus ring at the 3:1 boundary floor — so this row adds the BINDING plus the gap none of them owned: the SET. Closed by reflection: every UPPERCASE module-level collection holding hex members must be one of the five declared vocabularies, and every member of the REFLECTED set clears 4.5:1 against its label ink (white, except the one declared queued dark-ink exception) and 3:1 for its computed focus ring. The walk is RECURSIVE (nested dicts, tuples, sets, bare strings), counts any 3/4/6/8-digit hex form as hex-bearing, and requires every member be CANONICAL 6-digit lowercase. The theme-token half is DERIVED from the emitted CSS: every fill:var(--x) token must carry a declared role, white-text-fill roles clear 4.5:1 under white in BOTH themes, container-fill roles keep --text readable in both. SCOPE — and no wider: contrast arithmetic over declared constants and emitted theme tokens (the ring boundary also binds under SR-054 T5 as LLR-105); a lowercase module-level palette would evade the reflection and is forbidden by the module's UPPERCASE-constant convention, a recorded reliance. Rendered-pixel measurement is not asserted.
- **Rationale**: Each sibling test sweeps a hand-copied list of the palettes, so a sixth vocabulary could ship outside every floor check while all six stayed green — the arithmetic was right and the SET was the hole. A new vocabulary must enter the arithmetic by EXISTING rather than by being remembered, which is what reflection buys and a seventh hand-copied list would not. Two shapes of that same defect must not return: a flat-dict-only walk misses nested and non-dict collections, and a hand-listed theme-token set is the very defect this row claims to close — both were live here, which is why the walk is recursive and the token set derived.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-119)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### TC TC-104 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-104
- **Verifies**: SR-052;LLR-101
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Three complementary checks. (1) Unit: _svg_role classifies a native <a href> body, and tabindex in double/single/no quotes, as NOT img, while a static graphic whose text mentions an escaped link stays img. (2) Fixture document walk: generate from a tiered registry and walk every emitted <svg> - an svg declaring role=img must hold no focusable descendant and must carry its own accessible name. (3) Shipped-artifact walk: the same invariant over this repo's own committed PROJECT_STATE.html, which exercises every emitter that actually renders (the loops diagram included) rather than only the two the fixture happens to render. The subtree split strips <script> regions and requires an attributed <svg ...> tag, because embedded JSON prose can contain a literal <svg> that both invents a phantom subtree and swallows a real one.

- **Expected**: On the shipped artifact: 44 svg containers, 0 role=img, 0 focusable descendants inside a children-presentational subtree, 1171 focusables total (non-vacuous). Asserts NOT-children-presentational rather than role=group specifically, so dropping the role or using graphics-document would also pass - the test must not pin an implementation LLR-101 leaves open.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_traj_render.py::test_a2_no_focusable_node_sits_inside_a_children_presentational_svg tests/test_traj_render.py::test_a2_role_predicate_counts_native_links_as_focusable tests/test_traj_render.py::test_a2_the_repos_own_shipped_dashboard_holds_the_invariant tests/test_traj_render.py::test_svg_nodes_carry_escaped_title_tooltips

### TC TC-118 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-118
- **Verifies**: SR-052;LLR-113
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Over the shipped dashboard plus seven fixture renders: resolve the document's token definitions, collect every legend swatch carrying a non-empty word label (var() resolved), collect every vocabulary member painted in a PAINT surface of the script-stripped markup (style blocks, style attributes, fill/stroke attributes), and assert each painted member's hex appears as a worded swatch in the SAME document; assert every vocabulary is exercised somewhere in the sweep. Plus the tier-legend bijection: the icicle legend's swatch list equals TIER_FILL in declaration order.

Verified to fail against the stale hardcoded TC swatch, a legend entry going missing (the legend-less flat seam graph was caught live on this test's first run), the stale JS map (caught live on the committed artifact before its fix), a drifted --done token, and an emitter dropping a tier member.

- **Status**: Approved
