## SR-053 — Dashboard UI uniformity (rubric-adjudicated)

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-053 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-053
- **Title**: Dashboard UI uniformity (rubric-adjudicated)
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-023
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The state view the delivered generators produce shall read as one system across its tabs and views: one type scale and spacing rhythm; one status/phase/type color vocabulary applied consistently wherever the same concept renders; uniform node, edge, legend, and detail-panel styling across the SVG emitters; the same interaction idiom (expand, hover, detail) wherever the same structure appears.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — how alike is alike enough is subjective at the margins, which is why this row states the coherence property and lets each clause of it be pinned separately rather than pinning the whole to one threshold and pinning the wrong thing. Every clause has since been pinned, so the method is mechanical and the acceptance is the child chain. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is the anchor census of the uniformity bar as it was first written — five anchors, three of them leaving a mechanized residue — not a decision count; this row states one cross-view coherence property, and every child exists because an anchor was bound to a test rather than because a second contract was merged in. Hat-derived (hat.CONSISTENCY): one meaning given two treatments across tabs, emitters and states is that charter's failure class verbatim, and it is the only lens in the roster that asks it. STATED HONESTLY RATHER THAN CLAIMED: the charter did not exist when this row was written — three independent derivations (needs-only, frame-only, hat-aware) each failed to produce this obligation, which is why it was the one row the exercise could call underivable rather than merely underived — and it was drafted at this pass (2026-08-16, finding R-4) to close that gap. The owner RULED the charter in on 2026-08-16, so this row has a deriving lens for the first time and the disposition question it carried is settled. The narrower reading survives the ruling and is the one to re-open if the charter is ever cut: no stakeholder need states cross-view coherence, so the lens — not a need — is what reaches this row. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16; charter ruled, row retained.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Across every tab, view and emitter of the produced state view: every font size resolves to a step of the one declared type scale and every spacing value to the one rhythm; every status, phase and type fill resolves to its one declared colour vocabulary, with no colour reused across unrelated vocabularies and no concept painted two ways in two places; node, edge, legend and detail-panel styling is uniform across the SVG emitters, with one highlight idiom and declared weight tokens throughout; the same structure answers to the same interaction idiom (expand, hover, detail) wherever it appears — one idiom per node role. A value outside the declared token sets, a second treatment of an already-styled concept, or a second idiom for the same role is a fail; the sweep is closed over every emitter, so a new emitter joins the bar by existing.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 3
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-102 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-102
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: One-concept-per-colour de-collision (U5 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U5 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md): no hex value is reused across STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS for an unrelated concept (the intentional TIER_FILL<->OKF_TYPE_FILL SN/SR/LLR/TC mirror is the one exception, asserted as a bijection rather than a set match), no PHASE_ACCENTS entry equals the CSS --accent token, and PHASE_ACCENTS clears a PAIRWISE (not merely adjacent) deltaE floor of 15. SCOPE: hex-identity and deltaE across these five declared palettes; the near-duplicate residue across vocabularies belongs to LLR-109/TC-114.
- **Rationale**: One hex carrying two concepts makes the legend a lie in both directions, and a phase sharing the --accent token makes that phase's focus and hover ring invisible — a control the reader cannot see they have reached. Comparing only ADJACENT entries was rejected: a palette is read as a whole, so two members that collide are a collision wherever they sit in the list.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-105)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-103 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-103
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Uniform node/edge/legend/highlight styling (U3 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _ring_ink/_ring_style/when_view legend
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U3 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md): the hover/focus highlight ring resolves to ONE shared per-fill `--ring` custom property across every SVG emitter (drill/icicle/flat-DAG/knowledge) rather than a different hue per emitter, and the When tab's phase-accent key renders through the SAME `.legend`/`<i>` component every other legend in the document uses rather than a bespoke inline-chip idiom. SCOPE: highlight-token equality and legend-component equality, asserted structurally against the emitted CSS and markup. Spacing and exact visual weight belong to LLR-110/TC-115.
- **Rationale**: A highlight that changes hue between views teaches the reader a different interaction language per tab, and a legend built twice drifts in one copy while the other stays right. Per-emitter hues were the shipped state (an accent token in the drill views, a hardcoded amber elsewhere) and were rejected precisely because nothing held them equal: sharing one token means a change lands everywhere or nowhere.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-106)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-104 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-104
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Process-tab node-label type scale (U1 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: --nhead/.stgt/.stgn/.hooplab/.hubname
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md): the Process tab's working-loops SVG no longer hardcodes 12px/9.5px/13px/13px on .stgt/.stgn/.hooplab/.hubname. .stgt/.stgn are the same per-node-label role as the shared --nlabel/--nsub tokens the icicle, dag and knowledge views already share, and reuse them directly; .hooplab/.hubname are a once-per-diagram headline label, a genuinely different role, so they take the one documented --nhead scale step. SCOPE: these four selectors resolve to a declared token, asserted structurally against the emitted CSS. Whether the sizes read as uniform belongs to LLR-111/TC-116.
- **Rationale**: A magic number is invisible to every later change: two independently-drifting literals doing one job diverge the first time somebody adjusts one of them. Collapsing all four selectors onto the node-label tokens was rejected because a once-per-diagram headline is a different role, and forcing one token onto two roles is the same defect in the other direction.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-107)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-106 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-106
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Single-sourced concept colour vocabulary (U2 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: SW_NODE_FILL/STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Every concept's fill is LOOKED UP from a declared module-level palette, so the same concept cannot render two hexes — there is only one place a hex can come from. Concretely: every #rrggbb appearing in a rendered dashboard is either a member of a declared palette collection or a `--token:#hex` theme definition (surfaces, text and borders, which encode no concept). SCOPE: this proves single-sourcing of the VOCABULARY, not that a concept was assigned a well-chosen hue — collision and perceptual separation belong to LLR-102.
- **Rationale**: A vocabulary is one vocabulary only if every member is in it: a bare hex literal outside the declared collection is invisible to the collision sweep and to the ring-ink enumeration alike, so it evades both the checks that would catch it. That is not hypothetical — a four-kind vocabulary declared only three because one badge fill sat inline in its emitter. Auditing hues by review was rejected: review cannot see a member that was never declared.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-109)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-107 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-107
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: One interaction idiom per node role (U4 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_views.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _drill_layer_svg/arch_icicle/dag_svg/know_graph_svg node groups
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Two node roles exist and each is spelled ONE way in every emitter that renders it: an IDENTIFIED node (icicle .cell, flat-DAG .wi, knowledge .knode) advertises its registry id through `data-id`; a DETAIL-BEARING drill node (.block, in both the When and How-SW drills) advertises its detail-map key through `data-node`; and a node of either role is made focusable the same way, `tabindex="0"` on the group. Neither role ever carries the other's attribute. SCOPE: ATTRIBUTE parity, not runtime behaviour parity.
- **Rationale**: One node addressed two ways means every later consumer must handle both, and the second spelling is the one that gets forgotten. Proving that two emitters' handlers BEHAVE alike was considered and rejected as out of reach without a browser harness — what is provable from the markup is that they are wired through one vocabulary, and that is the narrowing this row accepts explicitly rather than claiming more than it checks.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-110)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-109 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-109
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Pairwise deltaE floor across every vocabulary (U5 residue)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
- **Detail**: Mechanizes what LLR-102 leaves perceptual: a PAIRWISE deltaE floor across every declared vocabulary, not only within PHASE_ACCENTS. Two floors, each a recorded judgement — 15 WITHIN a vocabulary (every member can sit beside every other in one legend) and 12 ACROSS (two vocabularies share no legend, so the floor is lower rather than absent). The declared tier<->okf mirror is exempt BY DESIGN and is asserted BYTE-EQUAL, so 'exempt' cannot drift into 'nearly equal'. SCOPE: perceptual SEPARATION of the declared palettes. Whether a given hue suits its concept is not asserted.
- **Rationale**: 'A collision the identity check misses' looked like a judgement call only because the formula was already there and nobody had widened the set it ran over — the same arithmetic on more members. A cross-vocabulary floor of zero was rejected on evidence: a reader conflated a pair drawn from two different vocabularies, so sharing no legend reduces the risk without removing it. Three live conflations were sitting in the shipped palettes at the binding — sw external/component at deltaE 8.6, SR/phase[3] at 9.5, Interface/phase[7] at 10.5.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-114)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-110 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-110
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Declared weight/alpha/corner tokens (U3 residue)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: --w-*/--o-*/--r-* tokens; SVG_RX
- **Detail**: Mechanizes what LLR-103 leaves perceptual. Fourteen CSS tokens plus SVG_RX name one step per role for stroke-width, opacity and corner radius, near-duplicates merged into the role's step, and every emitted value must resolve to a declared token. stroke-opacity is checked under the opacity scale deliberately — it is the same scale applied to a stroke. SVG_RX is enforced against both the source literals and every render, because the rx presentation attribute cannot read a CSS custom property portably. SCOPE: token membership, asserted over paint surfaces (style blocks and style/fill/stroke attributes), never over prose that quotes CSS.
- **Rationale**: 'Spacing and exact visual weight' was undecidable because the weights were never declared: the shipped page carried 8 stroke-widths, 7 opacities, 5 corner radii and 6 SVG rx values, of which five stroke widths were doing the single job 'draw a connector' and four rx values 'round a node box'. Nothing can be uniform against an undeclared scale, so declaring the roles is what turns the judgement into a check; leaving it to review was rejected because a near-duplicate weight is exactly what an eye does not catch.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-115)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-111 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-111
- **SR-Refs**: SR-053
- **Title**: Declared type scale, no raw font-size literals (U1 residue)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: --nlabel/--nsub/--nhead + --tiny/--xsmall/--small/--body/--lead/--display/--hero + --rel
- **Detail**: Mechanizes what LLR-104 left perceptual. 'Do the sizes read as visually uniform' was undecidable because the scale was never written down: 18 raw font-size literals against 5 tokens, with .7/.75rem, .9/.95/.98rem, 1.05/1.1rem and 8.5/9px each being near-duplicate steps for ONE role 3-7% apart. Eleven steps are now declared in THREE documented families - node (px, because SVG geometry is fixed px and a rem resizes labels out of their boxes), page (rem), and one relative step (em, for text sized against its parent) - and every emitted font-size must resolve to one. Claiming a single scale across those units would be false, so the LLR states three. SCOPE: step membership over paint surfaces. Whether the chosen steps are well-proportioned is not asserted.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-116)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

## SR-054 — Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-054 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-054
- **Title**: Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-023
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The state view the delivered generators produce shall support its core reading tasks with low friction: a first-time reviewer finds the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect, each within one tab switch; views default to a legible density (start-collapsed per the greater-than-3 rule) and reveal detail without losing context; labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — task-level usability is perceptual (is this findable, is this legible), so a test can confirm an element exists and not that a reader can use it. The bar is therefore written from the stakeholder intent rather than from the test, because a test written by the builder encodes the builder's own idea of usable. Most of it turned out to be pinnable and is now held mechanically; one clause about a first-time reader's experience is not, and it rests on a recorded one-time judgement rather than on a standing re-judgement — the residue named at the child, and stated there as a limit rather than implied as coverage. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is that same anchor census, not a decision count — this row states one perceptual property, and each anchor pinned to a test as it was mechanized left an LLR behind, so the fan-out rises as the row is mechanized. Hat-derived (hat.UX-DESIGNER + hat.UX-ENGINEER): findability is the designer's — a declared reader and the decision answered without hunting (C-UXD-1) — and legibility-as-robustness is the engineer's — real widths and real volumes, nothing clipped or overlapping (C-UXE-2). Both are `always` in this repo, so unlike its two siblings this row's deriving lens is reachable today. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A first-time reviewer reaches each core reading task — the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect — through a labelled entry point within one tab switch; every view whose element count exceeds the declared threshold starts collapsed, and revealing detail preserves the return path to the parent context; at default zoom, over the real registry volumes rather than a trimmed fixture, no label is clipped, overflows its own box, or crosses an unrelated element. Any entry point missing or unlabelled, any oversized view starting expanded, any lost return path, or any illegible label is a fail. The one clause no automated check holds — that a first-time reader in fact finds the entry points usable — rests on a recorded human judgement, a stated limit rather than an implied coverage.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 3
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-055 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-055
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Dashboard usability rendering
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_views.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_html/_nav/when_view
- **Detail**: Adjudicated against the usability rubric (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md) by the LLR-048/LLR-082 critique loop: labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text, and the reading experience the rubric's remaining anchors describe. Byte-deterministic; --check unchanged. This row keeps ONLY what a test cannot pin: the mechanized cores have left for child rows that name the tests owning them — T2 start-collapse (LLR-099/TC-102), T3 breadcrumb-restores-parent (LLR-100/TC-103, the whole anchor, no residue here) and T1 labelled entry (LLR-115/TC-120, the whole anchor, no residue here). Do NOT restate a child's claim here: an anchor lives in exactly one row.
- **Rationale**: A critique that re-judges what a test already enforces spends its budget twice and can disagree with the harness, so an anchor whose bar becomes operational leaves this row entirely rather than being kept as a duplicate. T3 and T1 are the two that left whole: a critique cannot verify T3's return path from a static shot set while a test can click the crumb, and T1's 'obvious' was always glossed as labelled-not-unlabelled, which is mechanizable once written that way.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-055)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-099 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-099
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Default-density start-collapse (T2 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: know_view/sw_view/_render_drill
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): a view whose scale EARNS tiering — a bundle spanning more than 3 OKF types, a module set above the component threshold (the LLR-052 '>3' rule) — renders START-COLLAPSED as the shared type-tiered drill, one block per type or container, rather than a flat wall of nodes; at or below the threshold it stays flat. Covers the Knowledge, How-SW and landing What views; the What icicle start-collapses to one block per SN with descend-on-click, reusing _drill_layer_svg/_render_drill so the interaction idiom is single-sourced. SCOPE: T2's remaining 'reads legibly at default density' judgement stays perceptual under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: Tiering imposed on a small project costs a click to reach what already fitted on one screen, so it is earned by scale rather than switched on globally. Capping DEPTH instead of collapsing was rejected and the emitter records why: height is leaf-proportional, so stopping at the SR lane still stacks one unit per SR and the wall returns. The What icicle is the case that forced the widening — it rendered the whole spine at LEAF scale, one unit per test case.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-102)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-100 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-100
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Detail-in-context breadcrumb (T3 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _render_drill/DRILL_SCRIPT
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T3 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): descending a container never replaces the view with no way back — the drill emits a declared breadcrumb nav (<nav class="crumbs"> with a data-root-crumb root label) whose crumb click TRUNCATES the trail to that ancestor, restoring the parent view. SCOPE: the WHOLE anchor — the breadcrumb's presence AND its restore behaviour; T3 is retired as a critique anchor and no residue is left under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: A drill that descends with no way back strands the reader in a view they cannot leave, and 'keeps the reader oriented' never independently passed or failed a round — the breadcrumb IS that orientation, so the claim becomes testable only once it is the thing asserted. Leaving the anchor to critique was rejected because a critique CANNOT verify it: a shot matrix captures initial views only, so a round can pass on markup while the return path itself stays unverified, whereas TC-103 clicks the crumb. It follows that a critic finding T3 violated has found a gap in TC-103, which routes through change-intake to harden this test rather than through a verdict.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-103)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-105 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-105
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Interactive-control contrast in both themes (T5 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _ring_ink/_ring_style/_cedge_marker
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T5 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): EVERY control painted over a node's own fill clears the WCAG 3:1 UI-boundary floor against EVERY node fill declared in STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS, in BOTH themes — computed per fill (white or near-black, whichever contrasts more), never a single fixed hue. Covers the descend/hover/focus ring AND the containment/descend arrow (.cedge shaft plus its marker head), which derives from the same per-fill --ring token; its marker head takes a per-ink marker because marker content is rendered from the defs tree and cannot see the referencing node's custom properties (a constraint recorded in _arrow_markers), and RING_INKS is the closed ink set that makes that emission total. SCOPE: a control's contrast against its host fill, arithmetic like the sibling A4 tests. Whether the affordance otherwise reads as inviting stays perceptual under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: A fixed hue vanishes on whichever fill happens to match it, and the failure is total where it lands: the measured collision put a control at 1.00:1 against its host, i.e. invisible. Fixing only the ring was rejected once the same fixed token was found on the descend ARROW — the very control a reader must find to descend a tier — measuring 1.06:1 light and 1.99:1 dark on a shipped phase fill. One control's fix does not generalise; deriving every such control from the host fill does.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-108)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-115 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-115
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Task findability - labelled entry within one tab switch (T1 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_html/_next_work_html/_nav
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Each of the three core reading tasks the rubric concretizes — find the project state, find the next work, find how the parts connect — is reachable within one tab switch of the landing view, its entry point a LABELLED nav control or a NAMED surface on the landing view, never an unlabelled region a reader must discover by clicking. THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT that makes the check honest: it is judged against the RENDERED artifact carrying real registry data, NEVER the nav skeleton. The guard resolves each task's entry point from the emitted document (task 1 -> the landing Definition-completeness/Execution cards, 0 switches; task 2 -> the landing Next-work card, 0 switches; task 3 -> the How-SW nav tab, 1 switch), asserts each is LABELLED and within one switch, and asserts the next-work surface is NON-EMPTY on the zero-active/all-queued fixture. SCOPE — PROVEN: a labelled entry point per task, reachable in one switch, and the next-work surface non-empty across the practice fixtures (zero-active, waiting-with-predecessor, drained). DELIBERATELY NOT: whether the reader subjectively knows what to do next; T1 leaves the critique entirely, with no residue under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: 'Obvious' was always glossed as labelled-rather-than-unlabelled, and that gloss is the whole bar — once it is written operationally there is nothing perceptual left to adjudicate, so the anchor can leave the critique instead of being re-judged every round. The rendered-artifact constraint is the load-bearing part and must not be relaxed to a structural check: a next-work surface can EXIST in the emitter and render nothing when no row is active, so a nav-skeleton check passes on exactly the artifact a reader finds empty.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-120)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-116 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-116
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Viewport-fit by scale-to-fit with a legibility floor (T7 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _svg_fit_style/SHRINK_FLOOR
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T7 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Every emitted SVG (all three wrappers: _svg_wrap for dag/sw/know, the icicle panel, _drill_layer_svg) carries width:100% + max-width:<natural> + min-width:<natural x SHRINK_FLOOR> instead of a FIXED pixel width, so a diagram fills its container and scales down as the viewport narrows. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowing: a view whose natural width exceeds 390 / SHRINK_FLOOR still scrolls horizontally WITH its cue — the row's own fallback rule made mechanical. Closing that remainder needs a narrow-width re-layout (stacked columns), which is not this row.
- **Rationale**: A fixed pixel width pins the rendered size whatever the container does, which is why every narrow view demanded sideways scroll and one graph clipped a component label mid-word. A viewBox alone cannot fix it — the fixed width still wins. The legibility floor exists because pure scale-to-fit TRADES this anchor for T4: a 900px graph squeezed into 390px shrinks a label past readable, so the floor stops the fix from creating the defect it is next to.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-121)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-117 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-117
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Theme-lock: one theme mechanism and no mixed-family surface/ink pair (T6 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: HTML_TEMPLATE theme tokens + every emitter's paint
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T6 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). The measurable content is FAMILY PAIRING, not the absence of literals: a fixed status swatch carrying fixed white ink is theme-locked CORRECTLY, since neither side flips. A page inverts mid-view in exactly two ways and this row forbids both. (1) A SECOND THEME MECHANISM below :root — the document declares exactly one prefers-color-scheme block, targeting :root, and one color-scheme, also at :root. (2) A MIXED PAIR — a theme-varying ink on a theme-invariant surface, or the reverse. The two families are DERIVED from the emitted document (the dark block's override set IS the definition of theme-varying), never hand-listed, and the dark block may not override a token :root never declares. Applied to CSS rules declaring both a background and an ink, to every emitted SVG node pairing an inline rect fill with an inline text fill, and to CSS-declared SVG text fills paired against the family their host nodes actually paint in. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: --ring is excluded because it is stamped FROM the host fill (LLR-105 owns its floor); the JS-composed badge idiom pairs background and ink at runtime from the invariant vocabulary, where LLR-114's arithmetic owns it; and the page CSS has exactly ONE rule declaring a classifiable background AND ink today (body), so that half is prospective while the sweep's weight sits on the node and text-fill halves. Whether the chosen palettes are PLEASANT stays perceptual.
- **Rationale**: A per-component theme override is a seam the reader crosses while scrolling, and a mixed pair reads correctly in the theme it was authored in and inverts in the other — both are invisible to the author working in one theme. Forbidding colour LITERALS was the obvious rule and is the wrong one: it fails a correct fixed-on-fixed pairing and passes a varying-on-invariant one, so it would flag the safe case and miss the defect. Deriving the families from the emitted document rather than listing them means a new token joins a family by existing.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-122)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-119 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-119
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: T4 label legibility, mechanized: ink inside its box, truncation with a reveal
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _drill_block_label/_fit_lines/_next_work_title
- **Detail**: The mechanized floor of usability anchor T4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md), widening the anchor's existing owner rather than adding a rubric anchor. The clause made objective: no character a block renders may fall outside that block's own rect, horizontally or vertically. The emitter FITS the sub-label to the column it will actually be drawn in — a second line when one will not hold it, then an ellipsis — with three text lines the ceiling, reusing the grid the id/name wrap idiom already proved fits row_h; a sub-label that already fits one line renders byte-identically to before. SCOPE — PROVEN: for every block of every drill layer in the emitted document, each label line's ink box lies inside its own node rect, measured from the emitted geometry with the emitter's declared per-character widths and the document's declared font sizes, entities counted as the single glyph they render. Second half: this emitter does not truncate the next-work title by a fixed budget — the card emits the whole clause and lets its own width do the fitting, the declared bound _NEXT_WORK_TITLE caps ONE item's HEIGHT rather than its text, and where it bites the remainder discloses through a NATIVE <details> cut at a word so head and remainder rejoin continuously when opened. DELIBERATELY NOT: whether an ellipsis a reader meets is ACTIONABLE — truncated-without-affordance stays T4's perceptual residue under LLR-055/TC-055, so T4 remains a live critique anchor; the full string keeps its homes in the block's title, aria-label and data-summary.
- **Rationale**: A label is illegible AS a label the moment its ink leaves the box it names, which is the half of this anchor a reader cannot argue with. Column right-sizing (LLR-057) cannot absorb it: right-sizing clamps at MAX_TIER_COL, so a 400-character need asks for a 2000px column and gets 172 — the overflow is structural, not a tuning problem. A FIXED character budget was the shipped approach on the next-work card and is rejected: it spends the same budget at every width, cutting a title mid-word on a wide screen with the card half empty. Native <details> is the point of the reveal — operable by pointer AND keyboard with no script, in a file opened from disk, which is what makes the affordance assertable from the markup instead of needing a browser. The height bound is measured over 320 registry rows: median 44, p90 126, max 609 characters of leading clause.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-124)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-120 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-120
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: No edge through an unrelated node box (T8 objective floor)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_graph.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _route_edges/_detour_d/_polyline_hits
- **Detail**: The mechanized floor of usability anchor T8 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). T8 is two clauses and this row claims only the first: NO EDGE PASSES THROUGH AN UNRELATED NODE BOX. SCOPE — PROVEN: across the shipped dashboard plus one fixture per emitter, every emitted wire, sampled as the polyline a reader's eye follows, clears every node rect that is not its own source or target. MEASURED at the binding: 264 shipped wires, 0 through a box. DELIBERATELY NOT: crossing count, lane separation, or where a crossing falls — that residue stays under LLR-055/TC-055, which is why T8 remains a live critique anchor. Separately and NOT claimed here: at the router's own 3px clearance rather than the bare box, one wire does graze, so _detour_d exhausting its lane candidates and returning the least-bad fallback is a reachable state rather than a theoretical one; that wire's identity and the lane-separation work belong to the open work item.
- **Rationale**: A wire crossing an unrelated box reads as a connection that is not there, which is a wrong answer rather than an untidy one — geometry a reader cannot argue with. The claim about the ARTIFACT is deliberately separate from the router's own unit tests and is why this row exists: _detour_d returns a LEAST-BAD fallback when no candidate lane clears, so 'the algorithm detours' does not imply 'nothing shipped through a box', and the artifact-level claim had only ever been hand-verified once.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-125)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-055 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-055
- **Verifies**: SR-054;LLR-055
- **Level**: Critique
- **Method**: A fresh, family-heterogeneous CRITIQUE session (LLR-048/LLR-082 loop) adjudicates the generated PROJECT_STATE.html against docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md, receiving the rubric + SN/SR intent + the rendered PNG matrix from scripts/dashboard-shots/shoot.mjs (widths x themes x tabs) as the artifact recipe and never the implementer's self-assessment; VERDICT machine line cites numbered anchors.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: APPROVE citing numbered anchors. The rubric is the single home of the live-vs-retired anchor set (its header states the live set; each retired anchor carries its retirement and binding in place): a verdict cites only anchors the rubric lists live and never a retired one, and a clause a test now holds is verified through the LLR/TC chain the registry records, not by a verdict. (The live/retired enumeration formerly copied here was the third home of that fact and is deliberately not restated.) STANDING LIMIT, stated so this row is never read as live coverage: the verdict recorded in Evidence is a ONE-TIME judgement, not a repeating one. Nothing re-fires it — the staleness guard that would selects on a parent method this row's parent no longer declares, and re-arming it was refused (an independent critical eye is an expensive judge and an any-change trigger would spend it on nearly every change). So this row's assurance is as old as its Evidence and the surface it judged has moved since; a clause that needs assurance newer than that is one to bind to a test, not to re-judge here.
- **Automated**: No
- **Evidence**: docs/reviews/074-CRITIQUE.md
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 3

### TC TC-108 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-108
- **Verifies**: SR-054;LLR-105
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Two halves, one floor. (a) Compute _ring_ink for every fill declared in STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS and assert the WCAG contrast ratio against that fill is >= 3, for every fill -- not a hand-picked example. (b) resolve what a browser actually paints for the containment arrow's shaft (.cedge stroke) and marker head (.cedgehead fill) -- mirroring CSS var() fallback against each block's inline --ring and the theme's --accent -- and assert the same >= 3:1 over the same closed fill set in BOTH themes, plus the theme-token (var(--surface)) container fills the fallback still serves; then assert on the RENDERED artifact that every emitted arrow references a marker whose own --ring equals its host block's, since a shaft that passes while a shared head is painted from one hue is precisely the defect.

- **Expected**: Every declared fill clears >= 3:1 against its computed ring ink; worst case observed ~4.58:1. Half (b) was proven against the pre-fix code first, failing at the critique's measured 1.0596:1 (#4f46e5 on #0369a1).

- **Verifies**: SR-054;LLR-117

- **Method**: Sweep EVERY emitter document (the shipped artifact plus a fixture per emitter, the A2-review rule that a walk judges only what its fixture renders) and, per document: derive the theme-varying/-invariant token families from the emitted CSS; assert exactly one prefers-color-scheme block and one color-scheme declaration, both at :root; assert body's own background and ink are theme-varying; assert no CSS rule pairs a background and an ink from two families; assert no emitted SVG node pairs an inline rect fill with an inline text fill from two families. Then, accumulated ACROSS the sweep (because one document cannot distinguish -paints on the page background- from -this emitter does not render those nodes-), assert every CSS-declared SVG text fill matches the family its host nodes actually paint, or is theme-varying when it has no host anywhere.

Guard proven to bite on four independent mutations, each caught by its own assertion: dropping --text from the dark block (half-flip), a phase block's ink switched to var(--text) over its fixed fill, #ice .cell text switched to var(--text) over its invariant host, and a second prefers-color-scheme block scoped to .detail. Source restored byte-identical after each.

### TC TC-124 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-124
- **Verifies**: SR-054;LLR-119
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Generate the dashboard over a tiered fixture carrying one sentence-length stakeholder need (the shape that overflowed), then sweep EVERY drill block in the emitted document - not a per-view list, because the defect lives in the one shared label emitter that feeds four views. For each block, take its rect and each label line's own x/dy from the markup, measure the line's ink with the emitter's declared per-character widths and the font sizes the emitted CSS declares (unescaping entities first, so an apostrophe is one glyph and not six), and assert the ink box lies inside the rect on both axes. Assert the sentence-length need is actually among the swept labels, so the sweep cannot pass vacuously; separately assert the fit path itself (a second line broken on a word, then an ellipsis, with the full string still reachable) and that a sub-label which already fits keeps the untouched two-line grid. Second half: render the next-work card from a fixture carrying the 109-character _NW_LONG clause and assert it appears WHOLE with no ellipsis in the list, the fixture asserted longer than the retired 60-character budget so the check cannot pass vacuously; then, from a fixture past the declared bound, assert a native details/summary with a visible cue, that the emitted head and remainder rejoin to exactly the clause and the cut fell on a word, and that the reveal is neither script-dependent nor hidden by the emitter's own CSS.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Parameters**: tests/test_gen_trajectory.py (_spine_with_a_long_need: 8 SNs, SN-001 carrying the real 138-character need); NW_HEADER fixtures for the next-work card (_NW_LONG, the 109-character title clause, and one past the bound)
- **Expected**: Zero label lines outside their own block across every drill block of the emitted document; the sentence-length need present among them. Guard proven to bite on the original defect: reverted to the pre-fix emitter it fails naming SN-001's ink at x=-241..449 in a block spanning x=18..190, and the fit-path and helper guards fail with it, while the two-line-grid guard correctly passes both before and after (it guards over-application, not the defect). Source restored byte-identical. Second half: the 109-character clause renders whole; past the bound the disclosure is present, operable and lossless. Both proven to bite on the pre-fix emitter, which renders the critic's exact string 'tiering expo…' and has no _NEXT_WORK_TITLE bound at all. Source restored byte-identical.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_traj_views.py::test_t4_no_block_label_renders_outside_its_own_box tests/test_traj_views.py::test_t4_a_sentence_length_sub_label_wraps_and_ellipsizes tests/test_traj_views.py::test_t4_a_short_sub_label_keeps_the_two_line_grid tests/test_traj_views.py::test_fit_lines_breaks_on_words_then_ellipsizes tests/test_traj_panels.py::test_t4_next_work_title_is_not_budgeted_by_character_count tests/test_traj_panels.py::test_t4_an_over_bound_next_work_title_discloses_operably
- **Status**: Approved

## SR-070 — Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkable

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-070 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-070
- **Title**: Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkable
- **SN-Refs**: SN-010;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered generator set shall derive each artifact it produces from the tracked registries alone — self-contained and usable without network access, byte-stable across regeneration from unchanged sources, and carrying a freshness contract that fails when the committed copy has drifted from those sources — omitting a view whose source registries the adopting repository does not carry rather than emitting an empty one.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-010 and the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check, by isolating one independently reviewable obligation formerly carried by SR-038; with SN-012, since a repository that carries no source registry pays nothing for the view that registry would have fed. SPLIT 2026-08-16 (re-tier v2 S4, ruling R1 — one decision per row): this row had accreted the SR-census's "natural parent of the generators cluster" role and carried sixteen direct children spanning two unrelated obligations — the INTEGRITY of a generated artifact, and what the state view must SHOW. Those fail independently: a view can be perfectly offline, deterministic and drift-checkable while showing nothing about the work in flight or how the parts connect. The content half sheds along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws ("progress AND how its parts connect") to SR-168 and SR-169; this row keeps the artifact-integrity contract alone. The subject is "each artifact the generator set produces" rather than "each view" deliberately: the knowledge-export bundle is not a view, and a fourth row minted to hold it would be the filler tier the ruling forbids. The child/parent phase spread the 2026-08-14 ledger recorded as INTENDED is untouched — this split is by observable class, not by phase, which that ledger considered and rejected.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Regeneration from unchanged sources is byte-identical; a generated artifact opens and stays usable with no network access and no external fetch; a committed copy that has drifted from its sources fails its freshness contract while a current one passes; a repository lacking a source registry omits that view and stays byte-stable rather than emitting an empty one.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: trajectory

## SR-111 — Kit-version stamp

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-111 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-111
- **Title**: Kit-version stamp
- **SN-Refs**: SN-001;SN-007
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered scaffold generator shall record a kit-version stamp carrying the kit commit SHA and date, marked -dirty on an uncommitted kit tree.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-007 — without a recorded origin an adopter cannot tell which kit version they are on, so a re-sync degrades from a diff into a guess and the ADOPTING.md re-sync procedure has no baseline to diff against. Deriving the version from file hashes was rejected: it reports that something differs without saying from what. The stamp is what lets a maintainer identify the upstream version a scaffold came from and compute a re-sync diff. Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER): C-MNT-7 — "every generated view shall identify itself as generated and name the inputs it was generated from" — is the MAINTAINER clause this row answers, and a scaffold carrying no recorded origin cannot answer it. The clause is DEFINED in the recorded derivation (`docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md`, hat.MAINTAINER §C-MNT-7), not in the roster file itself; the citation was challenged on 2026-08-16 as unsupported (adversarial round, finding F10) and the source was verified, so the label stands with its home named — and the sentence above stands without it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The recorded stamp carries the kit commit SHA and date; a scaffold generated from an uncommitted kit tree is marked -dirty.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-121 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-121
- **SR-Refs**: SR-111
- **Title**: Kit-version stamp
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/bootstrap.py
- **CodeSymbol**: write_kit_version
- **Detail**: write_kit_version stamps docs/kit-version (SHA + date, -dirty on an uncommitted kit tree) so a re-sync is diffable.
- **Rationale**: Split from the idempotent-write row: the two obligations share a module but not a failure mode — a scaffold can write correctly and still leave the adopter unable to say what they scaffolded from.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 1

### TC TC-011 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-011
- **Verifies**: SR-011;LLR-011;SR-111;LLR-121
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the bootstrap suite; a re-run leaves existing files unchanged, --force overwrites, and docs/kit-version is stamped.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-011 and SR-111 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_bootstrap.py
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-112 — Checked per-agent skill fan-out

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-112 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-112
- **Title**: Checked per-agent skill fan-out
- **SN-Refs**: SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The per-agent skill copies shall be a checked, generated fan-out of the one neutral skill source.
- **Rationale**: Realizes the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check — one skill maintained in three agent-specific copies rots into three different skills, and the copy an agent actually loads is the one nobody edited. Maintaining a single shared directory was rejected because each agent harness looks in its own path; the fan-out is forced, so the copies are generated and drift is a finding. Generated marking is what stops a maintainer editing a disposable copy. Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER): the same clause's other half — C-MNT-7's "identify itself as generated" — and one skill fanned into three per-agent copies is that surface multiplied by the number of harnesses. Same disposition as SR-111 on 2026-08-16 (adversarial round, finding F10): the clause is defined in the recorded derivation (`docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md`, hat.MAINTAINER §C-MNT-7) rather than in the roster file, the source was verified, and the sentence above stands without the citation. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A per-agent copy that diverges byte-for-byte from source is detectable and refreshed by one command.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-114 — Kit scripts run across the supported OSes

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-114 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-114
- **Title**: Kit scripts run across the supported OSes
- **SN-Refs**: SN-003;SN-011
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The kit scripts shall run on Python 3.11+ across Linux, Windows and macOS.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-011 — a script that works only where its author ran it turns portability into a defect the adopter finds, and the path, line-ending and locking differences that break it are invisible on a single platform. Testing one platform and reading the others by inspection was rejected: every one of those differences has been a real failure here. The macOS exclusion argument: the 3.11 floor is a platform-agnostic language/stdlib guarantee already exercised on Linux and Windows, while macOS's job is OS-specific behavior (fcntl.flock, paths, git worktrees) that does not vary by Python version — a redundant-coverage call, not a runner-availability workaround (3.11 has arm64 macOS builds).
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The CI matrix spans Linux, Windows and macOS on Python 3.11 and latest (3.x) and is green; the macOS+3.11 cell is excluded as a redundant-coverage call recorded in the rationale; the hosted matrix as an evidence surface is SR-152's contract.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Analysis
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: portability

## SR-129 — Registry representation migration

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-129 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-129
- **Title**: Registry representation migration
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall convert the work-item registry between its current and legacy representations without losing or altering any cell, refusing a conversion whose declared inputs it cannot vouch for and any conversion attempted while either representation is in use.
- **Rationale**: RE-VOICED 2026-08-17 (Sol F15, applied): the shall named the spec folder layout (status = directory, TOML frontmatter, Deliverable in the body), the retired flat CSV, and the drained-stop mechanics - implementation and history at the requirement tier, with no artifact-altitude waiver recorded. Those move to where they belong: layout and claim detection to LLR-136, the current-and-legacy carriers to that design row's cells as current-carrier evidence (moved off the acceptance cell at the 2026-08-17 item-19 ruling). What the row states is the capability. The 140-cell lesson: an unproven representation change is where a registry silently loses cells. The converter is the migration path every adopter of the folder home takes, and the single spec writer the filing seam reuses (concurrency-restructure spec, Phase 2). Hat-derived (hat.TEST-ENGINEER): a converter is the check on a representation change, and the charter's second half — can it be shown to fail when it should — is exactly what a cell-exact round-trip over the live registry provides; the 140-cell lesson in this row's own rationale is that failure class already observed once. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A verification pass over a populated registry exits 0 reporting cell-exact equality; an unknown status refuses naming the row; a round-trip from the current representation through the legacy one and back is parse-equal, including a hand-filed spec with no order key; an in-flight claim refuses conversion by name.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 4
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-136 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-136
- **SR-Refs**: SR-129
- **Title**: Registry converter (CSV <-> spec folder)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/wi_convert.py
- **CodeSymbol**: to_specs/to_csv/verify/write_spec_file
- **Detail**: Converts between the spec folder (the live docs/work/ home) and the retired CSV form (docs/requirements/work-items.csv) per the Phase 2a grammar (TOML +++ frontmatter, Deliverable in the body, status from the directory, order key only as a CSV-order migration artifact). --verify round-trips CSV->specs->CSV in a temp dir, exits nonzero on any cell mismatch, and reports byte identity with a reason. write_spec_file is the single spec writer (to_specs and plan_artifacts filing both go through it). Refuses unknown statuses by row, non-empty targets without --force, and any in-flight active/<branch>/ claim by name (status is the FIRST path component, the loaders' rule; conversion is a drained-stop operation per §3.2 - the Phase 4 claim protocol made the state reachable).
- **Rationale**: One writer keeps migration and filing byte-compatible; refusal over catch-all keeps a malformed registry loud rather than silently reclassified.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-129)

## SR-137 — One policy home, with a checked shape

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-137 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-137
- **Title**: One policy home, with a checked shape
- **SN-Refs**: SN-028
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-01;B-04
- **Requirement**: The kit shall read every process policy dial from a single policy declaration home whose line shape is a checked contract (one key = value per line under a bare [section] header; no dotted keys, inline tables or multi-line strings), refusing — never resolving by precedence — a repo in which any dial is declared both there and in its legacy one-word file.
- **Rationale**: Two grammars read this file — `tomllib` in Python and the three git hooks in pure sh, so a Python-less box still fails closed on a declared privacy gate (M-42). TOML is far more expressive than a `grep -E` can follow, so every shape only one reader understands is a silent flip of a security gate; the first cut shipped five such shapes, three of them fail-OPEN. Narrowing the shape and CHECKING the narrowing is what makes one file safe for two readers. ONE HOME, settled 2026-08-15: the two-grammars-agree observable — the same answer from the hooks' sh and `tomllib` over the adversarial file-shape table — is SR-031's and SR-031's alone. Both rows claimed it and the texts had already DIVERGED, only SR-031's naming the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN, which is the whole reason the table exists. The partition that survives: SR-031 states the package-wide coherence property (every enforcer reads one value per dial, both grammars agreeing) at B-05; this row states the dial's HOME and its CHECKED SHAPE, and the refusals a session gets back, at B-01/B-04. What stays here is the shape check's own fail-safe direction — a shape only one reader understands is refused AND still reads ON in both — because that is a property of the narrowing, not of the comparison. CROSSING ATTRIBUTION EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED 2026-08-15 (the act-7 revision ["B-01","B-02"] -> ["B-01","B-04"], applied and flagged for overrule): read against external.toml, B-02 carries exactly three things - rulings, attestations and Status flips - and this row's shall contains none of them. Its subject is the dial file's HOME and SHAPE, so its observables are the config edit arriving as a governed write through the hook floor (B-01) and the refusal going back out naming the key and both files (B-04). The contrary reading - that declaring a policy dial is itself an authority act - was weighed and REJECTED on the partition it would destroy: the dial's MEANING is SR-139's and that row keeps B-02, so putting B-02 here would give one authority observable two homes, which is the defect this campaign exists to remove. One honest strain is recorded rather than hidden: B-04's `carries` names the hook floor and subagent_gate specifically, while this row's refusals also fire at the dispatcher preflight, intake's adjudication arm and the integrator's verdict gate - session-facing guardrail verdicts of the same class, but not the two the crossing enumerates. If that enumeration is meant as exhaustive, B-04's own text is what needs widening, not this row's attribution.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A dial declared in both homes produces a refusal naming the key and both files, at every guarded entry point (the dispatcher's pre-claim preflight, intake's adjudication arm, the integrator's verdict gate); a dotted key, inline table, multi-line string or hook-read key under the wrong section is refused by the shape check AND still reads ON in both readers; a wrong-typed or out-of-range dial is refused rather than defaulted.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

- **SR-Refs**: SR-137;SR-139
- **Title**: The config layer: one reader, checked shape, one comparison
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_common.py
- **CodeSymbol**: process_config/process_shape_findings/config_conflicts/ratification_level/human_holds
- **Detail**: `process_config` parses `docs/process.toml` once; `declared_policy` reads a dial through it with the legacy file as fallback; `process_shape_findings` refuses every line shape the hooks' sh cannot follow; `config_conflicts` returns refusal STRINGS (never raises — `dispatch.run` sits in the tick loop's caller, where an exception would rewrite an exit-code contract) covering the double-declaration, the wrong type and the out-of-range value; `ratification_level` + `human_holds` are the ordinal and the ONE comparison every consumer makes.
- **Rationale**: Five entry points read policy without passing through `agent_loop.main`, so the refusal has to live where the value is read. The comparison is stated once for the same reason the shape is checked: the failure modes here are silent, and a rule restated at four call sites is four chances to state it differently.
