## SR-140 — Each acceptance is recorded by a copy riding its own approval commit

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-140 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-140
- **Title**: Each acceptance is recorded by a copy riding its own approval commit
- **SN-Refs**: SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-02
- **Requirement**: The kit shall record each acceptance as a byte-identical copy of the accepted
registries riding the same reviewed commit as the approval itself - so the
commit, its date and its author come from git rather than from a cell, and the
transition accepted and the acting reviewer that accepted it are distinguishable
BY QUERY, an approval delegated to automation on a tier the declared ratification
level released from a human one.
- **Rationale**: Attestation STATE is already the row's own Status - derive_gate computes the gate from those cells and reads no other source - so a separate ledger was a second home for a fact the row already carried, and three of its seven columns duplicated cells outright (Decision=ratified|meaning IS Status; superseded IS SupersededBy). What the ledger got right is the BASELINE ITSELF: 'has this been ratified' was answered by walking git for the newest commit where a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - precisely the sanctioned path the staged-amendment guard deliberately ignores, and precisely what the D-9 ladder deletes, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge.
RE-BASED 2026-08-15 (log 2026-08-15d, owner in session) onto the mechanism that was RULED. The on-row anchor this row used to require - a digest of the row's own NORMATIVE cells, written on the row, never in a second registry, and refused when written in the same commit as the text it anchors - is SUPERSEDED AND NOT BUILT; repo-lock D-1's anchor half goes with it. The baseline is instead a whole-file copy of the accepted registries into docs/archive/last_approved/, riding the SAME reviewed commit as the approval (design plans/2026-08-15-baseline-snapshot-design.md §A2/§A3/§F3). Three things follow. First, the commit, date and author the digest design wanted written on-row come FROM the approval commit, so no cell has to carry them and no cell can lie about them. Second, the record is diffable by every git tool and by eye - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit. Third, THE SAME-COMMIT CLAUSE REVERSES SIGN, and the reversal is honest rather than a softening: under the anchor design a stamp written beside its own amendment HID the amendment, so it was an error; under copies the copy MUST ride the approval commit, because riding it is the only thing that makes "this copy is what was blessed" decidable. The anti-laundering property the old clause bought is carried instead by the MIRROR INVARIANT (§F3 - any commit touching the record must leave it byte-identical to its live counterpart, so the only way to write text INTO the record is to write it live first, in a reviewed commit) together with the SEED RULES (§A3 - the first copy refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, so the initial blessing cannot happen by accident, and an absent record is None rather than {} so it can never read as "re-bless everything with no diff shown").
Adversarial round 2 (cross-family, 2026-08-15) is what caught the contradiction: this row still required the abolished design while LLR-173 claimed to implement it. A requirement its own design row contradicts is worse than no requirement, because it reports green.
SPLIT 2026-08-18 (one-decision doctrine R1, the F16/F18 pattern): this row carried THREE shall clauses under a 13v waiver, and the waiver is now SPENT - it covered the one-shall finding only, and the finding gated the traceability step regardless of it, which is the whole reason the waiver was never a resolution. The three clauses fail in different directions and therefore separate cleanly: RECORDING an acceptance (this row), REPORTING text that has moved away from the record (SR-178), and REFUSING a record written from anything but the live text (SR-179). A record that exists and rides its approval commit while nothing ever compares live text against it satisfies this row and not SR-178; a comparison that reports drift faithfully against a record a hand edit wrote satisfies SR-178 and not SR-179. The 13p one-pair concern the waiver argued from is answered rather than overridden: three rows on one (SN-029, B-02) pair is what a need with three separable obligations looks like, and the alternative - one row whose partial pass has no id to report against - is the defect the split rule exists to prevent. Children moved with the obligations: LLR-173/TC-167 stay here (the record), LLR-158/TC-153 to SR-178 (the comparison basis), LLR-178/TC-173 to SR-179 (the mirror invariant).
SN-029's DELEGATED-APPROVAL RECORD is this row's, and stays: "each automated approval on a released tier appends a durable record (row, transition, acting reviewer, commit) distinguishable from a human approval by query - releasing a tier means the review is delegated, never skipped" had ZERO textual occurrence in the SR layer while `orphans=0` held (neither `delegated` nor `acting reviewer` appeared in any of the 64 rows then live). Two of its four fields are supplied by the mechanism rather than by a cell: the ROW is in the copy, and the COMMIT is the approval commit. The transition (the copy's Status against live's) and the acting reviewer (the approval commit's author) are the half that had no home, and they belong HERE rather than on SR-139 because this row is the one that says what an acceptance record contains and where it lives, while SR-139 says which tiers are held. The query-distinguishability is the load-bearing part: a record that cannot separate a machine approval from a human one turns a released tier into an unauditable one, which is the failure the need exists to prevent.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A row claiming approval that no acceptance record records, or whose recorded copy reads at a status making no such claim, is reported rather than passing silently — an approval that never rode a record; once a record exists, a registry absent from inside it is itself a finding, so the vacuous state is exactly one and it is "nothing has been approved yet"; an acceptance recorded with no acting reviewer, or one whose acting reviewer cannot be told apart from a human's by query, is a finding — releasing a tier delegates the review, it does not skip it; the recorded copy is byte-identical to the accepted text and rides the same reviewed commit as the approval, so the commit, its date and its author are answerable from history rather than from any cell; and the FIRST record refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, reachable from no automated step, so the initial blessing cannot happen by accident and can only ride the acceptor's own reviewed signing commit.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-173 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-173
- **SR-Refs**: SR-140
- **Title**: The last_approved snapshot: the record of what was blessed
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/baseline_snapshot.py
- **CodeSymbol**: copy_live/load_all/rows_for/exists/stamp/is_drifted/drifted_cells/unanchored_findings
- **Detail**: The approval RECORD, sibling to LLR-158's comparison basis (which serves SR-178's drift rule) and to LLR-178's mirror invariant (which serves SR-179's refusal): SR-140 says an acceptance is recorded as a byte-identical copy of the accepted registries riding the approval's own reviewed commit - this is where the RECORDED side lives, and the two sibling rows are the readers that make it worth recording. copy_live(root, seed=False) mirrors seven registries (the four spine tiers plus interfaces/external/components, which carry human-only approval and state cells) BYTE FOR BYTE into docs/archive/last_approved/, preserving repo-relative paths so spine_carrier.resolve/carriers/stem resolve under the snapshot root verbatim, carrier fallback included; the stale other-carrier file for a stem is deleted in the same act, or the next read raises "exists under BOTH carriers". Whole files rather than extracted rows is what makes "snapshot file == live file at the copy commit" DECIDABLE - LLR-178 carries that mirror invariant, which fails a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live on one comparison - and it is also what makes the UNANCHORED rule decidable, because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell, so a live row claiming approval whose snapshot copy reads below it is an approval that never rode a copy. Three refusals are load-bearing and each is the opposite of a convenient degrade: copy_live REFUSES to CREATE the directory without seed=True, because the first snapshot blesses whatever text is in the tree and must ride the owner's reviewed signing commit (reachable only from `intake.py snapshot --seed`); load_all returns None for an absent directory and RAISES on a file that exists and will not parse, because {} and None are opposite claims and the empty one reads as "re-bless everything with no diff shown"; and rows_for is the ONE place the None sentinel is collapsed, so no caller invents its own `or {}`. is_drifted/drifted_cells delegate the cell comparison to LLR-158's split_changed_cells and arm on the RATIFIED half only. stamp() is advisory and derived FROM GIT rather than from a file, so it can never quietly become the ledger this mechanism replaced; every arm degrades to ("", ""). SR-140's two remaining record fields come from the same two places and neither is a cell: the TRANSITION is the copy's Status read against live's (the comparison above), and the ACTING REVIEWER is the AUTHOR of the commit that wrote the copy - which is why the copy must ride the approval commit and why the author is queried from git rather than stamped, since a stamped reviewer is a claim and a commit author is a record. ADVISORY TODAY: unanchored_findings returns strings and joins no failure set - it is promoted to the integrity floor at migration step 7, and not before, because against a pre-seed or pre-rename snapshot it would red every row.
- **Rationale**: The baseline had to leave git history. The derivation it replaces walked the registry for the newest commit at which a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - and the D-9 ladder deletes the flip, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION, on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. A snapshot on disk is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file, which is the one thing a walk over the live file can never be. docs/archive/ is the placement rather than an accident: check_vocab.EXEMPT_GLOBS already exempts it, and the snapshot legitimately holds the PREVIOUS vocabulary, so anywhere else reds the vocabulary enforcer on every signing. The design's own argument for whole-file copies is that a human can open the record and `git diff` it - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit.
- **TestRefs**: TC-167
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-167 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-167
- **Verifies**: SR-140;LLR-173;IF-123;IF-128;IF-129
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the record half of the baseline-snapshot suite over real temp repos - the half TC-153 does not cover, which is drift. (a) VACUITY: with no snapshot directory every reader is vacuous by ABSENCE, load_all returns None rather than {}, and the mechanical flip is a no-op before the first signing. (b) THE BOOTSTRAP GUARD: copy_live REFUSES to create the directory without seed=True, and --seed is proven UNREACHABLE from every loop module, hook and check step, so the first snapshot can only ride the owner's signing commit. (c) THE COPY CONTRACT: the copy is byte-for-byte and preserves repo-relative paths, and a stale other-carrier file for the same stem is DELETED in the same act (or the next resolve raises "exists under BOTH carriers"). (d) UNANCHORED, BOTH DIRECTIONS: an approved row absent from the snapshot is reported, an approval whose snapshot copy reads BELOW approval is reported (the case that is only decidable because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell), a registry missing from an EXISTING snapshot is reported, and an unparseable snapshot RAISES rather than reading as empty. (e) The snapshot-directory constant has ONE value in both homes, and the `intake.py snapshot` CLI seeds then refreshes. The MIRROR INVARIANT half moved to TC-173 with the SR-140 split (2026-08-18) rather than being covered twice.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-140 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-173 detail contract, and covers the IF-123 provider seam with the two seams this module itself consumes (IF-128 the carrier, IF-129 the comparison basis). The three CONSUMPTION rows IF-124/125/126 are the consumers' own to cover; IF-124 rides TC-161, and IF-125/126 are uncited today
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-144 — Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-144 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-144
- **Title**: Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, and refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first.
- **Rationale**: Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence merged rejected code onto trunk on 2026-08-03: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable. Hat-derived (hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): a claim a dead lane never releases is C-UNA-3's deadlocked-until-morning case, and the immutable per-close record is C-UNA-5's answer to a failure that pages nobody — a close no one can read is externally indistinguishable from a run that finished. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A partial close is clean under the real validator (the terminal Deliverable rule is exempt, because an early close leaves the spec's definition byte-identical by design); a terminal spec with no report is LOUD rather than silently un-disposed; a second close is refused and leaves no residue in the lane; a multi-spec close that refuses restores every report it wrote.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-144 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-144
- **SR-Refs**: SR-144
- **Title**: Terminal lane outcomes (handback + quarantine)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
- **CodeSymbol**: close_partial/quarantine/report_path/render_report/_restore/diff_records
- **Detail**: close_partial: commits the lane's work as-is, moves each claimed spec to the TERMINAL partial/ (nothing re-claims it, so nothing strands) and writes ONE IMMUTABLE per-close report under docs/handbacks/ naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split. The report is the close EVENT's identity — immutable so it never moves, unique so a second close is a second document — which is what dissolved the starvation class five mutable-proxy dedup mechanisms died on. A second close is refused rather than overwriting the first, and any refusal restores every report the call wrote (schedule reads queued+blockref as blocked) and the driver cannot claim and return it forever. quarantine: the ruled red arm - reverts the product paths to the merge base and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch under docs/work/handback/, reading `git diff --name-status -z` as three-field RECORDS so a rename cannot revert the wrong path, checking every git return code, and restoring rather than reporting when a revert step fails. Both leave the branch finished, so integrate.branch_outcomes reads the outcome off the spec's DIRECTORY - complete/=merged, cancelled/=cancelled, any open folder=handback - and every lane ends in a merge.
- **Rationale**: Making the outcome the directory leaves no fourth answer and no state file that could hold one; partial work lands in trunk where a future WI can find it instead of on a branch nobody will look at.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-138)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-161 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-161
- **SR-Refs**: SR-144
- **Title**: The close ritual and the disposition it owes
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
- **CodeSymbol**: close_partial/render_report/report_path/_restore/_split_lines
- **Detail**: `close_partial` writes the report FIRST (it is the event's identity), refuses when one already exists, and restores EVERY report this call wrote on any refusal — so a multi-spec lane cannot half-close. `intake._close_drafts` titles the disposition on the report PATH and nothing else, and says so LOUDLY when a terminal spec has no report rather than minting zero rows quietly. Every partial and cancelled close earns exactly one disposition row — a re-sweep mints none, a genuinely second close is a second report and so a second row, and a disposition row never mints a disposition for itself (refused at both ends); a complete close is spot-checked at the declared rate rather than gated; continuing partial work drafts a successor carrying explicit lineage that survives the id change, never re-opening a terminal row.
- **Rationale**: The path is the only token in the event's identity that cannot move. An earlier cut folded the report's `claimed_outcome` in as well, which re-read a MUTABLE field on every sweep, so one edit minted a second disposition for the same close — the starvation class returning through a new proxy.
- **TestRefs**: TC-156
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-167 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-167
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
- **Detail**: The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
- **Rationale**: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).
- **TestRefs**: TC-161
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

The quarantine arm reverts the product paths and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch, reverts a RENAME correctly (the --name-status -z stream read as records, not pairs - mutation-proven), leaves the handback bookkeeping alone, refuses when the red is not the lane's own code, and restores rather than reports when a revert step fails.

## SR-146 — Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-146 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-146
- **Title**: Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail
- **SN-Refs**: SN-005
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Every prompt the delivered loop launches shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.
- **Rationale**: Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.) Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-5 requires that content composed for dispatch to an external model runner carry a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — a prompt assembled inside source is an unreviewed egress path no write-side gate covers, and shipping it as a reviewable file with a digest is what makes that rule readable. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: An unknown or unfilled slot is a refusal; a missing shipped template is a named preflight refusal rather than a first-session crash; the catalogue lists every shipped prompt with its digest and `--check` fails when stale; a line-ending difference is not a digest change; an operator override is named in the session record rather than only in the launch flags.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-162 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-162
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: Prompts as loaded files, catalogued and fingerprinted
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/prompts.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load/fill/strict_check/digest/catalog_rows/preflight
- **Detail**: `load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
- **Rationale**: Normalizing before hashing is not cosmetic: the same template checked out CRLF on Windows and LF on POSIX is the same prompt, and a telemetry field that said otherwise would report a model change on every clone.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-163 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-163
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: Argv arrays, and the adjudicator as a routed phase
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
- **CodeSymbol**: split_cmd/build_argv
- **Detail**: `split_cmd` recognises a JSON array ONLY when the value parses as a list, so no existing template can change meaning; a non-string token raises rather than being stringified. The prompt-transport decision reads the resulting TOKENS, so it is identical for both forms. Alongside it, `ADJUDICATE` joins `agent_route.WEIGHT_PHASES` and `agent_loop.DEFAULT_PHASE_TIER` with its own `route_intent` arm: cross-family like the reviewers, tier pinned by the row's measured estimate, and deliberately deaf to the implementer's escalation overrides.
- **Rationale**: A silent coercion in a registry is a launch that does not match its declaration. And an adjudicator inheriting the implementer's escalation would silently re-tier a judgement because a different row's build went badly.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-164 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-164
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: The generated prompt catalogue and its freshness gate
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_prompt_catalog.py
- **CodeSymbol**: render/main
- **Detail**: `render` is PURE — it takes no arguments and reads only the shipped templates — so `--check` and the write path cannot disagree about what "current" means. `--check` compares on NORMALIZED line endings, for the same reason the digest is taken that way: a CRLF checkout is not a stale catalogue. The file is declared in `docs/stack.ini [generated]` as kind `promptcatalog`, so the trunk lane owns it and a work branch never commits it.
- **Rationale**: A generated artifact nobody re-generates is a document that lies, and a lying catalogue is worse than none: it invites the operator to conclude the wrong thing about the session they are debugging. Same contract `gen_arch_map --check` and `gen_skills_index --check` hold.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-147 — One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-147 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-147
- **Title**: One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall hold every spine tier in one machine-parseable representation, reached by a migration proven cell-for-cell over the live registries before the new representation becomes authoritative.
- **Rationale**: RE-VOICED 2026-08-17 (Sol F16, applied): the shall carried the migration HISTORY - naming the two prior carriers and the 2026-08-10 cutover - plus a second obligation, which is normative history and a merged decision at the requirement tier. The history lives here now, where a rationale may hold it, and the row states one obligation. The recorded 13v waiver covered the one-shall finding ONLY and is SPENT: the row no longer carries a second shall and trace.py no longer reports it. The two-carrier split has no recorded rationale and costs on both sides. Reading 32 need rows takes ~166 code lines across 14 functions in 8 modules - six bespoke scanners, two of them F5 twins pinned by nothing but a docstring, which have already drifted once and rendered a phantom root in the dashboard - while reading all 436 SR+LLR+TC rows takes csv.DictReader. CSV also cannot represent the cells that actually exist: five hold a literal pipe, 552 hold commas, the longest is 1,553 characters, and an embedded newline has no representation at all. A structured carrier turns three integrity rules into properties of the parse - a duplicate id becomes a decode error, a ref list becomes a typed array (retiring the split-on-whitespace rule that read `and` as an orphan), and an empty cell becomes an absent key, so `unset` and `set to empty` stop being the same value. The converter is the migration's evidence, not its convenience: SR-129's 140-cell lesson is that an unproven representation change is exactly where a registry silently loses cells. One-shall waiver (13v): the single-carrier claim and its converter-proof are one contract — the converter is the migration's evidence — and the converter's mechanics already decompose to this row's LLR chain; splitting would separate a claim from the proof that makes it checkable. Hat-derived (hat.TEST-ENGINEER): the single-carrier claim is checkable only because the converter proves it cell-for-cell BEFORE the authority flip — the charter refuses an obligation whose enforcer has never been shown to bite, and a carrier change is precisely where a silent loss hides. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A migration check over the live registries exits 0 reporting the round-trip clean for every row of every tier; a cell that is dropped, truncated, re-typed or re-ordered is REFUSED with exit 1 and a finding naming the registry, the row id and the field; and the emitted text re-parses under the declared format's standard parser.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-165 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-165
- **SR-Refs**: SR-147
- **Title**: The spine carrier converter (markdown + CSV -> TOML)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/migrate_carrier.py
- **CodeSymbol**: convert/rows_to_toml/sn_to_toml/compare/toml_scalar
- **Detail**: convert() walks the four tiers and returns (findings, written_paths), so the caller decides whether a lossy conversion writes. rows_to_toml emits `[<table>.<ID>]` with the id PREFIX RETAINED and BARE - TOML allows `-` in a bare key - because ~6,400 hand-authored citations use the prefixed token and a numeric key buys no type change (TOML keys are always strings, and `001` and `1` are two legal DIFFERENT keys, which is a near-miss the prefixed form does not have). cell_to_value types by COLUMN, not by guessing: REF_COLS become arrays, INT_COLS become ints, an empty cell becomes an absent key, and everything else stays a string - this is a carrier change, not a schema change. toml_scalar emits multi-line basic strings for anything long or newline-bearing (tomllib is read-only by design, so the kit emits its own, as it already does in wi_convert.toml_string and bootstrap.set_process_key). sn_to_toml keeps the edge-case tier's own lifecycle/scenario/expected fields as THEMSELVES and writes kind= from the heading, retiring section-as-state. compare() re-reads what was emitted and diffs it against an {id: {key: text}} projection built from either carrier, so ONE comparison serves both instead of a second copy that drifts.
- **Rationale**: The round-trip check is the requirement, not a convenience - and a check that cannot fail would be the false green SN-008 forbids, which is why the loss detector is driven against real corruption classes rather than only against a clean run. Ref cells compare on the TOKEN LIST because the conversion re-joins them with the canonical separator and the original spacing was never part of the meaning; comparing raw text there would report a loss that is not one. The edge-case fold that traj_parse._sn_fields performs is a PRESENTATION rule the markdown table forced, so it stays in the exporters - baking it into the carrier would make the exporter's reading the only reading there is.
- **TestRefs**: TC-159
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-166 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-166
- **SR-Refs**: SR-147
- **Title**: The spine carrier's one reader, and the dual-home refusal
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/spine_carrier.py
- **CodeSymbol**: SPINE_TABLE/SPINE_COLUMN/rows_from_toml/rows_from_text/resolve/load
- **Detail**: PURE like trace_text (text in, rows out; no git, no argv) except resolve()'s existence check, because the dual-home refusal is the one rule that must not be duplicated. rows_from_toml presents rows under TODAY'S COLUMN NAMES via SPINE_COLUMN - the exact inverse of migrate_carrier.KEY - so the carrier change is data plus a loader rather than a sixteen-module rename; SPINE_TABLE is keyed on the ID COLUMN, not on a path, because a path carries a carrier suffix and therefore moves. An absent key stays absent rather than becoming "", and a decode error returns None rather than {}. resolve() RAISES when both carriers exist rather than picking by precedence, and load() raises on a carrier that exists and will not parse. carriers() returns BOTH paths so an applicability test spans the cutover.
- **Rationale**: None-vs-{} and absent-vs-empty are the same defect twice: {} says the registry had no rows, which for a baseline read means re-bless everything with no diff and for a gate means nothing to check, so a carrier that cannot be read must be ABSENT rather than empty. The dual-home REFUSAL follows the rule bootstrap --migrate-config already applies to the policy dials: a precedence rule lets a half-finished migration keep working while quietly reading the stale half, so the conversion is never finished and nobody finds out. ONE HOME for the vocabulary is an owner ruling (2026-08-10, repo-lock D-6) amending F5: duplicating a vocabulary fails silently - the copy that has not learned a column returns a row missing that cell, which every consumer reads as "the cell is empty".
- **TestRefs**: TC-160
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-159 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-159
- **Verifies**: SR-147;LLR-165
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the carrier-converter suite: it converts the live spine, re-parses the emitted TOML with tomllib and asserts the round-trip is cell-for-cell identical to the source for every row of every tier, then DRIVES the loss detector against corruption classes (a dropped row, a dropped field, a truncated value, a re-typed value, a mangled ref list) and asserts each is REFUSED with a finding naming the row and field, so the clean result is evidence rather than a check that cannot fail.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-147 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_migrate_carrier.py
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-160 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-160
- **Verifies**: SR-147;LLR-166
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Drive the carrier reader over a REAL repo whose spine is cut over from CSV to TOML in one commit, and assert the four properties that fail open if wrong: the pre-migration revision still reads through the carrier it used then; the post-migration revision reads through the new one presenting the same column names; a lossless cutover is SILENT to the amendment guard while text smuggled into that same commit is NAMED with its row and cell; and a carrier that does not parse - including the duplicate id the carrier turns into a decode error - is reported absent rather than empty. Then pin the vocabulary against drift: the reader is the exact inverse of the writer's KEY, and every column of every LIVE spine header is driven through the pair so the agreement cannot be vacuous.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-147 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_trajectory_staged.py::test_baseline_read_survives_the_carrier_cutover
- **Status**: Approved
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-148 — Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-148 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-148
- **Title**: Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order
- **SN-Refs**: SN-025;SN-029;SN-027;SN-006;SN-008;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall select the work an unattended run does next from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone — shipping no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and deriving from no prose surface and no predefined track — in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, human holds applied only from the declared ratification level, nothing admitted past a human-held stop, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.
- **Rationale**: Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. Absorbs SR-141 (census F5, ruled overlap): both rows stated where adjudication ranks at admission and the acceptance texts had already diverged in nuance — one SR per (SN-025, loop selection order) per 13p; SR-141's stable-partition mechanism lands as an LLR under this row, and its id retires under the D-1/D-4 doctrine with the forwarding record in the log. ABSORBS TWO MORE, 2026-08-15, on the same doctrine and the same invariant: the top-down read found SN-025's loop work-selection stated THREE times — this row, SR-153 ("Ready work derives from tracked state") and SR-059 ("Retire next-wi; generated status surface") — with the ordering claim made twice and "no hand-maintained pointer" three times, against 13p's one-SR-per-(need, property) rule. SR-153 contributed the derivation source (tracked registries and git history alone), the determinism of the eligible set and its order, and the per-item fail-closed rule for missing, undeclared or contradictory declared inputs; SR-059 contributed the no-pointer-surface obligation in its strong form (no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links one) and the generated, freshness-gated status surface. Both ids retire; `sn_refs` unions to SN-025 + SN-029 + SN-027 + SN-006 + SN-008 + SN-010 so no need loses its carrier, and every shed clause is token-checked into this row's requirement or acceptance with the map recorded in the log. The alternative — an explicit three-way partition — was rejected for the reason census F5 gave when the same question was asked of SR-141: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. RESTORED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F5): SR-059 required the deletion of the two pointer files in BOTH directions — "fresh scaffold AND migrated repo" — and the merge carried the fresh-scaffold half only, so an adopter upgrade could retain the retired authority files while this row still passed. The migrated-repo/resync half is back in the acceptance; it is the half that matters most, because a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep. Fan-out re-stamp: this row states one decision — what an unattended run's next work derives from and in what order — and its children are the successive stages of that one selection, not separable contracts; the width is the deliberate consolidation recorded above, which folded three rows stating the same invariant into one, so re-splitting to satisfy the bound would restore the duplication that merge was convened to remove.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A ready adjudication row admits ahead of every other class, the ruled rank table unchanged (--explain still reports it) and non-adjudication rows keeping their relative order; an unresolved handback is selected before spine or implementation work, and processing it either resolves the record or mints a provenance-linked successor exactly once; with no unresolved handback, only the earliest incomplete spine tier is eligible; implementation work is ineligible while any required test case remains unlaid; at human_ratification_through = 0 no spine tier produces a human hold, levels 1 through 4 hold exactly their documented cumulative tiers, and a human-held attestation still drains and surfaces with the adjudication row waiting behind the stop; every selection records the chosen class, source identifier and hold decision. Two resumes over the same tracked state select the same eligible set in the same order; an item with missing or contradictory declared inputs is excluded with a stated reason while disjoint classified work stays eligible; no prose surface participates in the derivation; the ordering and exclusion reasons are exposed for replay. A fresh scaffold ships no next-wi/run-phase pointer and is parallel-by-default; no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links such a pointer; a build-out lane's phase resolves from its branch name with no pointer file; an upgraded or resynced repository that once carried such a pointer carries neither pointer file after the migration and no live dependency on either survives, so the deletion holds for a migrated repository and not only for a fresh scaffold; the status snapshot regenerates mechanically and its freshness is gated.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: unattended-loop

### LLR LLR-149 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-149
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: §A8 admission: the kind x ratification-level table + the spine barrier
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _kind_action/_admission/_lane_count/gap_census
- **Detail**: The dispatcher's one scheduling decision (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4/§A8), consuming schedule.kind_of - the §A1 classification's enforcement point. _kind_action is the ruled §A8 table verbatim per kind x human-held answer (`agent_common.human_holds`), and a human-held attestation/gate row SURFACES even under keep_nondependent — that dial answers whether OTHER lanes keep running, never whether the machine may ratify; _admission is the barrier as a pure decision: any exclusive-kind frontier row stops new admission (wait) until every lane is back, then ALL spine rows admit together as one exclusive batch (one branch, one agent_loop --wi 'A;B' worker, one re-attest window), attestation/gate rows surface under attended (drain, exit 0 naming the open-items cards) and admit as the queued batch at single-ratify's close; _lane_count resolves the lanes dial CLI > AGENT_LANES > stack.ini [agent-loop] > 1 (absent key = 1; the template seeds 2); gap_census is the empty-frontier ladder's rung-1 seam - unverified in-scope SRs, orphan rows and draft SNs from trace.analyze, handed to the intake mint helper (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2), never minted in the lane.
- **Rationale**: The §A4.1 authority flip: admission replaced the claim rung's deleted safety_class refusal, so the policy must live at ONE decision point and be driven as a pure function.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-143)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-152 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-152
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudication kind: rank-1 slot + no-bar arm + the bar strictness key
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/schedule.py
- **CodeSymbol**: SAFETY_CLASSES/_KIND_CONCURRENCY/_KIND_RANK
- **Detail**: The `adjudication` kind (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2) joins the declared SafetyClass vocabulary: exclusive on the concurrency axis, rank 1 on the order axis - the slot §A1 reserved, filled without renumbering the ruled table. The kind's lane runs NO product bar (integrate._lane_bar_directives + _refresh_bar: the refresh still merges trunk in, runs the trunk step and commits a verified Bar-Green attestation whose summary reads no-bar honestly) because its outputs are Status cells and the work registry, nothing a product bar can speak to; a mixed batch or unreadable frontmatter fails TOWARD the bar. The optional `bar = DevStg-Reqs|DevStg-Tests|DevStg-Impl` frontmatter key rides the same directive read: bar declares verification strictness for this row's lane; it never affects scheduling (load_wis deliberately does not parse it) - the refresh passes the batch's strictest value to check.py --gate and refuses a malformed one loudly.
- **Rationale**: Narrowing the amendment detector to ratified cells without the kind would move mis-fires from spurious window to MISSED window; the two are a pair (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-146)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-159 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-159
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: Admission order and the red-TC census
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _judgement_first/_admission/red_tc_census/parse_red_tc/_implemented_ids
- **Detail**: `_judgement_first` is a STABLE PARTITION over the frontier, applied inside `_admission` before the surface/dispatch split so it can never move work past a human-held stop. `red_tc_census` rides the existing finding-string seam with a distinct prefix; `_red_tc_line` and `parse_red_tc` sit adjacent because the grammar is a contract between two modules, and `intake` routes on the parser rather than re-splitting prose. red_tc_census names, as its own class, a test case that is not green while every requirement it verifies is cited by a work item recorded done — Approved, Drafted and Modified test cases are exempt, which under the narrowed enum is the whole closed vocabulary, so the census is structurally dead in a conformant repo (retire-or-re-arm is the owner's recorded judgement item, 2026-08-15 sitting sweep); an open, partial or cancelled citer makes nothing red, and a test case verifying a mix of claimed and unclaimed targets is not red; each named line routes to an adjudication row whose suggested tier is estimated from measured breadth, never defaulted; the census line's grammar has exactly one reader (parse_red_tc).
- **Rationale**: The alternative to the partition — renumbering `schedule._KIND_RANK` — would change ordering for every downstream repo for a rung that belongs to admission. The alternative to the parser is prose carrying control flow, which is the exact defect the typed `suggested_tier` field replaced.
- **TestRefs**: TC-154
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-167 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-167
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
- **Detail**: The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
- **Rationale**: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).
- **TestRefs**: TC-161
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-149 — Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-149 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-149
- **Title**: Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces
- **SN-Refs**: SN-004;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The harness shall report every occurrence of a retired process tag in a live authored surface — naming the file and line and the vocabulary that replaces it, at a severity that warns by default and fails under --strict — reporting none in a declared historical, generated or attestation-quoting surface, nor in a line or file marked as a declaration site.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-004 (the ladder's vocabulary is the one the project is held to) and SN-010 (docs stay honest). OI-21 made this check a CONDITION of the vocabulary conversion rather than a follow-up, on measured evidence: an earlier sweep removed a retired-tag construct from this repo's own open-items registry and it REGENERATED within days, caught only by an audit. A ~2,500-edit conversion held in place by attention is a conversion that comes undone. The carve-outs are as load-bearing as the rule: history is not rewritten and an attestation is never re-worded, because a swept sign-off makes a signed record claim something was signed that was not. Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER): C-MNT-3 gives every declared vocabulary value exactly one normative definition in one home; a retired tag surviving in a live authored surface is a second, contradictory definition of the same word, which is why the earlier sweep regenerated within days when only attention held it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A planted retired tag in a live authored file is reported with its path and line, at exit 0 under the default severity and exit 1 under --strict; a file under any declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, attestation briefs, closed and cancelled work-item specs, generated surfaces) reports nothing, including at arbitrary depth; a line carrying the allow marker or a file carrying the allow-file marker reports nothing; and the word "gate" used to mean a check that can fail reports nothing.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-169 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-169
- **SR-Refs**: SR-149
- **Title**: Retired-vocabulary enforcer
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_vocab.py
- **CodeSymbol**: scan
- **Detail**: Walks the tree, classifies each file through in_scope (declared carve-out globs for historical, generated and attestation-quoting surfaces, plus a text-suffix filter), and reports one finding per offending LINE - not per tag, since a line naming three tags is one sentence to rewrite. Matching is TAG-SCOPED (a whole-word alternation over the retired G tags), never the word 'gate', which survives wherever it means a check that can fail. Declaration sites opt out through an explicit in-file marker rather than a heuristic, because a heuristic that can be satisfied by accident is how the vocabulary grows back through the enforcer meant to stop it. Wired into check.py at EVERY bar with --strict added from DevStg-Tests on, mirroring check_trajectory's severity ladder: the surfaces the tags regrow into are authored hardest at the lowest bar.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-163)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-163 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-163
- **Verifies**: SR-149;LLR-169
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Plant a retired tag in a live authored file and one in each declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, an attestation brief, a closed work-item spec, a generated surface, the owner scratchpad), plus one at depth under a carved-out directory; run check_vocab.py at both severities; plant a line marker and a file marker; plant the word 'gate' in its surviving sense; and run the checker over the real meta-repo tree.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: The live file is reported with its path and line; the default severity exits 0 and --strict exits 1 on the same finding; no carve-out is ever reported, at any depth; a marked line is skipped while its unmarked neighbour is not, and a marked file is skipped whole; the surviving sense of 'gate' is clean; and the meta-repo itself is clean at the ERROR severity, so the assertions cannot pass against fixtures while the conversion rots.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_stage_ladder.py
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-150 — Stakeholder-need cells stay in stakeholder language

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-150 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-150
- **Title**: Stakeholder-need cells stay in stakeholder language
- **SN-Refs**: SN-033
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The harness shall report each stakeholder-need `need` cell that contains an internal path, an implementation-only identifier or a process citation, naming the row and the offending phrase — at a severity that warns by default and fails only under --strict, exempting a name declared on a reviewed exception list that ships empty (a name that is itself a user-facing interface belongs in a need), and reporting nothing for acceptance or engineering-requirement cells, which SN-033 exempts by its own text.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-033 — its ratified acceptance commissions exactly this check, and until this row it did not exist. Landed while the need tier was measured clean (0 of 27 cells; decision 7 rider 2, ruled 2026-08-13u), deliberately AHEAD of the SR re-tier's churn: locking the clean state in costs one warn-first step now; trusting a large re-statement pass not to dirty it costs an audit later. Warn-first is the ruled posture, not a softness — a form heuristic over ratified stakeholder prose must not gate without an owner ruling, and that ruling has not been made.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A need cell planted with an internal path, an implementation-only identifier and a process citation is reported, naming the row and each offending phrase separately rather than the row alone; the report leaves the run passing by default and failing under the declared strict mode, which is the whole of the severity claim; a token on the reviewed exception list is not reported, and that list ships empty; the same three tokens planted in an acceptance or engineering-requirement cell are not reported, because the need governs the need cell only; and the live need registry reports zero findings.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-170 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-170
- **SR-Refs**: SR-150
- **Title**: Need-form checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_need_form.py
- **CodeSymbol**: scan/need_findings/load_allow
- **Detail**: Reads the needs registry through spine_carrier.load_needs (either carrier answers; an unreadable registry REFUSES loudly rather than scanning as empty), and a registry that is PRESENT but yields zero scannable need cells - an empty file, or real rows with no need field - is reported VACUOUS rather than clean, while an absent registry stays the pre-scaffold clean skip and a -000-only scaffold registry stays a blank form (REVIEW-A round 1 drove the hole: at DevStg-Reqs an emptied registry hard-failed nothing else in the harness). The three token classes are data (CLASSES), labelled with SN-033's own vocabulary so a finding names the class the acceptance names. Two scope decisions are deliberate and documented in the docstring: SN-### is NOT a process citation (the SN registry is the stakeholder's own document, so a need pointing at a sibling need keeps the reader at the stakeholder tier - the live SN-025 case), and a single-slash, dot-free token is a path only when it RESOLVES in the scanned tree (docs/archive names a real directory and reports; English either/or pairs like subjective/perceptual resolve to nothing and stay exempt - review round 1 narrowed the original blanket exemption). URL spans - scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms alike - are suppressed whole, so a path-shaped tail inside an external address never reports under any class; the span stops at prose delimiters (comma/semicolon) and the www. form requires a multi-label host, so a genuine token abutting a URL and a local www.assets/logo.png both still report (round 3); a sentence-final token's trailing full stop is stripped before the phrase is judged or reported, so its reviewed exception matches (round 2). One finding per token: a later class re-matching inside an already-reported span is the same phrase to rewrite. The exception list (docs/need-form-allow) requires the ' — <reason>' separator per entry, the literal em-dash with spaces (ALLOW_SEP) - a line authored with a plain hyphen, or with no separator at all, declares nothing, so a malformed entry can only ever fail LOUD, never silence a finding. Wired as check.py's need-form step at every bar with NO --strict promotion, deliberately unlike check_vocab's severity ladder: promotion is an owner ruling, and the wiring says so.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-164)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-164 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-164
- **Verifies**: SR-150;LLR-170
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Construct a needs registry whose need cell plants one token of each declared class (an internal path, an underscore identifier, a registry id and a section mark); run check_need_form.py at both severities; plant a user-facing interface name with and without its reviewed exception-list entry, and with a malformed entry missing the reason separator; plant the same dirty tokens in why/acceptance cells only; plant an English either/or pair, a sibling SN-### citation and a -000 example row; plant a one-level dot-free token both with and without a resolving target in the scanned tree; plant a URL whose tail is path-shaped, in scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms, one sharing its cell with a genuine internal path, one abutting a second path through a comma/semicolon, and a single-label www.-prefixed local path; end a cell with a path token and with an English either/or pair at the full stop; empty the registry file, then strip the need fields from real rows, then leave only a -000 example row; run over a tree with no needs registry at all; and run over the real meta-repo tree.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: The dirty cell reports the row id AND each offending phrase, one finding per token (the path's inner filename is not a second finding), exit 0 by default and 1 under --strict; the exception-list entry silences exactly its token while the malformed entry silences nothing; exempt cells, either/or pairs, SN cross-references and example rows report nothing; a one-level dot-free token reports as a path only when it resolves on disk, and a URL's path-shaped tail never reports in either form while its cell-mate genuine path, a delimiter-abutting neighbour token and a single-label www.-prefixed local path all still do; a sentence-final path is named without its trailing full stop — so its reviewed exception matches — and a sentence-final either/or pair stays exempt; a registry that is present but yields zero scannable need cells reports as vacuous (strict exit 1) while an absent registry is a clean skip and a -000-only registry stays clean; and the live registry is clean at zero findings, so the assertions cannot pass against fixtures while the tier dirties.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_check_need_form.py
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-151 — Hosted CI runs the declared bar per trigger

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-151 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-151
- **Title**: Hosted CI runs the declared bar per trigger
- **SN-Refs**: SN-005;SN-004
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The shipped reference CI workflow shall invoke, for each declared hosted trigger (push, pull request, schedule) across its declared OS x Python matrix, the same documented harness entry point at the tier the declared moment-to-tier table assigns to that moment.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-005 — one definition of passing per moment, its acceptance's own words — and SN-004. The obligation existed but had no row of its own: it rode SR-019's acceptance cell (census finding F1). Attributed to B-05, the delivered package, on the DESIGN-CONTROL reading (owner ruling 2026-08-16): what this system delivers is a METHOD for a CI runner to invoke, and it holds no design authority over whether an external runner honours the configuration it is handed — so the obligation this row can actually carry is a property of the shipped workflow file, not of a hosted trigger arriving. The frame already reasons this way twice: REL-002 records that the generators ship as B-05 content while the workflow running them is adopted, and REL-003 lands the provider-surface obligation on "delivered loop content (B-05), exercised session-side" rather than minting a crossing for EXT-005. Stated artifact-shaped per §1R.6: the job files are a standing thing whose declarations are pinned by test. An adopter-edited workflow copy is outside this claim, exactly as SN-005 narrows it. Current carriers, recorded here while the row is undecomposed (no design or test row yet exists to hold the binding): the moment-to-tier table is docs/stack.ini [ci-tiers]; the pinning test is tests/test_ci_tier_declaration.py.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The shipped workflow's trigger set and per-trigger tier match the declared moment-to-tier table, pinned by test; a workflow trigger absent from the table, or one invoking anything other than the documented harness entry point, is a finding; the declared OS x Python matrix cells appear in the shipped workflow; SR-019's acceptance cross-references this row rather than restating it.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-152 — Hosted CI verdict is the harness's own

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-152 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-152
- **Title**: Hosted CI verdict is the harness's own
- **SN-Refs**: SN-008;SN-005
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The hosted CI job verdict shall carry the harness's own exit for the declared bar — the job failing when the harness exits nonzero, with a step log naming each step's outcome and no silently skipped required step — as the declared backstop re-run behind the bypassable local hook floor.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a green) and SN-005. This is the second half of the honest-limit pair SR-019 states from the local side: a local floor is bypassable (git commit --no-verify), so no-unchecked-write-enters-governed-state rests on the verdict at the moment of the act PLUS a re-run of the same bar away from that bypass. Attributed to B-05, the delivered package, on the DESIGN-CONTROL reading (owner ruling 2026-08-16, applied to SR-151 first and to this row on the same argument): the shall constrains what the SHIPPED WORKFLOW declares — no continue-on-error, no conditional skip, the harness's own exit as the job's — and the system holds no authority over an external runner honouring it. STATED SO IT IS NOT READ AS COVERAGE: the re-run therefore has no crossing of its own in this frame. That is the ruling's deliberate consequence, not an oversight — a hosted runner is an ADOPTER's boundary, and the frame already handles an uncontrollable external the same way at REL-003, where the provider-surface obligation lands on delivered loop content rather than minting a crossing for EXT-005. SR-114 keeps the portability obligation; the CI matrix as an evidence surface is this row's.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The shipped workflow contains no continue-on-error or conditional skip around a required harness step; a nonzero harness exit fails the job; the step log names the harness invocation and its tier. This row is the backstop half of the pair SR-019 states from the local side.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-154 — Independent review routed across families

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-154 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-154
- **Title**: Independent review routed across families
- **SN-Refs**: SN-026;SN-024;SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: When unattended work reaches integration, the delivered loop content shall obtain each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared ratification level.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on — had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A declared review policy of N schedules N independent reviewer sessions; the declared (family x model x tier) pair-rows of the delivered agent registry are the only candidate source, and the declared consent surface's presence is what turns managed selection on — absent, managed selection is off and no row is resolved; present, exactly one row resolves per in-process phase and tier; a configured cross-family draw is preferred and its unavailability degrades to the documented mode, never a silent skip; every selection is logged before launch, never a silent model swap; rework iterates within the declared budget and pages per the declared ratification level on exhaustion.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: unattended-loop

## SR-155 — Contested planning rounds for marked work

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-155 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-155
- **Title**: Contested planning rounds for marked work
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-006;SN-026;SN-002
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Where work is declared as contested planning, the delivered loop content shall produce the selected decomposition through a bounded round of two independently authored rival plans — cross-critiqued, mechanically coverage-compared and arbitrated by fresh sessions — committing the selected outcome atomically as registry-valid queued rows and surfacing a non-converging round through the declared ratification level, never falling back to a single uncontested plan silently.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 (a decomposition judged by a competing plan and an independent arbiter rather than its author), with SN-026 (the planner pair prefers distinct families), SN-002 (the selected outcome lands as registry-valid rows, not prose) and SN-006. One row because the dual-plan round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class (SELECT or PAGE); its state machine, briefs, adapters and isolation rules are the component tier's. Fan-out re-stamp: the round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class, and its children are the successive phases of that single round — planner pair, briefs, coverage comparison, arbitration, isolation, disposition — none of which an adopter can obtain, refuse, or observe independently of the round, so the child count measures the round's step count rather than a merge of contracts.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A row marked for contested planning is refused on the direct build path; the round runs planner pair, cross-critique, mechanical coverage comparison and position-swapped arbitration as fresh sessions within declared budgets; SELECT files children that pass the registry validator atomically; PAGE preserves the round's evidence and maps through the declared ratification level.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-156 — Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landing

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-156 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-156
- **Title**: Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landing
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027;SN-008;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall run ready work on bounded, mutually isolated lanes and narrow every lane back to the integration branch through one serial, fail-closed seam that runs the declared bar on the composed tree — parking a red candidate and stopping the queue loudly, draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and reconstructing claim and queue state from version-control history alone at any crash boundary, never a half-integrated authoritative state.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-027 WHOLE — ready work fans out across bounded parallel lanes and that fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed) and SN-025 (recovery from version-control history alone). SPLIT 2026-08-16 (re-tier v2 S4, ruling R1): the row's former "one row because serial integration is one delivered contract" argument was sound for the SEAM, but three children were not the seam — lane execution, claim authority, and the trunk-side mint of work-item identity had ridden in under it. The measured corroboration: no SR in the corpus stated SN-027's FIRST half at all (a grep of every requirement cell for bounded lanes, isolated working trees or a worker ceiling returned nothing), so the merge was hiding a coverage hole rather than expressing one contract — exactly what the fan-out detector exists to surface. This row therefore widens to the honest lane LIFECYCLE (claim, run isolated, close, land) and closes that hole in the same act; the trunk-only authority contract sheds to SR-170. The two fail independently: a perfectly serialized landing that lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies this row and not that one. OVERREACH RETRACTED 2026-08-16 (S6 second top-down read, finding M4): the widening had also taken "closing a lane that cannot finish into a terminal state", which is SR-144's shall verbatim in substance ("close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch") under the same SN-027 — two rows for one observable, against 13p, and the sign of it was LLR-144 sitting here while SR-144 stated its contract. The clause and its handback.py carrier are struck; what remains is this row's own half, that a terminal branch still lands through the one serial seam. LLR-144 re-parents to SR-144 with TC-138.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A launch with independent ready work runs up to the configured lane ceiling concurrently in isolated working trees, and a single-lane setting preserves the serial semantic; a finished branch reaches the integration branch only through the serial seam with the declared bar green on the composed tree — a red parks the candidate and stops the queue loudly; a declared pause stops claiming and drains in-flight lanes to a merged stop; a lane closed under the terminal-close contract (SR-144's, not restated here) reaches the integration branch through this same serial seam and by no other path; a crash at any lifecycle boundary recovers from version-control history without double-assignment.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

The legacy untracked lane/pause half retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5 - a stray local file is inert, pinned by test. A pause stops claiming only — everything in flight finishes and integrates to a merged stop; pause_reason keeps the unchanged None/''/reason return contract.
