### LLR LLR-140 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-140
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Local integrator (claim + queue + audit)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: claim/finished_branches/_verdict_gate/integrate_one/audit
- **Detail**: claim: §2.3 steps 1+2 with the refusal ladder (tracked pause via agent_common.tracked_pause, dirty tree, existing branch, unsafe name, non-ordinary safety_class, off-frontier id) before a git mv + trunk commit + branch cut. finished_branches: a claimed branch whose tip holds no spec under active/<branch>/ (the closing move IS the signal). integrate_one: --no-ff --no-commit merge, trunk_step.py folded into the merge commit so it passes its own floor, check.py --jobs 0 at the declared tier on the composed tree read fail-closed (nonzero exit OR any SKIP line refuses), verdict gate per docs/process.toml [policies] review_rounds (legacy docs/review-policy read through the migration window) using score_reviews.parse_verdict with the verdict's last branch commit required no older than the last non-review non-fragment commit, ff-only trunk advance, branch deleted on green, candidate parked on red. audit: git log --first-parent --no-merges over --since..HEAD; paths outside docs/work/, docs/log.d/, docs/log.md and the stack.ini [generated] set flag the commit. A missing stack.ini or an empty [product] test declaration refuses the merge; a red bar parks the candidate and stops the queue rather than composing past it.
- **Rationale**: One serial actor, one page; every refusal names its reason; the queue stops at the first red rather than composing past it.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-132)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-145 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-145
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Link-aware spec-move ritual (move + relink indivisible)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/spec_move.py
- **CodeSymbol**: move_spec/expected_relink/archive_dest
- **Detail**: move_spec moves a registry/spec file and keeps every markdown link in the repo true as ONE indivisible operation: the moved file's own relative targets are re-relativised against its new directory, every inbound link whose target resolves to the vacated path is redirected (link text untouched, #fragments carried, external/protocol-relative/bare-anchor targets left alone, root-relative targets excused only in the rebase half, line endings preserved via newline=''), and every rewritten file is staged with the move so the ritual lands as one changeset. integrate.claim and handback.close_partial call it for the claim and close moves; the CLI performs the worker's terminal close move and the dated docs/archive/specs/ archival. expected_relink is the pure oracle integrate._abandoned_claim compares byte-for-byte to excuse a crashed claim's own relink writes without widening its conviction to any .md edit.
- **Rationale**: A bare move strands both link directions and the break surfaces late, on the composed tree (measured: one probe archival took the broken-link count from 4 to 8 - 3 outbound, 1 inbound). Making move+relink one indivisible operation is a constraint that leaves the two-thirds state unrepresentable, rather than a check that detects it after the parallel work is done.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-139)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-150 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-150
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Lane mechanics: worktree, worker subprocess, §A2 refresh subprocess
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/lane.py
- **CodeSymbol**: ensure_worktree/worker_argv/spawn_worker/run_worker/spawn_refresh
- **Detail**: One lane's mechanics extracted from the dispatcher (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.2): ensure the lane worktree (integrate.lane_worktree, the one home both the worker and the refresh agree on), build the worker argv with the plain launch's session dials forwarded explicitly, launch the worker subprocess (spawn_worker non-blocking for the lane table and dispatch._launch's own default; run_worker its blocking form - a DECLARED SEAM with no in-kit caller, kept for a synchronous single-lane run, and the shape dispatch._launch's worker= injection substitutes), and run the §A2 station refresh as the lane's OWN subprocess (spawn_refresh - the invoker's integrate.py refresh CLI under the harness interpreter) so N lanes' bars overlap instead of queueing at the dispatcher (§A4.3). Reports outcomes, decides none: the tree signal (specs moved out of active/<branch>/) plus exit codes are the whole protocol - no state file, no back-channel.
- **Rationale**: The §A4.2 split keeps the deciding half (dispatch.py) apart from the running half, so at lanes=1 the dispatcher degenerates to the serial loop and the concurrency is a table size, not a second code path.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-144)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-151 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-151
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Dispatch-lock claim rung + the spine batch claim
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _dispatch_lock/claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim
- **Detail**: The §A4.1 authority flip's integrator half (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.1): _claim_refusal's safety_class != ordinary arm is DELETED (admission is the dispatcher's decision) and the claim REQUIRES the dispatch lock (agent_common.dispatch_lock_path, out/agent-loop.lock - the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime), acquired at ONE site (_dispatch_lock, mirroring _slot's one-site discipline for the merge slot's own lock) with the ladder run before it so the clean-trunk rung never refuses over the lock's own file: a hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim passes dispatch_lock_held=True. claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim/_claim_delta are batch-aware: a spine batch is ONE claim commit moving every batched spec (subject joins the ids; the single-WI subject stays byte-identical), and integrate() takes an optional branches= restriction so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes.
- **Rationale**: A constraint instead of a check: the state the old refusal guarded against (a claim written beside live lanes) is now unrepresentable, and the batch makes N spine changes cost one re-attest window (§A4).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-145)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-157 — Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-157 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-157
- **Title**: Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (queue-overlap visibility). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Deliberate pair (census F6): the PB-format invariant itself is SR-015's; this row's decomposition carries the checker. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Each declared registry rule violation is reported with row-level attribution; the declared strict set exits nonzero at its gate and advisory classes never join the exit code; absent or placeholder-only optional registries and declared opt-outs are vacuous; the declared verification vocabulary (including Critique) is accepted and an unknown value fails. The declaration this is read against is the rule inventory the delivered checkers themselves publish — the id shapes, the required cells and the closed vocabularies (with the interface and component entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the strict flag families and their stated exit contracts, the gating form and provenance findings beside the never-gating paraphrase and acceptance advisories, and the work-item coherence rules with their declared opt-out — each recorded at its declaration site, not restated here. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1

- **Detail**: Loads the four registries, computes the orphan set (SR without LLR/TC, LLR/TC without a parent, SN without an SR), and exits 1 under --strict. A fully linked chain exits 0 under --strict, and the orphan report names each at-fault id.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-001)

- **Component**: CMP-006

- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Id + registry-CSV-structure + citation integrity
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
- **CodeSymbol**: integrity_findings/structure_findings/triangle_findings
- **Detail**: Duplicate/malformed id detection per ID_PATTERNS, a per-row column-count check over each CSV registry, and SR/LLR citation coherence (triangle_findings); the always-valid --strict-integrity floor. The id vocabulary spans SR/LLR/TC/PB/REPO/MOD/PART/ASSET/CMP/IF (MOD the legacy compatibility id for current REPO rows); a clean registry set exits 0, and orphans never affect the --strict-integrity verdict.
- **Rationale**: These three are wrong the moment they are written rather than at a gate, and each corrupts the join before the orphan rules could notice — so they run on every commit rather than waiting for a gate that would inherit an already-broken registry.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-002)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

The -000 example-row scan spans SR/LLR/TC and the SN doc and rides --no-placeholders; --strict-schema is the gate for the required-field and Verification/Tier closed-vocabulary rules.

### LLR LLR-034 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-034
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: WI dependency-DAG validation
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load_wis/validate
- **Detail**: Loads the WI registry - the docs/work/ spec folder, the ONE home since the CSV home retired at concurrency-restructure Phase 5 (a stray work-items.csv is itself an integrity finding, never a second home) -; validates WI ids, predecessor resolution, and hard-edge acyclicity; respects the absent/placeholder/opt-out vacuity rules. Findings name the offending row; the declared opt-out home is docs/process.toml [checks] trajectory_check = false, with the legacy one-word docs/trajectory-check still read while the declared migration window stays open.

### LLR LLR-133 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-133
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Spine stand-alone rule
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: provenance_findings
- **Detail**: provenance_findings flags a WI id or a process-doc citation across the normative cells of all three spine registries, joining the --strict failure set via exit_code. The normative cells are the SR Title/Requirement/Rationale/AcceptanceCriteria, the LLR Title/Detail/Rationale, and the TC Method/Expected/Parameters; pointer columns are out of scope, and each finding names the registry, row and cell. Not a finding: an artifact path, a rubric, a sibling spine id, and a port-script name — the ten declared external ports (check.py, bootstrap.py, agent_loop.py, check_vendored.py, gen_cases.py, gen_release_checklist.py, subagent_gate.py, run_menu.py, integrate.py, trunk_step.py) — the script-name carve-out reaching no wider than that set.
- **Rationale**: A citation is a pointer a later reader may be unable to follow, and the row's reason travels with it: when the work item is closed and archived, the row keeps its claim and loses its justification, which is the failure this catches. Applied to every spine registry rather than the SR alone, because the layer the rule could not originally see held the largest pocket of violations — a narrower scope would guard the cleanest layer and miss the dirtiest.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-134 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-134
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Requirement form rules
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: form_findings
- **Detail**: form_findings applies six rules — one obligation per requirement, no non-obligation modal, no actorless passive, no unfalsifiable term, no open-ended clause, no obligation keyword in an LLR Detail — skipping Draft rows, and joins the --strict failure set via exit_code. The negative set holds: a Draft row, a zero-obligation row, a project's own declared alternate obligation keyword, and a multi-clause AcceptanceCriteria are not findings — stated keyword-free here deliberately, since the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use.
- **Rationale**: A row carrying two obligations reports neither when one fails, and undecidable wording is signed on impression rather than evidence — that is the failure all six rules exist to prevent. Draft is skipped because a Draft row is exempt from the child-completeness rules elsewhere in this harness and is by convention the state a requirement occupies while still being written, so an unfinished acceptance criterion is expected there rather than defective; flagging it would make the state unusable for the drafting it exists to allow. This cell says "obligation keyword" rather than quoting the keyword itself because the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use, and an exemption a checker cannot see is one an author can reach for.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-135 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-135
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Paraphrase advisory
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: paraphrase_advisories
- **Detail**: paraphrase_advisories compares a child cell's significant words (alphabetic runs of four characters or more, lowercased) against its parent's and reports when the share of the CHILD's words that also appear in the parent exceeds the declared threshold — the child is the denominator, so a long parent cannot dilute a short echoing child. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set. The two watched relationships are an SR Rationale against its own Requirement and an LLR Detail against its parent SR Requirement — the pairs where the parent is unambiguous; each finding emits a stdout WARNING plus a report line with the exit code unchanged.
- **Rationale**: A child that re-words its parent adds a second home for one fact and no decomposition, so a later amendment updates one copy and the two disagree with nothing to report it. The advisory has its own pipe rather than sharing the acceptance-criteria counter because a shared count would report 'ac-advisories' about a finding that is not one, and a reader acts on the counter name.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-160 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-160
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: The mechanical queue-overlap pre-filter
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: queue_conflict_findings/_title_tokens
- **Detail**: Three signals over OPEN rows only — Jaccard over title token sets at a deliberately high bar, shared `SR-Refs`, and a shared anchor-stripped `SpecRef` — each reported once per pair per signal with both ids, in sorted order. Printed on the warn-only line beside backlog staleness; never appended to `errors`. A shared stopword alone is never a signal, and the findings stay out of the exit code even under --strict.
- **Rationale**: A low similarity bar turns a useful warn into noise that gets ignored, which is worse than not having the warn: this rung catches the same job minted twice, not two rows in the same area.
- **TestRefs**: TC-155
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-179 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-179
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: EARS statement-pattern advisory
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: ears_advisories
- **Detail**: ears_advisories reads the opening of each non-example SR Requirement cell and reports the row when that opening is a condition dressed in a keyword outside the four EARS condition keywords (When / While / If / Where) — a bare subject (the ubiquitous pattern) and an empty cell pass. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set; the scan covers Drafted rows, unlike the gating form rules in the same module, and the SR tier alone.
- **Rationale**: EARS puts the condition in front of the subject so a reader learns when a requirement applies before what it obliges, which is also what makes the opening scannable by eye and by tool; a row opening 'Before X, the system shall …' carries the same condition where nothing looks for it. Advisory rather than gating deliberately: which pattern a row IS — ubiquitous with a qualified response, or event-driven with the trigger fronted — is a judgement about the obligation, and the same sentence can be honest in either shape, so a gating version would overrule the author on the one question the author is better placed to answer. Drafted rows are in scope because an opening is finished the moment it is written and a warn costs a drafter nothing, and because both rows this rule found at landing were Drafted — skipping them would have shipped a guard that had never once fired.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-174)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-158 — Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-158 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-158
- **Title**: Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier
- **SN-Refs**: SN-010;SN-008
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a green never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A dead intra-repo link, an absent or repeated vision declaration, a stale generated doc under the staleness check, a dangling backticked path or symbol reference, and a declared figure marker missing its command or revision each produce a finding at their declared tier; the declared carve-outs (generated blocks, per-line and per-file allow markers, unmarked figures, URLs and placeholders) stay silent. The declared tiering, read off each class's declaration site rather than restated: link and vision findings gate from the first requirements bar on; orphans and staleness warn by default, orphans gating only under their declared strict flag; the reference and figure checks gate at the release bar, gating on dangling references alone, with untraced ones counted and never gating. A class whose severity is stated at no declaration site is undeclared, and this row is unsatisfied until it is declared there.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-012 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-012
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Link + vision-tag checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_docs.py
- **CodeSymbol**: main
- **Detail**: Resolves intra-repo links, requires a PROJECT-VISION tag, and checks generated-doc freshness under --stale. A dead intra-repo link or an absent PROJECT-VISION tag fails; a clean doc set passes. Wired as check.py's built-in doc-navigability step, running from DevStg-Reqs on; orphans warn unless --strict-orphans, with docs/orphans-allow as the reviewed carve-out, and --stale is warn-only.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-012)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-013 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-013
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Flow-doc contract checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_flows.py
- **CodeSymbol**: main
- **Detail**: Validates an authored runtime-flow doc against its structural contract; a malformed flow fails. A conformant doc passes. Bound to its input surface: the authored runtime-flow docs are docs/architecture.md's Runtime flows section, whose retirement is ruled — when that surface goes, this check's input class goes with it and the check stands down, an explicit recorded dependency rather than a silent retirement.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-013)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-038 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-038
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Path + sym: reference checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_doc_refs.py
- **CodeSymbol**: findings_for/load_symbol_oracle/is_path_shaped
- **Detail**: Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md; a backticked token is path-shaped when it carries a separator plus a known extension or top-level prefix (URLs/globs/placeholders excluded); GENERATED marker blocks and path-ok lines are skipped; sym:<module>.<name> is resolved against the module map parsed from architecture.md (module tail -> public symbols); warn-first, --strict exits 1. With no module-map inventory the symbol tier skips cleanly while the path tier still runs. Declared to the harness as docs/stack.ini's [step:doc-refs] row (gates = DevStg-Impl, beside [step:figures] for the figure-provenance checker); docs/declared-absences is the reviewed absence carve-out.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-041)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-146 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-146
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Declared-figure provenance presence check
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_figures.py
- **CodeSymbol**: findings_for/judge_marker/marker_segments
- **Detail**: Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md (check_doc_refs.doc_files - the same walk, so the two doc checks agree on the surface and generated trees stay skipped) plus docs/stack.ini, with docs/reviews/ records out of scope (a verdict quotes defective markers as evidence); each opt-in fig: marker is judged on its own attributes and must carry a non-empty, wordful cmd= and rev= pair (rev bare or quoted), or a non-empty derived= derivation; a marker is grammar prose that declares nothing only when every attribute value is wholly a placeholder token (angle-bracket-wrapped, or the bare ellipsis - so the convention text ships scaffold-clean); in a judged marker a placeholder-shaped value counts as absent, never as satisfying (an unfilled rev=<revision> names no revision), while a metacharacter inside a longer value is command text and the marker is judged on its attributes; findings name the missing half and quote the line; GENERATED marker blocks and fig-ok lines are exempt; warn-first, --strict exits 1. Presence only, never truth - re-derivation is rung 2, deliberately not built.
- **Rationale**: Would have flagged all three 2026-08-01 false figures; opt-in keeps the claim honest - declared figures carry provenance, not all figures do.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-140)
- **Status**: Approved
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-159 — Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reported

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-159 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-159
- **Title**: Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reported
- **SN-Refs**: SN-023;SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report declared-architecture connectivity gaps — an unresolvable interface row, an undeclared endpoint or direction, a cross-component import no declared interface covers, an invalid spec interface citation, and a top view past its declared bound — warn-first with --strict gating, vacuous where the optional inventories are absent.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-023 (the parts' connections are as much of the truth as the requirement tree, checked mechanically warn-first — the need's own words), SN-002 (interface rows stay resolvable) and SN-012 (opt-out and vacuity keep the layer free for non-users). One row: the delivered contract is one connectivity verdict; the checkers below it are its decomposition.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Malformed or unresolvable IF rows are findings; undeclared endpoints, uncovered cross-component edges, invalid spec citations and an oversized top view warn plain and fail --strict; declared opt-outs and small or absent inventories are vacuous; declared seams render without changing the verdict. The rules and their opt-outs are read off their declaration sites, not restated here: the interface and component id shapes and the off-spine back-link to the spine sit on the always-on integrity floor and join --strict, their required-field entries and enums staying advisory by the recorded warn-first sequencing; the connectivity rules join every module of the generated architecture inventory against the declared interface endpoints (a module resolving to no endpoint is the "connectivity undeclared" warn), report a cross-component import no declared interface covers and a spec interface citation resolving to neither a declared seam nor the intra-module escape, and hold the top view to its declared bound (warn plain, error under --strict); two declared opt-outs and a per-module source/sink honesty valve carve the layer out. A connectivity rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default. Carved out of that default (owner ruling 2026-08-15, second-read M1): the interface endpoint-pair cells and the closed discrete/variable Signal vocabulary are SR-162's observables — their resolution against the declared external frame is SN-037's need, stated there in the need's own words — while the generic required-field and closed-vocabulary checker those cells ride stays SR-157's; a rule landing at those two cells belongs to those rows, not this one.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted

## SR-160 — Front-door launchers for the two universal contributor actions

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-160 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-160
- **Title**: Front-door launchers for the two universal contributor actions
- **SN-Refs**: SN-034
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered scaffold shall place both universal contributor actions — preparing the development environment, and resuming the automated development loop — as launchers at the repository root for each supported platform, each starting its action as a single step — locating a working interpreter by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly rather than crashing cryptically when none resolves or a Store alias exits nonzero — or documenting the one platform-required step where a single click is impossible.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-034, whose row is the declared ONE home for launcher-class obligations (root visibility, one action per platform, both universal actions). SN-034 had no citing SR — the launcher rows realized SN-001 and left the need an orphan the strict trace names. One row per 13p: (SN-034, scaffold capability). ABSORBS SR-021's LAUNCHER HALF, 2026-08-15: this rationale previously said that the per-launcher robustness obligation REMAINED SR-021's, cross-referenced rather than restated — and a cross-reference to a row that no longer exists is not a home, so what was pointed at is now stated. SR-021 spanned two audiences at once, the git hooks and the root launchers, which is why it SPLIT rather than moved whole: the hook half is machinery under SR-019/SR-020 (LLR-021), and the launcher half is an observable of THIS row, because a launcher that crashes cryptically has not started its action as a single step. The wiring obligation stays SR-113's, which is a live row.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A fresh scaffold and this repository each carry both launcher sets (environment preparation and loop resume) at the root for Windows, macOS and Linux; each launches its action as a single step on that platform, or documents the single platform-required step (the Linux execute-bit case); with no working interpreter each root launcher reports clearly and does not crash cryptically, and with one it runs its action; the adopter-facing guide names the capability and invites reuse.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-161 — Decompositions carry a perspective record

SN-036 had no citing SR. The declared-perspective inventory is the ruled hats roster; the record proves coverage and provenance while adequacy stays with the independent review, exactly as the need partitions it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A decomposition round writes the perspective record beside its artifacts; a declared perspective applicable to the decomposition and absent from the record is reported; the record distinguishes not-applicable from considered-with-no-finding; the independent adequacy review reads the record rather than the authoring transcript.

- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-162 — Requirement boundary references resolve against the declared frame

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-162 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-162
- **Title**: Requirement boundary references resolve against the declared frame
- **SN-Refs**: SN-037
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall resolve every system-requirement boundary reference, and every declared interface's endpoint pair and signal type, against the declared external frame — an unresolvable crossing failing the strict trace naming the row — reporting requirements naming no crossing, crossings named by no requirement, declared crossings with no realizing interface row, an interface declaring no endpoint pair or no signal type from the closed discrete/variable vocabulary, and a seam joined to a counterpart whose signal type is incompatible with its own, each as an advisory, and staying vacuous for a project declaring no frame.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-037 — every promised behavior's entry and exit locatable at a declared boundary, unresolved references mechanical findings, in the need's own words. SN-037 had no citing SR even though the machinery is already delivered (the frame registry, the boundary-reference field, the resolution and coverage rules); this row is the obligation that machinery enforces. Severity split deliberately: resolution hard, coverage advisory — the ruled warn-first sequencing while the corpus converges. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-037's SIGNAL-TYPING half — "every referenced interface identifies its boundary endpoints and whether its signal is discrete or variable ... incompatible signal types are mechanical findings" — had ZERO textual occurrence in the SR layer while `orphans=0` held, even though `interfaces.toml` already carries a `signal` cell on every row (many of them typed `variable` by a recorded default with a re-type note, which is exactly the state a check is owed for). NAMED RESIDUAL, stated rather than quietly dropped: the need's last clause — that a reviewed change altering one side of the requirement/interface relationship carries or justifies the corresponding change on the other — is a REVIEW obligation this row does not claim to mechanize, and no SR states it yet.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A Boundary-Refs value naming an undeclared crossing joins the --strict failure set naming the row; the uncovered-requirement count, the crossings named by no requirement, and the realization gap are each one advisory line; an interface row carrying no endpoint pair, or a signal value outside the closed discrete/variable vocabulary, is reported naming the row; two joined seams whose declared signal types are incompatible are reported naming both rows; entity and relationship references in the frame registry resolve; a project declaring no frame is vacuous.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-165 — The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-165 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-165
- **Title**: The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record
- **SN-Refs**: SN-040
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered planning content shall leave, with every component partition it produces, a machine-readable derivation record in the tracked component registry rather than session prose — the candidate partitions considered, the declared objective and constraints they were scored against, each candidate's score, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it — such that a live component row whose record omits any of those elements, or a selection the recorded scores do not rank first without a recorded human override, is a reportable finding.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-040 — a free-form partition leaves no record of the alternatives weighed, so the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents; the record is what lets the choice be re-examined. SN-040 had no citing SR, even as the P5 partition work performed exactly this record. Reproducibility is stated as a recomputation from the record's own declared inputs, not as a reviewer re-running a model session, because only the former can fail — the SR-161 form applied to the partition instead of the perspective set. B-05 alone: the human ruling is an element the record must carry, not an observable the system states at the authority input, which is SR-139's and SR-140's ground.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A partition round writes the derivation record into the tracked component registry, never into session prose or a plan document alone; a live component row with no record, or a record omitting the candidate partitions, the declared objective, the constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition or the human ruling, is reported naming the missing element; a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported unless the record carries the human override that chose it; the recorded ranking recomputes from the record's own objective, constraints and scores alone.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-172 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-172
- **SR-Refs**: SR-165
- **Title**: Component derivation record: element completeness + the ranking recompute
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
- **CodeSymbol**: component_findings
- **Detail**: Extends the existing component pass — the one home that already reads docs/requirements/components.toml and already emits CMP findings — with the derivation-record rules SR-165 states, rather than adding a second reader of the same registry. Per live CMP row: the record must declare the candidate partitions considered, the objective and the constraints they were scored against, a score per candidate, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it; a missing element is one finding naming the row AND the element. Two derived rules on top of presence, because presence alone is satisfiable by a record that says nothing: (1) THE RANKING RECOMPUTES — re-rank the recorded candidates from the record's own objective, constraints and scores and compare with the recorded order, a mismatch being a finding, which is what makes the record falsifiable without a reviewer re-running a model session; (2) A SELECTION THE SCORES DO NOT RANK FIRST is a finding unless the record carries a human override naming that choice, so a legitimate "the scores said A, the human chose B" is expressible and an unexplained one is not. Vacuous by construction on a project declaring no components, and on the example -000 rows. NOT BUILT YET: the record cells do not exist on the CMP schema today, which is why this row and TC-166 are Draft — SR-165 was flipped Inspection -> Test at act 7 precisely because the check is mechanizable, and this is the debt that flip incurred, recorded rather than silently owed.
- **Rationale**: The check belongs where component_findings already is for the reason the census keeps applying: a second module reading components.toml would be a second answer to "is this partition recorded", and the two would drift. The recompute is the load-bearing half — an element-presence check passes on a record whose scores contradict its own conclusion, which is the exact failure SN-040 exists to catch (the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents). The override arm is what stops the recompute rule from forbidding human judgement: the requirement is that a departure from the scores is RECORDED, never that it is disallowed.
- **TestRefs**: TC-166
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-166 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-166
- **Verifies**: SR-165;LLR-172
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run trace.py's component pass over fixture component registries: (a) a complete derivation record passes silently; (b) a record omitting each element in turn — candidate partitions, objective, constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition, the human ruling — is reported once per omission, naming the row and the missing element; (c) a record whose recorded ranking does not recompute from its own objective, constraints and scores is reported; (d) a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported, and the SAME record with a recorded human override naming that choice is silent; (e) a project declaring no components, and the shipped example -000 rows, are vacuous.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-165 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-172 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_components_registry.py (to be extended at implementation)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-166 — The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it will

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-166 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-166
- **Title**: The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it will
- **SN-Refs**: SN-001;SN-007
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered package shall be consumable at its own declared shipped-file manifest — every file the manifest names materialized at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and this repository's own instance carrying the structure of the template it ships — with a declared destination a fresh scaffold does not carry, and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's instance, each failing rather than degrading the delivered package silently.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-001 (an adopting team gets a working, gated, requirement-traced process without hand-building the tooling — which presupposes that what arrives is whole) and SN-007 (the kit is held to the standard it ships). §1R.6 names this as B-05's own artifact-shaped observable — the package "exists, is complete, and is consumable" — and records that its manifest is already pinned; the 2026-08 re-tier campaign minted fifteen rows and not this one, leaving the crossing the frame describes at greatest length with no requirement stating it, and MAPPING surviving only in SR-163's rationale. Verification evidence is named rather than owed: tests/test_bootstrap.py drives bootstrap.py's MAPPING against the file lists a fresh scaffold must carry, and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py pins this repository's own instance against the template it ships, so the manifest cannot declare one thing and the package deliver another. Deliberately distinct from its two neighbours: SR-163 asks why each shipped file EXISTS (SN-038, purpose coverage), SR-010 asks whether the generated scaffold runs GREEN, and this row asks whether what was promised ARRIVES WHERE IT WAS PROMISED. NARROWED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F7, convergent with the second read's H1): as minted, this row ALSO required that a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, each FAIL — and SR-163 already reports both classes under a configurable warn-versus-gate policy. Two rows prescribing different outcomes for one observable is a contradiction an implementer can only resolve by violating one of them, so the two manifest-PRESENCE clauses are shed here and referenced to SR-163; each was token-checked into SR-163's requirement ("missing files, stale inventory entries") and acceptance ("a shipped file absent from it", "a stale entry naming a file that no longer ships") before deletion, so no obligation vanished — only its second, conflicting statement. What is left is what SR-163 does not ask: not whether a file is accounted for, but whether it LANDS — at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and in this repository's own instance of the template it ships.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A manifest entry whose destination a fresh scaffold does not contain fails naming the destination; and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's own instance fails naming the surface. Manifest PRESENCE is deliberately NOT restated here — a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, are both SR-163's, reported under its declared warn-versus-gate policy.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-167 — Performance-budget breaches red their hard-gated rows

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-167 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-167
- **Title**: Performance-budget breaches red their hard-gated rows
- **SN-Refs**: SN-008;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall fail the bar it runs exactly when an in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row breaches — its measured metric worse than its absolute Budget, or regressed beyond its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline — while warn-gated rows and out-of-tier rows only warn, and a metric with no measurement this run is a reported skip, never a silent pass.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a green — a breach on a row the project chose to hard-gate must red the run, not ride as a tracked number) and SN-012 (the perf layer is opt-in; a repo declaring no PB rows pays nothing, while one that does gets the same honest-gate discipline as every other check). Minted at the owner's 2026-08-15 L2 ruling at the sitting sweep: the second read's L2 and adversarial F6 both found the tolerance verdict fully built (check_perf.py evaluate, the exit contract stated in the module docstring), fully decomposed (LLR-014 "Budget comparator", whose detail states it verbatim) and fully proven virtualized (TC-014 -> tests/test_check_perf.py: test_regression_beyond_tolerance_fails_when_gate_fail end-to-end against a bootstrapped scaffold, test_regression_uses_direction_and_tolerance at unit level, test_within_tolerance_regression_passes the passing arm, test_absolute_breach_fails_when_gate_fail the absolute arm) — yet stated by no SR: LLR-014 rode SR-006 (the step plan and missing-tool refusal), IF-004/IF-031 rode SR-006 + SR-015 (PB back-link resolvability), and SR-033 states only the warn-tier, never-fails side, so the verdict the interfaces' contracts describe had no requirement home (F6: "their contract has no home to point at"). One row for BOTH breach arms deliberately — absolute and regression are one delivered exit contract, the SR-157 one-verdict shape, and the second read's regression framing was the symptom, not the boundary. REWORDED 2026-08-15 (same day, on the owner's grounding question): as minted this row named check_perf.py in the requirement cell, but check_perf.py is NOT on the §1R.2 declared B-05 artifact list — it is the harness's internal perf step (IF-004, check_perf -> check), so under ruling 2.7(a) the SR states the harness-verdict observable and the comparator is named only in acceptance as the current carrier, the SR-157/SR-158 pattern. Vacuous in THIS repository — no performance-budgets.csv exists here and §9's perf layer is declared absent — but the template ships the layer, and the owner's ruling is that a shipped layer owes a stated, tested obligation even where locally unused. IF-004/IF-031 req_refs gain this row in the same act (off-spine, approval drafted); LLR-014.sr_refs and TC-014.verifies are deliberately NOT re-pointed here — both rows are Approved, so amending their cells overrides attestation, which is the sitting's act: when taken, LLR-014 adds this row beside SR-006 and TC-014 verifies it beside LLR-014. Hat-derived (hat.PERFORMANCE): the charter's third clause — what happens when the budget is exceeded — is this row's entire subject, and no other lens in the roster asks it. That dependency is now CLOSED: the PERFORMANCE charter was ruled `always` by the owner on 2026-08-16, so the lens this row derives from applies unconditionally rather than waiting on a tag; the rationale above already concedes the row exists because a layer ships rather than because a need asks, and naming the lens is what turns that from accretion into a reviewable derived requirement. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: An in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row whose measured metric breaches its absolute Budget, or falls outside its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline, makes the perf step exit nonzero naming the row and the bar red with it; the same breach on a warn-gated row, or on a row outside the run tier, exits zero and warns; a hard-gated row with no measurement this run is a per-row reported skip, never silent; a row with no baseline or no tolerance cannot fail on the regression arm while its absolute arm still applies; a reviewed baseline update is the acceptance path for an intended regression, proven end to end against a bootstrapped scaffold.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: perf

- **LLR-ID**: LLR-014

- **Detail**: Compares measured metrics (docs/test/perf-metrics.json) to declared budgets and the committed baseline (docs/test/perf-baseline.json) and fails beyond tolerance at the row's block/warn level; --update-baseline is the reviewed acceptance path for an intended regression. The step is wired into the harness at DevStg-Impl, per its declared gates in docs/stack.ini.

- **Method**: Run the check-perf suite; a within-tolerance metric passes and a regression fails.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies the acceptance folded into LLR-014, under both its parents: SR-167's perf-budget breach verdict (the absolute and regression arms this case drives) and SR-006's harness step plan that runs it, including the bar the step is wired at

- **Evidence**: tests/test_check_perf.py; tests/test_check_perf.py::test_harness_runs_perf_at_g3

## SR-168 — The state view shows current progress and next work

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-168 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-168
- **Title**: The state view shows current progress and next work
- **SN-Refs**: SN-023;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered generator set shall produce one state view from which a reviewer reads the adopted repository's completeness, its requirement decomposition, its planned and in-flight work, and any declared hold on that work, without consulting a second surface.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-023's FIRST conjunct — a reviewer sees the project's progress from one dashboard-like file — with SN-010 (what the reviewer reads there is the generated truth, never a hand-kept summary of it). Minted 2026-08-16 by the re-tier v2 S4 split of SR-070 (ruling R1, one decision per row): the parent had merged the integrity of a generated artifact with what that artifact must SHOW, and the two fail independently. This row is the progress half; SR-169 is the connection half, along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws. Deliberately NOT folded back into SR-070: a state view that regenerates deterministically, offline and drift-checkably while omitting the work in flight satisfies that row completely and this one not at all. Deliberately NOT folded into SR-169 either: a complete progress view can carry no seam graph whatsoever — the state this repository was in before its interface registry existed — so the two are separately observable and separately buildable. `phase` inherits the parent's 1 rather than tracking its children (which span 1 through 4), per the 2026-08-14 ledger's recorded finding that a phase records WHEN a piece shipped and child-later-than-parent is the ordinary case.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The state view carries per-tier completeness, the requirement decomposition down to its leaves, the planned and in-flight work in its declared order, and any declared hold — each reachable within the view rather than by opening a second file; a repository under a declared hold surfaces exactly one hold statement, and none once it is lifted.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: trajectory

## SR-169 — The state view shows how the parts connect

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-169 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-169
- **Title**: The state view shows how the parts connect
- **SN-Refs**: SN-023
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered generator set shall render the adopted repository's declared components and the interfaces between them as a navigable graph — showing which components sit inside which at more than one level, drawing an interface whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container to that container's edge, and showing a stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one when the repository carries the component and interface registries but declares no interface in them (a repository carrying no such registry at all omits the view under SR-070, not under this row).
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-023's SECOND conjunct, in the need's own words: the relationships between the parts (which module talks to which, over what contract) are as much of the project's truth as its requirement tree, and scattering them hides architectural drift. Minted 2026-08-16 by the re-tier v2 S4 split of SR-070 (ruling R1): the parent merged artifact integrity with view content, and the content half divides exactly where SN-023 divides. Kept separate from SR-168 rather than filed as one content row because the two fail independently and are built by different means — the progress views read the requirement and work registries, this one reads the declared component and interface registries, and either can be complete while the other is absent. The empty-view clause is load-bearing: a repository that has declared no interfaces yet must get a stated, legible surface, because an empty graph is indistinguishable from a broken generator. REWORDED 2026-08-16 for cold readability (adversarial round, finding F19, CONFIRMED): the row read "structural units", "seams terminating at the containing boundary" and "the artifact contract's omission rule" — vocabulary a first-time reader cannot resolve, the last of them an internal cross-reference with no id. Same obligations, plainer words, and the absent-registry case now names SR-070 explicitly; the empty-vs-absent split itself is unchanged. `phase` inherits the parent's 1; its children span 1 and 2, the ordinary child-later-than-parent case the 2026-08-14 ledger recorded as intended.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Declared components render with their containment preserved at each visible level; each declared interface connects its correct endpoints, and one whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container is drawn once at that container's edge rather than duplicated; descending a level and returning restores the reader's prior place; a repository whose component and interface registries are present but declare no interface renders the stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: trajectory

## SR-170 — Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-170 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-170
- **Title**: Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027;SN-010;SN-008;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.
- **Rationale**: SPLIT 2026-08-17 (Sol F18, applied): this row merged three obligations that fail independently, and the shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. It keeps the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract; ordering-and-all-or-nothing left to SR-173 and identity allocation to SR-174, each with its own children. The clue Sol read correctly is that the non-reuse clause appeared only in this row's acceptance - an acceptance minting a requirement, the same defect as F12 - and it now has a shall of its own at SR-174. Terminology plained with the split: shared authority surface, mint, composed tree and serial integration seam are gone in favour of what they name. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). Minted 2026-08-16 by the re-tier v2 S4 split of SR-156 (ruling R1): the parent stated the serial LANDING and, separately, that the shared authority surfaces regenerate only there — two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already owner-ruled for the identity mint (log.md Decisions 2026-08-01, R1/R3 — a work branch never mints a work-item id) and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts are written only by the serial merge step and never committed from a work branch; a work branch that regenerates one is refused or its result is not what lands. Ordering and partial-result behaviour are SR-173's; identity allocation is SR-174's; the harness work-branch freshness skip is SR-006's clause and is not restated here.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

- **Title**: next-wi/run-phase retirement + generated status/run-state
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: REGEN_STEPS/regen
- **Detail**: next-wi/run-phase are gone from every surface; the status snapshot is generated by gen_trajectory --status and re-derived by the trunk lane (trunk_step --regen) after each merge - never hand-copied, never written on a work branch. (The run-state generation half retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.) "Every surface" is BOTH directions of SR-148's restored deletion clause, and they discharge in different places: a FRESH scaffold never writes either file (bootstrap.py's MAPPING does not carry them), and a MIGRATED repo deletes them under project-trajectory/RESYNC_PACK.md's upgrade recipe, which names both paths in its "delete local reliance on the retired surfaces" step. The migrated half is the one that can silently fail, because it is the only one where the files exist to be kept.

- **Detail**: docs/status.md's generated block is maintained by the trunk lane only: trunk_step --regen runs gen_trajectory --status after derive_gate (the gate is its input), and the status-map freshness gate byte-compares it on the trunk while work branches skip (SS5.2). (docs/run-state and its four-valued dispatcher outcome retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.) The snapshot is an adopted-toolkit output surface (REL-002), not a system-under-development output; a work branch never commits the artifact.
- **Rationale**: Split from the retirement row: removing the old pointer and generating the surfaces that replace it are separable, and the second is what keeps the replacement from rotting the same way.

Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op.

- **Detail**: On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
- **Rationale**: A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.

## SR-171 — Bounded retry on a declared transient model limit

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-171 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-171
- **Title**: Bounded retry on a declared transient model limit
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall retry a declared transient provider limit under a bounded backoff, surfacing the wait so a reader can tell a throttled run from a wedged one, rather than ending the run.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied). This obligation was live but stated ONLY in SR-026's acceptance cell, where it minted a requirement the shall never made. Deleting it would have dropped real behaviour, so it is stated here instead. Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run survives the conditions it will actually meet - a rate limit is the ordinary one) and SN-025. The bound is what separates this from a retry loop: unbounded retry against a limit that is not transient is indistinguishable from a hang, which is the failure SN-006 exists to prevent. Surfacing the wait is the other half - a silent backoff and a wedged run look identical from outside.
RE-VOICED TO ONE `shall` 2026-08-18, with NO obligation dropped: the cell said "shall retry ... and shall surface", which is the two-obligations-in-one-row shape the form rule catches - and it was invisible only because `form_findings` SKIPS `Drafted` rows, so the finding would have fired the moment this row was approved. Both clauses are kept, the surfacing now stated as a property of HOW the retry happens, which is the honest reading: an unsurfaced bounded retry and a wedge are indistinguishable from outside, so a retry nobody can see does not discharge this row. If the sitting judges surfacing to be a decision that fails independently of the retry, this is a split rather than a re-voice - flagged, not decided here.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A declared transient limit is retried under the declared backoff and the run continues; the wait is visible on the run surface rather than silent; a limit that is not declared transient ends the run with its own outcome code rather than retrying; the retry count and interval come from a declared value, never a literal.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-174 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-174
- **SR-Refs**: SR-171
- **Title**: Rate-limit recognition + bounded, surfaced wait
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
- **CodeSymbol**: limit_reset_hint/seconds_until_reset/main
- **Detail**: A rate limit is recognized only on an error signal (the JSON result's is_error, or a nonzero exit for a plain-text template) whose message carries a reset wording - a healthy transcript merely mentioning limits never reads as a throttle - and it wins the outcome ladder as WAITING, bypassing the stall guard. With --wait-on-limit set, a parsed reset within that ceiling is slept and the session retried, the sleep printed with its reset time; an unparseable wording sleeps min(--limit-retry-fallback, ceiling), printed, and retries. Past the ceiling, or without the flag, the run stops at EXIT_WAITING (5) with a banner naming the resume time. Both dials are declared flags with declared defaults (0 / 3600), never in-line literals. The outcome ladder itself (WAITING ahead of every other code) is LLR-028's home; this row owns the recognition gate, the two clocks and the wait/stop fork.
- **Rationale**: The wait/stop fork keys on the one ceiling the human consented to, so an unbounded silent sleep is unrepresentable - the fallback nap is capped at the same ceiling for the same reason, and every arm prints what it is waiting for so a throttled run is tellable from a wedged one at the console.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-168)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-168 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-168
- **Verifies**: SR-171;LLR-174
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the agent-loop rate-limit cases against the fake-agent CLI: a throttled session exits WAITING (5) with the banner naming the parsed reset time and the WAITING outcome in the iteration log - never a stall abort, even at stall-limit 1; with --wait-on-limit set, a parseable reset within the ceiling drives the wait through the reset-clock parser - the printed wait names the reset time, the printed seconds are the slept seconds, and the session retries (the wall-clock-sized sleeps are observed via a time.sleep-recording driver, never served); an unparseable reset wording sleeps the declared --limit-retry-fallback capped at the ceiling - the min() cap pinned load-bearing by a fallback-above-ceiling case (5 > 1 naps 1s), and the dial's declared default (3600) pinned by a no-flag run - prints the wait, and retries so the run continues and finishes; a healthy transcript that merely mentions limits reads COMMITTED, never WAITING; an error result that is no rate limit reads ERROR rather than being retried as a throttle; the reset-clock parser is unit-covered on both am/pm and 24-hour wordings.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-171 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-174 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_limit_hit_backs_off_without_counting_stall; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_parsed_reset_within_ceiling_sleeps_and_retries; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_unparseable_reset_falls_back_and_retries; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_fallback_nap_is_capped_at_the_wait_ceiling; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_limit_retry_fallback_defaults_to_3600; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_healthy_transcript_mentioning_limits_is_not_a_throttle; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_error_session_reads_error_not_no_commit; tests/test_agent_loop_policy.py::test_seconds_until_reset_parses_both_clock_formats
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-172 — A stalled session ends at its declared limit

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-172 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-172
- **Title**: A stalled session ends at its declared limit
- **SN-Refs**: SN-006;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall end a session that has made no declared progress within its declared stall limit, reporting the stall as its own outcome rather than as success or as an ordinary failure.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied), the sibling of SR-171 and from the same defect: the obligation lived only in SR-026's acceptance. Realizes SN-006 and SN-025. It is a SEPARATE row from SR-171 because the two fail independently and for opposite reasons - a backoff that never gives up is the failure this row catches, and a stall limit that fires on a legitimately slow provider is the failure SR-171's bound prevents. Stated as its own outcome because a stall reported as a plain failure sends a reader looking for a defect that is not there, and reported as success is a false green.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A session with no declared progress inside the declared stall limit ends and reports the stall as its own distinct outcome; a session making declared progress is not ended by that limit; the limit is read from a declared value; the stall outcome is distinguishable from both success and an ordinary failure at the run surface.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-175 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-175
- **SR-Refs**: SR-172
- **Title**: Consecutive no-progress stall guard
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
- **CodeSymbol**: LaneState.note_session/main
- **Detail**: Declared progress is a moved git HEAD: note_session resets the stall counter on a commit and increments it otherwise, so the window is consecutive and interleaved progress is never ended by the limit; a throttled (WAITING) session bypasses the counter entirely, and a timed-out one counts (it produced no commit). At --stall-limit consecutive no-commit sessions (a declared flag, default 3) the loop stops with EXIT_STALL (4) - its own exit code, distinct from success and from the error/config exits - under a STALL banner pointing at the latest iteration log. Which banner (the all-ERROR agent-unavailable wording vs the generic work stall) is stall_verdict's fork and LLR-028's home under SR-028; this row owns the counter, the declared limit and the distinct exit.
- **Rationale**: The counter reads committed evidence (HEAD movement), not session prose, so a session cannot talk its way out of the guard - and the reset-on-commit is what keeps the limit a stall detector rather than a slow-progress killer.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-169)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-169 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-169
- **Verifies**: SR-172;LLR-175
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the agent-loop stall cases against the fake-agent CLI: consecutive no-commit sessions abort at the declared --stall-limit with exit 4 and a STALL banner, stopping at the limit rather than the iteration budget; a commit RESETS the counter, so the same limit fires one session later when progress interleaves; a throttled session never counts toward the guard; a timed-out session does; an all-ERROR run keeps exit 4 but names an unavailable agent while a mixed run keeps the generic work-stall wording; a run that commits every session is never ended by the stall limit and reaches its iteration budget instead; with no flag passed the declared default (3) is what fires - the second consecutive no-commit session does not end the run and the third exits EXIT_STALL at exactly three sessions.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-172 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-175 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_stall_guard_aborts_after_no_commit_sessions; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_stall_limit_defaults_to_three_no_commit_sessions; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_commit_resets_the_stall_counter; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_limit_hit_backs_off_without_counting_stall; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_session_timeout_cannot_wedge_the_loop; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_all_error_stall_names_an_agent_error; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_mixed_no_commit_and_error_stall_stays_generic; tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_budget_ceiling
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-173 — Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial result

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-173 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-173
- **Title**: Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial result
- **SN-Refs**: SN-008;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall regenerate the shared derived artifacts in declared dependency order, stopping at the first failure and committing no partially regenerated set.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F18, applied) out of SR-170, which merged three obligations that fail independently. This is the TRANSACTIONALITY half: ordering and all-or-nothing are one decision about a regeneration run, and they are not the same decision as who is allowed to write (SR-170) or how identity is allocated (SR-174). A regeneration that runs in the right order and still commits half a set satisfies neither, which is what makes them separable. Realizes SN-008 - a first failure that stops loudly beats a half-regenerated set that reads as complete - and SN-010.
TRUTH-MATCHED 2026-08-18, and the direction is deliberate. The shall read "leaving no partially regenerated set BEHIND" while the acceptance read "not left COMMITTED" - two different guarantees in one row, the stronger one in the normative cell. Adjudicated from CODE TRUTH rather than from preference, and the code is unambiguous: the regeneration step touches git NOT AT ALL, so on a first failure it exits nonzero having run no later step, with HEAD unmoved and nothing committed - and it DELIBERATELY LEAVES the already-green steps' output dirty in the working tree, because the design assigns the commit to the caller (LLR-142's "never commits; the caller owns the commit"). The residue is not tolerated, it is REQUIRED: TC-170's own evidence test asserts `git status --porcelain` is NON-EMPTY after the failure, so an implementation that cleaned up after itself would fail the test that holds this row. "Behind" therefore claimed something the system does not do and is tested NOT to do; restating it to "committing no partially regenerated set" matches the proven guarantee rather than weakening the row, because the weaker-sounding word is the one the evidence supports and the stronger one was never true. The transactional rollback a reader might expect from "behind" exists, but it belongs to the WRAPPING callers (the integrator and intake each reset the branch to its last work commit on failure) and is their obligation, not this row's - which is why stating it here would have credited this row with a guarantee a bare invocation does not provide.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Regeneration runs the declared artifact families in declared dependency order, with a producer before every consumer that reads it; a failure stops the run at that point and reports it rather than continuing, running no later family; a failed run moves the recorded history not at all and commits no partially regenerated set, while the already-regenerated output stays uncommitted in the working tree for the caller to commit or discard; an absent artifact family is skipped with a stated reason rather than treated as a failure.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-142 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-142
- **SR-Refs**: SR-173
- **Title**: Trunk-step artifact regen (dependency order)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: regen
- **Detail**: Regenerates arch-map, okf, derived-gate, dashboard, status block and open-items as subprocesses in dependency order (derived-gate before its two consumers), skipping absent artifact families with a notice and stopping loudly at the first failure. Never commits; the caller owns the commit.
- **Rationale**: derive_gate runs before the dashboard/status regens because docs/gate is their input, not their output.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-135, TC-170)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-135 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-135
- **Verifies**: SR-173;LLR-142
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Run the trunk-step regen suite: --regen skips absent artifact families with a notice and stops loudly at the first failing generator, regenerating in dependency order.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-173 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-142 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_trunk_step.py::test_regen_skips_absent_artifact_families; tests/test_trunk_step.py::test_regen_fails_loudly_on_a_broken_generator
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-170 (current)

- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Run the trunk-step regen order/commit cases: the executed step sequence follows the declared dependency table - arch-map before okf, derived-gate before its two consumers (trajectory, status), open-items last - asserted on the printed per-step lines of a real run rather than on the table constant; a regen run creates NO git commit, a green step's output staying uncommitted in the working tree for the caller's one serial commit; and the failure path is executed, not inferred - a run whose later step fails after green steps have dirtied the tree exits nonzero with HEAD unmoved and the green output still uncommitted, so a later step's failure can never leave a partially regenerated set in history.
- **Tier**: Smoke
