- **Phase**: 4

## SR-174 — Work-item identity is allocated once and never re-issued

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-174 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-174
- **Title**: Work-item identity is allocated once and never re-issued
- **SN-Refs**: SN-008;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall allocate each work-item identity at most once, so that no two concurrent actors receive the same identity and an identity freed by a deletion is never re-issued.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F18, applied) out of SR-170. Identity allocation is its own decision, not a consequence of serialization: serializing the writer makes a COLLISION unlikely and does nothing about RE-USE, which is the half that silently re-points every commit message and archived document citing the freed id. The non-reuse clause previously appeared only in SR-170's acceptance, which is the same acceptance-mints-a-requirement defect Sol raised at F12 - stated here rather than left in a cell that cannot carry it. Realizes SN-008 and SN-025.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Two actors finishing against the same tree cannot obtain the same work-item identity; an identity freed by deleting its record is not re-issued to new work; allocation counts from the recorded high-water mark rather than from the live maximum.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-153 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-153
- **SR-Refs**: SR-174
- **Title**: The unified trunk-side intake mint + the context block + the ratification-level arms
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/intake.py
- **CodeSymbol**: intake_after_merge/mint_gap_rows/parse_dispositions/next_wi_id/context_block/flip_verified
- **Detail**: The R1/R3 invariant sealed (log.md Decisions 2026-08-01): a WI id is created only by a human trunk commit or this helper - lanes never mint. next_wi_id counts from max(the docs/id-watermark mark, the sweep of every spec FILENAME under docs/work/, active/<branch>/ included) + 1 - never max(live) alone, so an id freed by a deleted spec is never re-issued - and trace.read_watermark's refusal on an absent or malformed mark is deliberately NOT caught: a mint with no record of what has been allocated must not proceed on a guess (TC-158, IF-101). Three triggers, serial by construction: (a) the ratified/routed-cell diff on the merged commit via check_trajectory.staged_spine_amendments -> one adjudication row listing each changed row/cell/before-after (routed traced cells: SN-Refs, Verifies, and LLR SR-Refs per the ruled cell split); (b) a merged spec carrying ## Handback -> the disposition row (outcomes cancel / defer / re-queue with drafted follow-up / surface an open item; NEVER minted for an adjudication row - no recursion, and handback.hand_back refuses the act itself); (c) the dispatcher's gap census -> concrete gap-closure rows, deduped against every existing row. Drafts-not-mints: a merged adjudication row's ## Dispositions fenced-toml drafts mint at ITS merge, validated loudly. Tier signals are measurable (rows touched, gate delta, handback reason class). context_block renders the pure registry joins (cancelled precedent WITH REASONS first, pending OIs, the LLR/TC code map, CMP knowledge packs, IF seams, precedent reviews) - advisory-never-gating, three consumers. flip_verified enacts ruled decision 2: recommend-only under attended, flip under single-ratify/autonomous, unknown levels fail toward recommend. The mint commit mirrors the claim's bookkeeping shape; every derived title is deterministic, so the sweep CLI re-run is idempotent.
- **Rationale**: A detected event must FORCE a row into the registry with nobody watching, and an id two lanes could pick at once must stay unrepresentable (R1).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-147)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-175 — Declared inclusion rule for content dispatched to a model provider

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-175 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-175
- **Title**: Declared inclusion rule for content dispatched to a model provider
- **SN-Refs**: SN-026
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall compose what it dispatches to an external model runner under a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — the content eligible for each dispatched brief, and what is excluded, readable as a declared set — with the basis on which repository content crosses to a declared provider stated as the scope of the consent the enabling surface grants, never presented as a technical bound on what an invoked runner reads.
- **Rationale**: Hat-derived (hat.DATA-PROTECTION, with hat.SECURITY C-SEC-5 and hat.LEGAL C-LEG-3 converging on the same boundary control — three charters, three reasons, one crossing; clause texts in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): repository content is briefed to external model providers, and for an adopter that content's history carries every contributor's name, email and timestamp — personal data crossing with no declared basis or exclusion in any need. SN-026's own text governs WHICH model runs, never WHAT IT IS SHOWN, so this obligation is content beyond the parent's demand — the derived-requirement class, labelled so the deriving lens is reviewable and the need owner sees it fed back (ruled at sitting-3 §0.4 item 8, 2026-08-17, adopting the intake proposal, docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §1 option (b); the SN-026 amendment was the alternative, deliberately not taken alongside — one rule, one home). WHAT EXISTS TODAY IS A DISCIPLINE, NOT A DECLARATION: the push channel is measurably narrow — an allowlist-only planning surface, the project log excluded BY NAME in three composers, zero authorship fields formatted into any prompt — but the rule lives as a convention inside the composing functions, unfalsifiable because no requirement stated it; this row converts it into a stated one and the chain below pins the conventions as they exist. NOT YET MECHANIZED, stated rather than implied: the declared-set surface itself (a per-row inclusion/exclusion field on the provider registry, or a policy dial) and the planted-credential dispatch block in the acceptance are not built. THE PULL-CHANNEL LIMIT, the design-control honesty this row must carry: every enabled runner is launched with permission-bypass flags and can read the whole tree itself, so the declared rule can bound what the loop ASSEMBLES and state the scope of what the consent surface CONSENTS to — it cannot technically bound an invoked runner, the same no-design-control reading the frame applies to external actors at REL-003 and the B-06/B-07 cut. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The inclusion rule for each brief class the loop dispatches is readable as a declared set naming what is eligible and what is excluded, so a composer can be checked against it rather than against convention; a planted credential inside brief-eligible content blocks dispatch (C-SEC-5's fit criterion, on the same scanner the transcript path already runs); the basis and exclusion for repository content crossing to a declared provider are stated where the provider is declared, and the consent surface's documentation names them as the scope of what enabling consents to; the pull channel is presented as consent-scoped, never as filtered. The conventions this row makes declared are, today, the planning surface's allowlist-only registry read and the composers' by-name exclusions and clipped log reads; the declared-set surface itself is the row's stated build gap.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-176 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-176
- **SR-Refs**: SR-175
- **Title**: The push-channel inclusion conventions, pinned as they exist
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/plan_briefs.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_surface + agent_loop.worker_prompt/critique_brief + adjudicate_brief.compose + intake.context_block
- **Detail**: The brief composers' inclusion rule as it exists today, pinned so the convention is falsifiable while SR-175's declared-set surface is unbuilt: plan_briefs.build_surface reads ONLY the SR and IF registries (the two-file read IS the redaction boundary - status.md, log.md and every self-assessment are unreachable because the function never names them); adjudicate_brief.compose embeds oneline/name-status logs only, never commit bodies, and excludes docs/log.md by name, as do plan_briefs and intake.context_block; agent_loop.worker_prompt clips predecessor Deliverables, an --oneline --no-decorate log and a --name-status diff at declared caps; agent_loop.critique_brief embeds SR intent, TC Parameters and the rubric files. Across the whole composing path no authorship field is formatted into any prompt: a source sweep of the composing modules and the shipped prompt templates for the authorship tokens (%an, %ae, %ad, --author, git blame, git show) finds zero hits, and that sweep is a standing test rather than a one-time measurement. NOT BUILT YET, the same debt-stating pattern as LLR-172: the declared-set surface SR-175 requires (a per-row inclusion/exclusion field on docs/agents.toml, or a process.toml dial) and the planted-credential dispatch block do not exist - this row pins the conventions that declaration will state.
- **Rationale**: Pinning the conventions BEFORE the declaration surface exists is what stops the discipline regressing while the row is Drafted: the 2026-08-17 intake measurement (docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §1) found the rule real but held in place by nothing, the same shape as the no-stub detector's history - a real property nobody could falsify. The sweep asserts the absence class (no authorship egress) rather than any particular formatting, so a new composer joins the bar by default.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-171)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

- **Verifies**: SR-175;LLR-176
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Two arms, the push channel only (the pull channel is consent-scoped by design and has no filter to test): (a) LEAK BY CONSTRUCTION — a sentinel planted in status.md and log.md never reaches the planning surface, because build_surface's two-registry allowlist read cannot see it; (b) THE AUTHORSHIP SWEEP — a source scan of the prompt-composing modules (agent_loop, plan_briefs, adjudicate_brief, intake, prompts) and the shipped prompt templates finds zero authorship tokens (%an, %ae, %ad, --author, git blame, git show), so no composer formats commit authorship into a brief and a new composer joins the bar by default. ## SR-176 — A privacy finding persists by class and location, never by value

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-176 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-176
- **Title**: A privacy finding persists by class and location, never by value
- **SN-Refs**: SN-009
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Any durable record the delivered kit produces of a secrets or privacy finding shall identify the finding by its class and location, never by the matched value.
- **Rationale**: Hat-derived (hat.DATA-PROTECTION, C-DPR-2 — clause text in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): the finding record is the one artifact guaranteed to contain the personal data it reports, created by the control itself — SN-009 defeating itself, since a scanner that catches a value and then echoes it into a committed artifact has published what it protected. Ruled at sitting-3 §0.4 item 8 (2026-08-17), adopting the intake proposal (docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §2 option (b)), whose measurement grounds the row: the scanner mints no record of its own (check_privacy prints location, label and the MATCHED VALUE to stdout and writes nothing), but an unattended run's transcript is committed bookkeeping, so the durable copy of every finding is the session log — and the transcript path redacts credential shapes only. DELIBERATE NARROWING of the charter's ask, recorded here as the deriving decision: C-DPR-2 asks for a retention limit and an access rule, and a kit cannot honestly promise either over an adopter's git history (committed content is effectively forever; access is the host's) — what it CAN promise is that the matched value never reaches durable storage, which moots both. MECHANIZED TODAY for the always-on class: the transcript writer redacts the credential shapes and its summary line names class and count, never the value — pinned by the chain below. NOT YET MECHANIZED, stated rather than implied: the PII/identity classes the privacy gate adds are not in the redaction set, so for an adopter with the gate on a PII finding echoed into a session transcript would persist — closing that, on the same seam, is this row's build debt. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A planted value matched by a scanning class appears 0 times in any tracked artifact after the run that caught it; the durable trace of the finding names the class and the location (a count line, a path), never the matched text; the unredacted raw stream stays in untracked scratch space only. The one path by which a finding becomes durable is the transcript's redaction seam, which holds today for the credential classes; the privacy-gate PII/identity classes are the row's stated gap.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-177 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-177
- **SR-Refs**: SR-176
- **Title**: Transcript redaction: matched values never land in tracked history
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_common.py
- **CodeSymbol**: redact_secrets/write_session_log/_SECRET_RES
- **Detail**: write_session_log passes every transcript through redact_secrets before the tracked per-session log (docs/iteration/NNN-<stamp>.log) is written: each declared credential shape in _SECRET_RES (API keys, GitHub tokens and PATs, AWS key ids, Bearer tokens, Slack tokens) is substituted with [REDACTED], and the header reports the finding as class plus count ("# redacted: N credential-shaped token(s)") - the matched value has no path into the committed file. Deliberately imperfect by its own docstring: an unknown token shape passes through, and the raw unredacted stream stays in gitignored out/run-logs/ for debugging - the untracked half SR-176's acceptance names. THE GAP, stated as LLR-172 states its own: the set covers the always-on secrets classes only; the PII/identity classes check_privacy adds under the privacy gate are not in _SECRET_RES, so the planted-value observable holds today for credential shapes and not yet for the gate's added classes - extending the set on this same seam is the row's build debt.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-172)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### TC TC-172 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-172
- **Verifies**: SR-176;LLR-177
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Plant credential-shaped values (an API key, a Bearer token, an AWS key id) in a transcript and write the session log: each planted value appears 0 times in the written tracked file, [REDACTED] stands in its place, ordinary lines survive intact, and the header names the finding by class and count ("# redacted: 3 credential-shaped token(s)"), never by value. The PII/identity classes are SR-176's stated gap and are deliberately NOT tested until the redaction set carries them.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-176's planted-value observable for the mechanized classes via the LLR-177 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_session_log_redacts_credential_shapes
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-177 — Fan-out utilisation reported from the run's own telemetry

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-177 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-177
- **Title**: Fan-out utilisation reported from the run's own telemetry
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall report, per run, the utilisation of the fan-out it commissions — the lanes configured, the lanes actually occupied, and the work integrated per unit of wall time — derived from the run's own recorded telemetry, reported and never gated, with no declared improvement target.
- **Rationale**: Hat-derived (hat.PERFORMANCE, C-PRF-1 — clause text in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): the charter refuses a declared budget with no measurement behind it, and SN-027 is that finding inverted — the system's most complex machinery justified by a throughput claim no instrument measures, flagged unfalsifiable as written by three independent derivations. Ruled at sitting-3 §0.4 item 8 (2026-08-17), adopting the intake proposal (docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §3 option (b)), and DELIBERATELY LESS than the charter asks — the narrowing is the decision: C-PRF-1 wants a declared improvement over the serial semantic on a declared workload, but the wall time of an LLM loop is dominated by provider latency and model choice, so a numeric target would pin machine, provider and model conditions the kit does not control (one machine is one data point) — this row makes the throughput claim OBSERVABLE rather than BUDGETED. The concrete argument the instrument earns its keep: the lanes dial is undeclared in this repository and defaults to 1, so the machinery SN-027 justifies by fan-out runs SERIAL here and nothing today would say so — this report's first run would have printed lanes=1. The need's own justification was re-worded to the structural claim at the same ruling (SN-027's why, same-day amendment), so the need and the instrument stop overclaiming together. NOT DECOMPOSED, stated rather than implied: nothing aggregates the existing per-session telemetry (wall seconds, api seconds, turns — the session-log headers and the iteration index) by lane or by run, so there is no seam to pin and no test to cite; the row lands Drafted-undecomposed with the aggregation surface as its whole build gap, and the telemetry columns alone do NOT satisfy it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A completed run's report states the lanes configured, the lanes actually occupied, and the work items integrated per wall-hour, derived from the run's recorded telemetry; the report is informational — no threshold, no exit-code change; a serial run reports its single lane rather than omitting the line, so the configured-versus-occupied gap is always visible. The report aggregates the run's own recorded per-session telemetry; the aggregation itself is the row's stated build gap.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-178 — Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-178 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-178
- **Title**: Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported
- **SN-Refs**: SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-02
- **Requirement**: The kit shall report any recorded artifact whose text has moved away from the
copy recording its acceptance - stakeholder needs included, despite their
carrying no status cell - regardless of any Status movement.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-18 by the SR-140 split (one-decision doctrine R1). This is the DRIFT half, and it is separable from SR-140 in the direction that matters: a record that exists and rides its approval commit, with nothing ever comparing live text against it, satisfies SR-140 completely and leaves every later amendment invisible. That is not a hypothetical - it is precisely the failure the git-history derivation had, and the reason a baseline on disk was owed at all.
REGARDLESS OF STATUS MOVEMENT is the load-bearing clause, not a qualifier. The amendment machinery watches the Status flip, and the sanctioned staged-amendment path deliberately does not flip - so a rule keyed off the flip returns nothing on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. Reporting against the recorded copy is the only reading that survives that, because the copy is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file and a walk over the live file can never be one.
THE STAKEHOLDER-NEED RIDER is in the normative text rather than left to the acceptance because a need has no Status cell at all: every amendment-watching rule in the kit is keyed off a field the need tier does not have, so without the clause the tier a sitting most wants to hold is the one tier the rule cannot reach. Needs are covered by the whole-file copy rather than by a per-need projection - the three need tables have different shapes, and a projection would have recorded the garbling.
WHAT COUNTS AS MOVEMENT is one definition with one home, shared with the amend-without-flip guard: the id column and the Status cell are excluded STRUCTURALLY (the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in would make every flip read as an amendment and every amendment invisible behind its own flip), and the remainder splits into a ratified half and a traced half. Two readers of one basis rather than two rules that agree until they do not.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: An artifact whose normative text has moved since the copy that recorded its acceptance is reported whether or not its Status moved; a ratified cell moving under a row still claiming approval is reported, a merely traced cell moving is not, and a row below approval has no claim to fall from and is never reported; the status marker itself is never read as the movement, so a flip is neither an amendment nor a mask for one; and a stakeholder need is held to the same rule despite carrying no status cell for the amendment machinery to watch.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-158 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-158
- **SR-Refs**: SR-178
- **Title**: The one comparison basis an amendment is measured by
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: split_changed_cells/spine_cell_class/_RATIFIED_TEXT/staged_spine_amendments
- **Detail**: RE-POINTED 2026-08-15 (log 2026-08-15h) onto the mechanism that SURVIVED. This row used to describe a DIGEST engine (normative_text/sn_normative_text/digest/current_digests); the last_approved snapshot deleted it whole, because an approval that records what it blessed by COPYING the registries needs no canonical text to hash, no separator that cannot occur in a cell and no second exclusion list. The obligation is unchanged and is discharged by split_changed_cells(csv_path, id_col, head, row) -> {"ratified": {cell: (before, after)}, "traced": {...}}: the id column and Status are excluded STRUCTURALLY - the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in would make every flip read as an amendment and every amendment invisible behind its own flip - and the remainder is split by spine_cell_class, the SAME classifier the amend-without-flip guard reads, so the two can never disagree about what normative means. It is PUBLIC for exactly one reason: baseline_snapshot.is_drifted reads it as the drift basis (LLR-173), so the snapshot comparison and the staged-amendment guard are one rule with one home rather than two rules that agree until they do not. _RATIFIED_TEXT names the one Status value whose row TEXT is ratified (Approved — the D-9 fold collapsed Verified and Planned into it) and requires the SAME value on both sides, so prose rewritten under an Approved row is never invisible. It returns the before/after PAIRS a brief has to render anyway, which is why it replaced a hash rather than being wrapped by one. Needs are covered by the whole-file copy rather than by a per-need projection: the three need tables have different shapes, and a projection would have hashed the garbling.
- **Rationale**: A ledger that does not parse is not an anchor at all, and every reader then falls back silently to the git-history derivation it replaced. The copy-based baseline removes that failure mode rather than hardening it: the record is a file a human can open and `git diff`. What had to be kept is the DEFINITION of a change - which cells count, and which of them are ratified rather than merely traced - and that is one function, cited by both readers, rather than a hash whose inputs each reader re-derives.
- **TestRefs**: TC-153
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-153 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-153
- **Verifies**: SR-178;LLR-158
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: RE-POINTED 2026-08-15 (log 2026-08-15h) off the deleted digest suite onto the tests that verify the surviving basis. Run the drift half of the baseline-snapshot suite: it DRIVES the premise rather than asserting it - test_the_amendment_seam_is_BLIND_to_an_amend_plus_flip shows the staged-amendment guard silent on a sanctioned amend+flip over a real two-commit git repo, which is why a baseline recorded at acceptance time is owed at all - and then pins exactly what a change is taken over. test_a_ratified_cell_moving_under_an_approved_row_is_DRIFT (a ratified cell MUST move it), test_a_TRACED_cell_moving_is_NOT_drift (the traced half must not), test_status_itself_is_never_the_amendment (the marker is excluded structurally, so a flip is neither an amendment nor a mask for one) and test_a_row_below_approval_can_never_be_drifted (no claim to fall from) are the four corners of the split. That the guard and the snapshot comparison agree is no longer a test at all but a property of the code: split_changed_cells is the ONE public basis both staged_spine_amendments and baseline_snapshot.is_drifted call, so there is no second answer to disagree with.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-178 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-158 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-179 — The acceptance record can only be written by copying live text

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-179 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-179
- **Title**: The acceptance record can only be written by copying live text
- **SN-Refs**: SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-02
- **Requirement**: The kit shall refuse a recorded copy that is not byte-identical, in the commit
that writes it, to the live text it claims to record - so the only path into the
record runs through the live registry first.
- **Rationale**: MINTED 2026-08-18 by the SR-140 split (one-decision doctrine R1). This is the ANTI-LAUNDERING half, and it fails independently of both siblings: a comparison that reports drift faithfully against a record a hand edit wrote is a comparison against a forgery, so SR-178 can pass in full while this row is violated. It is what makes "the record holds what was actually blessed" a DECIDABLE property rather than a convention.
IN THE COMMIT THAT WRITES IT is the whole strength of the rule and the reason it is stated as a refusal rather than a report. One comparison, made at the moment of the act, catches a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live alike - three attacks that need three different detectors if the check is made after the fact. It is the replacement for the co-mutation guard the abandoned on-row anchor design would have needed: a stronger rule for less machinery.
NOTE THE REVERSED SIGN, recorded so it is never read as a softening: under the anchor design a stamp written beside its own amendment HID the amendment, so same-commit was an error; under copies the copy MUST ride the approval commit, because riding it is the only thing that makes "this copy is what was blessed" decidable. Its companion rule, the SEED, is deliberately NOT here: refusing to create the first record without an explicit seed is a statement about how a record comes into being and rides its approval commit, which is SR-140's subject, so it sits there. This row governs every write AFTER that, which is where laundering actually lives.
DELETION IS AN ATTACK ON THIS ROW, not on SR-140's: the cheapest laundering is never to forge the record but to remove the page, since the unanchored rule reports a row whose copy reads below it and deleting the copy deletes exactly that evidence. So a registry removed WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS is refused, and a wholesale replacement of the record is not - retirement is legitimate, and a rule that fired on it would make its own design undeployable.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A commit that writes a recorded copy differing from its live counterpart is refused, which is the one comparison that catches a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live alike; a faithful copy passes; a copy removed while the rest of the record stands is refused, while a wholesale replacement of the record is not; the first record refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, so no automated step can bless a tree; and prose that is not a recorded registry is exempt.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-178 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-178
- **SR-Refs**: SR-179
- **Title**: The mirror invariant: the record is only ever written by copying live
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: staged_snapshot_findings/_snapshot_survives
- **Detail**: MINTED 2026-08-18 by the SR-140 split: SR-179 is the anti-laundering half, and its mechanism is a sibling of LLR-158's comparison basis rather than a part of LLR-173's record. staged_snapshot_findings(root, base, head) is a whole-commit rule, not a per-row one: in ANY commit that touches the last_approved record, every touched file must be byte-identical to its live counterpart AS OF THAT SAME COMMIT. One comparison is what makes a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live all fail, and it is why no separate co-mutation guard is owed - the abandoned on-row anchor design would have needed one. It reads the INDEX and the commit-ish alike (matching _spine_revs' prefix contract), so it fires at the hook where the act happens rather than after the fact. THE DELETION ARM is the half a first reading misses and is deliberately asymmetric: _snapshot_survives is the two-line tree probe that separates a registry removed WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS (refused - deleting the copy deletes exactly the evidence the unanchored rule reads, which is cheaper laundering than forging one) from the record going away WHOLE (silent - retirement and a wholesale replacement are both legitimate, and a rule that fired on them would make its own design undeployable). The prose README inside the record is exempt: it is not a recorded registry, and holding it to a live counterpart that does not exist would red every signing. It joins the failure set at check_trajectory's main aggregation, so unlike LLR-173's advisory unanchored arm this one is a real gate today.
- **Rationale**: The record is worthless if anything but a copy can write it, and "only a copy writes it" is a claim no per-row check can make - a per-row rule sees the row it is given and never the commit that wrote it. Placing the invariant on the COMMIT is what converts a convention into a decidable property. It lives in check_trajectory rather than in baseline_snapshot because the writer must not also be the judge of its own writes: the module that copies has every reason to believe its copy is faithful, and the guard exists for the cases where it is not.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-173)
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-173 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-173
- **Verifies**: SR-179;LLR-178
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the mirror-invariant half of the baseline-snapshot suite over real temp repos - the half TC-167 no longer covers, which is the record's own contents. (a) A clean copy passes, so the rule is silent on the legitimate act it exists to permit. (b) A HAND-EDITED record file fails, and (c) a PARTIAL copy fails - the two forgeries the single byte-identity comparison catches without a second detector. (d) THE DELETION ASYMMETRY, both arms executed rather than argued: a record file deleted WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS fails (the cheap laundering - removing the page the unanchored rule reads), while deleting the WHOLE record is SILENT (retirement and wholesale replacement are legitimate). (e) The prose README inside the record is exempt, because it is not a recorded registry and has no live counterpart to be identical to.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-179 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-178 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_a_clean_copy_satisfies_the_mirror_invariant; tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_a_HAND_EDITED_snapshot_fails_the_mirror_invariant; tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_a_PARTIAL_copy_fails_the_mirror_invariant; tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_a_snapshot_file_DELETED_while_the_record_STANDS_fails_the_mirror; tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_deleting_the_WHOLE_record_is_SILENT; tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py::test_the_README_is_prose_and_is_exempt_from_the_mirror
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 5
